/home/dbslim/vackrooms/wasm_frontend/src/adapters/section_locator.rs
//! Names the section of the world the player is standing in, for the HUD.
//!
//! The locator re-derives the deterministic region plan for the player's
//! current region (cached per region key, so the plan is recomputed only on
//! a region crossing) and classifies the position against, in priority
//! order: anomaly footprints, assembly footprints, and circulation spines.
//! Platform-agnostic — no browser types — so the classification is natively
//! unit-testable.

use vackrooms::domain::entities::anomaly::AnomalyKind;
use vackrooms::domain::entities::architecture::{RegionPlan, SpaceProgram};
use vackrooms::domain::entities::position::Position;
use vackrooms::frameworks_drivers::simple_noise::SimpleNoiseProvider;
use vackrooms::use_cases::generate_chunk::GeneratorConfig;
use vackrooms::use_cases::region_plan::{REGION_SIZE, generate_region_plan};

/// Backrooms level ids, mirroring the engine's.
const LEVEL_HABITABLE: u32 = 1;
const LEVEL_GRASSLAND: u32 = 34;

pub struct SectionLocator {
    seed: u32,
    config: GeneratorConfig,
    noise: SimpleNoiseProvider,
    cached_region: Option<(i64, i64)>,
    plan: Option<RegionPlan>,
}

impl SectionLocator {
    pub fn new(seed: u32, config: GeneratorConfig) -> Self {
        Self {
            seed,
            config,
            noise: SimpleNoiseProvider::new(),
            cached_region: None,
            plan: None,
        }
    }

    /// Human-readable "where am I" line for the HUD, e.g.
    /// `LEVEL 0 · PILLAR EXPANSE · REGION (2, -1)`.
    pub fn describe(&mut self, level: u32, x: f32, z: f32) -> String {
        if level == LEVEL_GRASSLAND {
            return "LEVEL 34 · THE GRASSLAND".to_string();
        }
        if level == LEVEL_HABITABLE {
            // Level 1's sectors are a pure world-lattice function; no
            // region plan exists (or is needed) on the Habitable Zone.
            let sector = vackrooms::use_cases::level_one::Sector::at(self.seed, x, z).name();
            return format!("LEVEL 1 · HABITABLE ZONE · {sector}");
        }
        let rx = (x / REGION_SIZE).floor() as i64;
        let rz = (z / REGION_SIZE).floor() as i64;
        let zone = self.zone_name(rx, rz, x, z);
        format!("LEVEL {level} · {zone} · REGION ({rx}, {rz})")
    }

    fn zone_name(&mut self, rx: i64, rz: i64, x: f32, z: f32) -> &'static str {
        if self.cached_region != Some((rx, rz)) {
            self.plan = Some(generate_region_plan(
                self.seed,
                Position::new(rx as f32 * REGION_SIZE, rz as f32 * REGION_SIZE),
                REGION_SIZE,
                &self.config,
                &self.noise,
            ));
            self.cached_region = Some((rx, rz));
        }
        let plan = self.plan.as_ref().expect("plan cached above");

        // Anomalies dominate: standing inside one matters more than which
        // office footprint it swallowed.
        for anomaly in &plan.anomalies {
            if anomaly.footprint.contains(x, z) {
                return match anomaly.kind {
                    AnomalyKind::PillarExpanse => "PILLAR EXPANSE",
                    AnomalyKind::BlackoutExpanse => "BLACKOUT EXPANSE",
                    AnomalyKind::PitLattice => "PIT LATTICE",
                    AnomalyKind::RedRoom => "RED LOOP",
                    AnomalyKind::ArchwayRoom => "ARCHWAY ANCHOR",
                };
            }
        }
        for assembly in &plan.assemblies {
            if assembly.footprint.contains(x, z) {
                return program_name(assembly.program);
            }
        }
        for corridor in &plan.corridors {
            if corridor.distance(x, z) <= corridor.width * 0.5 {
                return program_name(corridor.spine_kind);
            }
        }
        "OPEN FABRIC"
    }
}

fn program_name(program: SpaceProgram) -> &'static str {
    match program {
        SpaceProgram::Arrival => "ARRIVAL",
        SpaceProgram::Reception => "RECEPTION",
        SpaceProgram::MainCorridor => "MAIN CORRIDOR",
        SpaceProgram::SecondaryHall => "SECONDARY HALL",
        SpaceProgram::OpenOffice => "OPEN OFFICE",
        SpaceProgram::PrivateOffice => "PRIVATE OFFICE",
        SpaceProgram::ConferenceRoom => "CONFERENCE ROOM",
        SpaceProgram::WaitingArea => "WAITING AREA",
        SpaceProgram::BreakRoom => "BREAK ROOM",
        SpaceProgram::Storage => "STORAGE",
        SpaceProgram::ServerRoom => "SERVER ROOM",
        SpaceProgram::RestroomCore => "RESTROOM CORE",
        SpaceProgram::Stair => "STAIRWELL",
        SpaceProgram::Mechanical => "MECHANICAL",
        SpaceProgram::Atrium => "ATRIUM",
        SpaceProgram::AbandonedExpansion => "ABANDONED EXPANSION",
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use vackrooms::use_cases::region_plan::spawn_point;

    #[test]
    fn grassland_never_computes_a_plan() {
        let mut locator = SectionLocator::new(42, GeneratorConfig::low_spec());
        assert_eq!(
            locator.describe(34, 12345.0, -9876.0),
            "LEVEL 34 · THE GRASSLAND"
        );
        assert!(locator.plan.is_none(), "no region plan needed outdoors");
    }

    #[test]
    fn spawn_is_on_the_main_corridor() {
        let mut locator = SectionLocator::new(42, GeneratorConfig::low_spec());
        let at = spawn_point(42);
        let text = locator.describe(0, at.x, at.z);
        assert!(
            text.contains("MAIN CORRIDOR") || text.contains("ARRIVAL"),
            "spawn should sit on readable circulation, got: {text}"
        );
        assert!(text.starts_with("LEVEL 0 · "), "{text}");
        assert!(text.contains("REGION (0, 0)"), "{text}");
    }

    #[test]
    fn region_plan_is_cached_until_a_region_crossing() {
        let mut locator = SectionLocator::new(42, GeneratorConfig::low_spec());
        locator.describe(0, 5.0, 5.0);
        let first = locator.cached_region;
        locator.describe(0, 6.0, 6.0);
        assert_eq!(locator.cached_region, first, "same region: no recompute");
        locator.describe(0, 5.0 + REGION_SIZE, 5.0);
        assert_ne!(locator.cached_region, first, "crossing must recompute");
    }

    #[test]
    fn forced_anomaly_is_reported_at_its_own_center() {
        let mut config = GeneratorConfig::low_spec();
        config.anomalies.forced_kind =
            Some(vackrooms::domain::entities::anomaly::AnomalyKind::PillarExpanse);
        let mut locator = SectionLocator::new(42, config);
        // Find the forced instance's center via the same plan the locator
        // uses; standing on its center must classify as the anomaly.
        let plan = generate_region_plan(
            42,
            Position::new(0.0, 0.0),
            REGION_SIZE,
            &config,
            &SimpleNoiseProvider::new(),
        );
        let forced = plan
            .anomalies
            .iter()
            .find(|a| a.kind == vackrooms::domain::entities::anomaly::AnomalyKind::PillarExpanse);
        if let Some(instance) = forced {
            let c = instance.footprint.center;
            let text = locator.describe(0, c.x, c.z);
            assert!(text.contains("PILLAR EXPANSE"), "{text}");
        }
    }
}

/home/dbslim/vackrooms/wasm_frontend/src/adapters/query_config.rs
//! URL query → generation settings. Lets players share worlds by URL:
//!
//! `?seed=1234&voxel_size=0.1&pillars=0.5&walls=1.2&anomalies=0.8`
//!
//! * `seed`    — world seed (u32; any text is hashed so words work too).
//! * `spec`    — shared case-insensitive `low`/`high` quality decision.
//! * `pillars` — structural column density multiplier (0 = none).
//! * `walls`   — office wall density multiplier (0 = open plan).
//! * `atria`   — how much of the world vaults into tall atria.
//! * `lights`  — ceiling light panel density.
//! * `voxel_size` — optional scene resolution in world units. The selected
//!   profile remains in force when the value violates generator limits.
//! * `anomalies`, `anomaly_size` and the per-family knobs control Level 0
//!   phenomena without changing ordinary fabric density.
//! * `remap_intensity`, `remap_distance` and `anomaly_safe_radius` control
//!   deterministic traversal-epoch transformations.
//!
//! Multipliers default to 1.0 and are clamped to 0..=4; physical distances
//! use narrower documented ranges. Kept free of
//! web-sys so it is natively unit-tested; the browser driver only hands in
//! `window.location.search`.

use vackrooms::domain::entities::anomaly::AnomalyKind;
use vackrooms::use_cases::generate_chunk::{AnomalyTuning, GeneratorConfig, LevelTuning};

use crate::adapters::surface_mesh::SurfelDensity;
use crate::application::quality::QualityProfile;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct GenerationParams {
    pub seed: u32,
    pub voxel_size: Option<f32>,
    pub tuning: LevelTuning,
    pub anomalies: AnomalyTuning,
}

/// Exact-key lookup in a location search string (`?a=1&b=2`, leading `?`
/// optional). Every query consumer must go through this (or an equivalent
/// `split('&')` loop): raw `str::find("key=")` substring probes have caused
/// settings to apply that nobody asked for — `?spawn_yaw=2` silently
/// matching `yaw=`, values containing `capture=1`, and the like.
pub fn query_param<'a>(query: &'a str, key: &str) -> Option<&'a str> {
    query
        .trim_start_matches('?')
        .split('&')
        .find_map(|pair| match pair.split_once('=') {
            Some((k, v)) if k == key => Some(v),
            _ => None,
        })
}

/// True when the query holds exactly `key=1`.
pub fn query_flag(query: &str, key: &str) -> bool {
    query_param(query, key) == Some("1")
}

/// Parses `window.location.search` (with or without the leading `?`).
/// Unknown keys are ignored; malformed values fall back to defaults.
pub fn parse_generation_params(query: &str, default_seed: u32) -> GenerationParams {
    let mut params = GenerationParams {
        seed: default_seed,
        voxel_size: None,
        tuning: LevelTuning::default(),
        anomalies: AnomalyTuning::default(),
    };

    for pair in query.trim_start_matches('?').split('&') {
        let Some((key, value)) = pair.split_once('=') else {
            continue;
        };
        let knob = |t: &mut f32| {
            if let Ok(v) = value.parse::<f32>() {
                if v.is_finite() {
                    *t = v.clamp(0.0, 4.0);
                }
            }
        };
        let ranged = |t: &mut f32, min: f32, max: f32| {
            if let Ok(v) = value.parse::<f32>() {
                if v.is_finite() {
                    *t = v.clamp(min, max);
                }
            }
        };
        match key {
            "seed" => {
                params.seed = value.parse::<u32>().unwrap_or_else(|_| hash_seed(value));
            }
            "voxel_size" => {
                params.voxel_size = value.parse::<f32>().ok().filter(|v| v.is_finite());
            }
            "pillars" => knob(&mut params.tuning.pillars),
            "walls" => knob(&mut params.tuning.walls),
            "atria" => knob(&mut params.tuning.atria),
            "lights" => knob(&mut params.tuning.lights),
            // World provisioning: drink, food, and level-door frequency.
            // 0 removes that class of content entirely.
            "almond_water" => knob(&mut params.tuning.almond_water),
            "rations" => knob(&mut params.tuning.rations),
            "level_doors" => knob(&mut params.tuning.level_doors),
            "anomalies" => knob(&mut params.anomalies.frequency),
            "anomaly_size" => ranged(&mut params.anomalies.size, 0.5, 2.0),
            "pillar_expanses" => knob(&mut params.anomalies.pillar_expanses),
            "blackouts" => knob(&mut params.anomalies.blackouts),
            "red_rooms" => knob(&mut params.anomalies.red_rooms),
            "pit_lattices" => knob(&mut params.anomalies.pit_lattices),
            "remap_intensity" => knob(&mut params.anomalies.remap_intensity),
            "remap_distance" => ranged(&mut params.anomalies.remap_distance, 4.0, 64.0),
            "anomaly_safe_radius" => ranged(&mut params.anomalies.safe_radius, 4.0, 32.0),
            // Gameplay tuning: the probability per closed-loop epoch that a
            // red room hashes a single far-side escape breach. 0 seals every
            // loop, 1 guarantees a breach; it is never the entrance.
            "red_escape_bias" => ranged(&mut params.anomalies.red_escape_bias, 0.0, 1.0),
            "archways" => knob(&mut params.anomalies.archways),
            // Deception: the fraction of blackout glimmers placed one
            // segment off the recovery skeleton. The default is high on
            // purpose — most glimmers lie.
            "blackout_decoys" => ranged(&mut params.anomalies.blackout_decoys, 0.0, 0.9),
            // Debug: guarantee one anomaly of this family on the spawn's
            // macro cell (settings menu "Spawn anomaly"). Unknown values
            // leave the world untouched.
            "force_anomaly" => {
                params.anomalies.forced_kind = match value {
                    "pillars" => Some(AnomalyKind::PillarExpanse),
                    "blackout" => Some(AnomalyKind::BlackoutExpanse),
                    "pits" => Some(AnomalyKind::PitLattice),
                    "archway" => Some(AnomalyKind::ArchwayRoom),
                    "redroom" => Some(AnomalyKind::RedRoom),
                    _ => None,
                };
            }
            _ => {}
        }
    }
    params
}

/// Seed + generator configuration derived from the URL query. The main
/// thread and every generation worker call this with the same query string,
/// so all of them voxelize the identical world by construction.
pub fn generator_setup_from_query(query: &str, default_seed: u32) -> (u32, GeneratorConfig) {
    generator_setup_for_quality(query, default_seed, quality_profile_from_query(query))
}

/// Parses `?surfel_density=`, which subdivides the surfel cloud below its
/// one-disc-per-voxel default.
///
/// Read on both sides of the worker boundary rather than sent in the chunk
/// request: `ChunkRequest` carries no spacing, and the workers are where
/// extraction actually happens. The archive follows automatically, because
/// `generator_id` is the query string's own hash — changing the density
/// changes the key, so a denser cloud never reads back a coarser cached one.
pub fn surfel_density_from_query(query: &str) -> SurfelDensity {
    query_param(query, "surfel_density")
        .and_then(|value| value.trim().parse::<f32>().ok())
        .filter(|value| value.is_finite())
        .map(SurfelDensity::new)
        .unwrap_or_default()
}

/// Parses the shared `?spec=low|high` decision. Values are case-insensitive,
/// matching renderer selection, while malformed or missing values retain the
/// established low-profile default.
pub fn quality_profile_from_query(query: &str) -> QualityProfile {
    query_param(query, "spec")
        .and_then(QualityProfile::parse)
        .unwrap_or_default()
}

/// Resolves generation from an already parsed quality decision. The browser
/// composition root uses this together with renderer profile construction so
/// both sides are guaranteed to observe the same value.
pub fn generator_setup_for_quality(
    query: &str,
    default_seed: u32,
    quality: QualityProfile,
) -> (u32, GeneratorConfig) {
    let params = parse_generation_params(query, default_seed);
    let base = match quality {
        QualityProfile::Low => GeneratorConfig::low_spec(),
        QualityProfile::High => GeneratorConfig::high_spec(),
    };
    let configured = base
        .with_tuning(params.tuning)
        .with_anomalies(params.anomalies);
    let configured = params
        .voxel_size
        .and_then(|voxel_size| configured.try_with_voxel_size(voxel_size).ok())
        .unwrap_or(configured);
    (params.seed, configured)
}

/// Non-numeric seeds ("?seed=kitten") hash to a stable u32 (FNV-1a).
fn hash_seed(text: &str) -> u32 {
    let mut h: u32 = 0x811C_9DC5;
    for b in text.bytes() {
        h ^= b as u32;
        h = h.wrapping_mul(0x0100_0193);
    }
    h
}

38. `raycast::traversal_step_budget` — fine.

39. `svo_segment_is_occluded` treats `ChunkMarch::Indeterminate` as occluded — fail-closed, fine.

40. Also `raymarch_svo_single` — `cursor.push()` — bounded stack 9, fine.

One correctness-ish subtlety in raycast: `pop_exited` uses >= vs > comparisons on float positions with next_up nudges. Documented. Not our concern for complexity audit.

41. `atlas::build_mips` recursion — recursion depth = SVO depth ≤ 20ish? depth_cells clamp svo_depth.min(20). Recursion depth ~20, fine. But note: `compute_mip` marks done before descending, and skips children `idx >= mips.len()`. If atlas has a cycle (malformed), done flag prevents infinite recursion. Good.

But there's a real algorithmic subtlety: build_mips is O(N) per upload, but `compute_mip` is called for every node index i in 0..node_count — each call recurses into children, but the `done` flag means each node is computed once total. However the *outer loop* still iterates N and the recursion revisits parent chains... `compute_mip(atlas, i, ...)` — each distinct node does O(1) work + recursion into children that are marked done. Total O(N). Good.

42. `surfel_cloud::build_surfel_cloud` — O(total surfels). `emit_quad` — per quad O(u_count × v_count). Total O(surfels). Fine.

43. `cpu_surfel::render_surfels` — per surfel: project + ellipse bounding box; per-pixel loop over the ellipse's bbox: O(surfel count × footprint area). Footprint area bounded by min_radius_px and screen. Worst case: a surfel near the camera can have a huge footprint (ext_x can be large — clamped by frustum cull with margin, but margin = radius/view_depth, so a huge disc right in front can still have a large footprint; the ellipse bbox could cover much of the screen). The depth/accum/weight are 3 full-frame vec allocations per frame: 3 × W×H × (4+12+4) bytes — for 1920×1080: 3×2M×20 = 120MB? depth f32 8MB, accum [f32;3] 24MB, weight 8MB = 40MB per frame, allocated fresh each render_surfels call (vec! macro). Per-frame allocations of ~40MB — Medium finding (reuse buffers). Also RGB output vec another 6MB. And `stats.fragments` per pixel inner loop — fine.

The ellipse pixel test per surfel: O(footprint) — no HZ culling, no per-pixel early-out; every surfel scans its whole bbox. Could early-exit or use tile culling. Also depth window blending: fragments accumulate without limit — weight can grow unbounded? weight[i] += 1.0 per coplanar fragment; number of coplanar fragments per pixel is bounded by surfel count. Not unbounded growth per se.

44. `face_instances` sort — one-time per chunk build. Fine.

45. `collect_emissive_lights`: `visited` vec allocation per chunk generation. Fine.

46. `section_locator::zone_name` — regenerates plan per region crossing: `generate_region_plan` cost is region-sized. Cached. But `describe` builds a String per call — per frame HUD. Fine.

47. `query_config::query_param` — O(query length). Called multiple times per parse. parse_generation_params is O(n) single pass. Fine.

Now concurrency: within my scope, concurrency surface is minimal:
- input.rs: reads AtomicU32 MOUSE_SENSITIVITY_BITS, AtomicBool INVERT_Y, DOOM_CONTROLS — Relaxed loads. Where are they set? In wasm_frontend/src/lib.rs (out of scope but mentioned in the task: AtomicU32/AtomicBool statics in wasm_frontend/src/lib.rs). The input adapter reads them Relaxed. The concern: MOUSE_SENSITIVITY_BITS is a bit pattern (f32) written by JS; Relaxed single-word atomic store/load is torn-free. Fine.
- LocalChunkSource/ArchivedChunkSource use RefCell — single-threaded; workers get their own sources? Generation workers presumably instantiate their own ArchivedChunkSource or LocalChunkSource. RefCell is not Send — so they're per-thread. No data races (they can't cross threads). Not an issue.
- The hero cache, SoftwareRasterizer — all single-threaded, owned by main thread.

Since the task says the concurrency surface in this repo is small, my area is mostly single-threaded. I should still inventory: RefCells (main-thread only), statics read via Relaxed (input.rs), and note the absence of synchronization issues in adapters. Also worker-related: archived_chunk_source is used in workers? The worker source file is out of my scope (worker_source.rs is in drivers). But ArchivedChunkSource with FileStorage could be used from workers. If two workers shared one ArchivedChunkSource... they don't (each worker has its own). Also the fetch path holds two RefCell borrows — if FileStorage or ChunkArchive::get internally calls into... not re-entrant. OK.

One more: `block_cache::BlockCache` — RefCell<BlockCache> in LocalChunkSource. `preload_targets` borrows mut and calls `cache.get_or_plan(...)` — fine.

Re-entrancy risk: `ArchivedChunkSource::fetch` holds `self.storage.borrow_mut()` and `self.archive.borrow_mut()` while calling `self.inner.load_with_artifacts` — no, the inner load happens BEFORE those borrows. Good. Actually wait, re-check the order:

```
let stored = { let storage = self.storage.borrow(); self.archive.borrow_mut().get(storage.as_ref(), key) };
if let Some(bytes) = stored && let Some(payload) = decode... { return payload; }
self.archive.borrow_mut().note_miss();
let payload = self.inner.load_with_artifacts(...);   // no borrows held here
let encoded = encode_chunk_payload(&payload);
let mut storage = self.storage.borrow_mut();
let mut archive = self.archive.borrow_mut();
if archive.bytes() + encoded.len() > max_bytes { archive.clear(storage.as_mut()); ... }
archive.put(storage.as_mut(), key, &encoded);
drop(archive);
payload
```

Fine. No re-entrancy.

Hmm — one potential issue: `archive.borrow_mut().note_miss()` — note_miss is called on every miss, including corrupt records. Fine.

Now let me also examine `archived_chunk_source` decode path: decode_chunk_payload(&bytes) — bytes from storage: `stored` is Vec<u8> presumably. The payload is fully decoded (owned), so the archive bytes are freed. Good.

  51: impl SoftwareRasterizer {
  52:     /// Returns no override for the reference/no-shadow path, otherwise an
  53:     /// id-matched binary SVO visibility sample for one real scene fixture.
  54:     pub(super) fn resolve_hero_light_visibility(
  55:         &mut self,
  56:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
  57:         center: [f32; 3],
  58:         receiver_world_size: f32,
  59:         is_emissive: bool,
  60:         receiver_distance: f32,
  61:         hero: Option<&LightSource>,
  62:     ) -> Option<HeroLightVisibility> {
  63:         let hero = hero?;
  64:         let affordable =
  65:             receiver_distance <= MAX_RECEIVER_DISTANCE && self.pixel_writes < SHADOW_PIXEL_BUDGET;
  66:         if self.settings.shadows != CpuShadowMode::Hero || is_emissive || !affordable {
  67:             return None;
  68:         }
  69: 
  70:         let query_key = hash_receiver_and_light(center, receiver_world_size, hero.id);
  71:         let cache_index = query_key as usize % self.hero_light_visibility.samples.len();
  72:         let should_trace = (query_key as usize + self.hero_light_visibility.frame_index)
  73:             .is_multiple_of(RETRACE_PERIOD_FRAMES);
  74:         let cached = self.hero_light_visibility.samples[cache_index];
  75:         if !should_trace && cached.valid && cached.query_key == query_key {
  76:             return Some(HeroLightVisibility {
  77:                 light_id: hero.id,
  78:                 visibility: cached.visibility,
  79:             });
  80:         }
  81: 
  82:         let visibility = trace_hero_fixture(
  83:             &self.atlas,
  84:             chunks,
  85:             center,
  86:             receiver_world_size,
  87:             hero.position,
  88:         );
  89:         self.hero_light_visibility.samples[cache_index] = CachedVisibility {
  90:             query_key,
  91:             visibility,
  92:             valid: true,
  93:         };
  94:         Some(HeroLightVisibility {
  95:             light_id: hero.id,
  96:             visibility,
  97:         })
  98:     }
  99: }
 100: 
 101: fn hash_receiver_and_light(center: [f32; 3], world_size: f32, light_id: u64) -> u64 {
 102:     let mut key = light_id ^ 0xcbf2_9ce4_8422_2325;
 103:     for value in [center[0], center[1], center[2], world_size] {
 104:         let bits = if value == 0.0 { 0 } else { value.to_bits() };
 105:         key ^= u64::from(bits);

   1: //! Facade for one complete visible-splat shading evaluation.
   2: 
   3: use crate::application::ports::{ChunkDraw, Environment};
   4: use crate::application::rendering::{DIFFUSE_REFLECTANCE_INV_PI, LinearRgb};

   6: use super::super::camera::Camera;

  22: /// Correctness path with the complete analytic fixture contract.
  23: pub fn shade_with_frame_lighting(
  24:     cam: &Camera,
  25:     environment: &Environment,
  26:     settings: &CpuRenderSettings,
  27:     atlas: &[u32],
  28:     chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
  29:     lighting: FrameLighting<'_>,
  30:     surface: &SplatSurface,
  31: ) -> [u8; 3] {
  32:     shade_predecoded_with_frame_lighting(
  33:         cam,
  34:         environment,
  35:         settings,
  36:         atlas,
  37:         chunks,
  38:         lighting,
  39:         surface,
  40:         decode_albedo(surface.base_color),
  41:         surface
  42:             .is_emissive
  43:             .then(|| emission_strength(surface.base_color)),
  44:     )
  45: }
  46: 
  47: /// Hot production path. Atlas upload already decoded authored sRGB and MIP
  48: /// filtering already averaged in linear space, so shading consumes the
  49: /// prepared albedo without repeating transfer-function powers per splat.
  50: pub(crate) fn shade_predecoded_with_frame_lighting(
  51:     cam: &Camera,
  52:     environment: &Environment,
  53:     settings: &CpuRenderSettings,
  54:     atlas: &[u32],
  55:     chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
  56:     lighting: FrameLighting<'_>,
  57:     surface: &SplatSurface,
  58:     albedo: LinearRgb,
  59:     emission_strength: Option<f32>,
  60: ) -> [u8; 3] {
  61:     let camera_to_surface = [
  62:         surface.center[0] - cam.pos[0],
  63:         surface.center[1] - cam.pos[1],
  64:         surface.center[2] - cam.pos[2],
  65:     ];
  66:     let reconstructed_normal = representative_normal(surface, camera_to_surface);
  67:     let normal = reconstructed_normal.unwrap_or([0.0, 1.0, 0.0]);
  68:     let surface_position =
  69:         representative_surface_position(surface.center, surface.world_size, normal);
  70:     let distance = view_distance(cam, surface, surface_position);
  71: 
  72:     if let Some(emission) =
  73:         emitted_radiance_with_strength(surface, albedo, normal, emission_strength)
  74:     {
  75:         return present_radiance(emission, distance, environment);
  76:     }
  77: 
  78:     let ambient_visibility = if settings.toggles.ambient_occlusion {
  79:         ambient_visibility(surface.crowded_siblings)
  80:     } else {
  81:         1.0
  82:     };
  83:     let mut irradiance =
  84:         ambient_irradiance(environment).map(|channel| channel * ambient_visibility);
  85:     // AO approximates only open environment visibility. The cached field
  86:     // already represents transported diffuse fill and must not be darkened a
  87:     // second time.
  88:     let cached = cached_fill_irradiance(surface, settings);
  89:     let direct_visibility = if settings.shadows == CpuShadowMode::Full {
  90:         DirectLightVisibility::trace_scene(atlas, chunks, surface.world_size)
  91:     } else {
  92:         DirectLightVisibility::unoccluded()
  93:     };
  94:     let fixtures =
  95:         fixture_irradiance_with_visibility(lighting, surface_position, normal, direct_visibility);
  96:     let dynamics = dynamic_irradiance(cam, surface_position, normal);
  97:     let flashlight = if cam.flashlight && reconstructed_normal.is_some() {
  98:         flashlight::beam_contribution(
  99:             cam,
 100:             atlas,
 101:             chunks,
 102:             flashlight::BeamReceiver::surface_sample(
 103:                 surface.center,
 104:                 surface.world_size,
 105:                 surface_position,
 106:                 normal,
 107:             ),
 108:             settings.flashlight_visibility(),
 109:         )
 110:     } else {
 111:         [0.0; 3]
 112:     };
 113: 
 114:     for channel in 0..3 {
 115:         irradiance[channel] +=
 116:             cached[channel] + fixtures[channel] + dynamics[channel] + flashlight[channel];
 117:     }
 118:     let reflected = [
 119:         albedo[0] * irradiance[0] * DIFFUSE_REFLECTANCE_INV_PI,
 120:         albedo[1] * irradiance[1] * DIFFUSE_REFLECTANCE_INV_PI,
 121:         albedo[2] * irradiance[2] * DIFFUSE_REFLECTANCE_INV_PI,
 122:     ];
 123:     present_radiance(reflected, distance, environment)
 124: }

   1: //! Orchestrate one complete CPU-splat frame.
   2: //!
   3: //! The sequence is intentionally visible in [`SoftwareRasterizer::render_cpu_frame`]:
   4: //! capture atmosphere, clear reusable buffers, build the camera, choose chunk
   5: //! order, reject non-contributing chunks, traverse, then draw flare cores.
   6: //! Individual rendering algorithms live behind those named operations.
   7: 
   8: use crate::application::ports::{ChunkDraw, FrameParams};
   9: 
  10: use super::super::camera::{Camera, dot};
  11: use super::SoftwareRasterizer;
  12: use super::frame_work_budget::FrameWorkBudget;
  13: use super::project_aabb_footprint::{
  14:     NEAR_PLANE_DEPTH, aabb_camera_depth_interval, project_screen_footprint,
  15: };
  16: use super::select_lights_for_chunk::select_lights_for_chunk;
  17: 
  18: impl SoftwareRasterizer {
  19:     pub(super) fn render_cpu_frame(&mut self, frame: &FrameParams, chunks: &[ChunkDraw]) {
  20:         self.environment = frame.environment;
  21:         self.frame_work_budget =
  22:             FrameWorkBudget::for_target(&self.settings, self.width(), self.height());
  23:         self.clear();
  24:         self.reset_frame_telemetry();
  25:         if self.atlas.is_empty() {
  26:             return;
  27:         }
  28: 
  29:         let camera = Camera::new(frame, self.width(), self.height(), &self.settings);
  30:         let ordered_chunks = chunks_in_draw_order(
  31:             chunks,
  32:             frame.camera_pos,
  33:             self.settings.toggles.front_to_back,
  34:         );
  35:         // Reused across chunks: one world-fixture scan per chunk, no
  36:         // per-splat allocation and no truncation of overlapping lights.
  37:         let mut chunk_scene_lights = std::mem::take(&mut self.chunk_light_scratch);
  38:         let global_hero_id = frame.active_scene_lights().first().map(|light| light.id);
  39: 
  40:         for chunk in ordered_chunks {
  41:             // Do not keep scanning fixtures/chunks after an absolute work
  42:             // budget has ended traversal for this frame.
  43:             if self.frame_work_budget.writes_exhausted(self.pixel_writes)
  44:                 || self.frame_work_budget.nodes_exhausted(self.visited_nodes)
  45:             {
  46:                 self.budget_exhausted = true;
  47:                 break;
  48:             }
  49:             if self.settings.toggles.distance_cull
  50:                 && !chunk_can_contribute(
  51:                     chunk,
  52:                     &camera,
  53:                     self.width(),
  54:                     self.height(),
  55:                     self.settings.max_draw_distance,
  56:                 )
  57:             {
  58:                 continue;
  59:             }
  60:             select_lights_for_chunk(frame.active_scene_lights(), chunk, &mut chunk_scene_lights);
  61:             self.traverse_voxel_chunk(&camera, chunks, chunk, &chunk_scene_lights, global_hero_id);
  62:         }
  63: 
  64:         self.chunk_light_scratch = chunk_scene_lights;
  65:         self.draw_visible_flare_cores(frame, &camera);
  66:         self.hero_light_visibility.advance_frame();
  67:     }
  68: 
  69:     fn reset_frame_telemetry(&mut self) {
  70:         self.visited_nodes = 0;
  71:         self.budget_exhausted = false;
  72:         self.max_virtual_depth_reached = 0;
  73:         self.splat_count = 0;
  74:         self.pixel_writes = 0;
  75:     }
  76: }
  77: 
  78: /// Front-to-back ordering is an optional performance policy; fine depth
  79: /// testing makes the original chunk order the output-equivalent reference.
  80: fn chunks_in_draw_order(
  81:     chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
  82:     camera_position: [f32; 3],
  83:     front_to_back: bool,
  84: ) -> Vec<&ChunkDraw> {
  85:     let mut ordered: Vec<_> = chunks.iter().collect();
  86:     if front_to_back {
  87:         ordered.sort_by(|a, b| {
  88:             chunk_distance_sq(a, camera_position).total_cmp(&chunk_distance_sq(b, camera_position))
  89:         });
  90:     }
  91:     ordered
  92: }
  93: 
  94: /// Conservative chunk-level distance and screen-bounds rejection.
  95: fn chunk_can_contribute(
  96:     chunk: &ChunkDraw,
  97:     camera: &Camera,
  98:     width: usize,
  99:     height: usize,
 100:     max_draw_distance: f32,
 101: ) -> bool {
 102:     let half_size = chunk.world_size * 0.5;
 103:     let center = [
 104:         chunk.origin[0] + half_size,
 105:         chunk.origin[1] + half_size,
 106:         chunk.origin[2] + half_size,
 107:     ];
 108:     let radius = chunk.world_size * 0.5 * 3.0_f32.sqrt();
 109:     let relative_to_camera = [
 110:         center[0] - camera.pos[0],
 111:         center[1] - camera.pos[1],
 112:         center[2] - camera.pos[2],
 113:     ];
 114: 
 115:     if dot(relative_to_camera, relative_to_camera).sqrt() - radius > max_draw_distance {
 116:         return false;
 117:     }
 118:     let depth_interval = aabb_camera_depth_interval(camera, relative_to_camera, half_size);
 119:     if depth_interval[1] <= NEAR_PLANE_DEPTH {
 120:         return false;
 121:     }
 122: 
 123:     project_screen_footprint(camera, chunk.origin, chunk.world_size, depth_interval[0])
 124:         .is_some_and(|footprint| !footprint.outside_framebuffer(width, height))
 125: }
 126: 
 127: fn chunk_distance_sq(chunk: &ChunkDraw, position: [f32; 3]) -> f32 {
 128:     let half = chunk.world_size * 0.5;
 129:     let dx = chunk.origin[0] + half - position[0];
 130:     let dz = chunk.origin[2] + half - position[2];
 131:     dx * dx + dz * dz
 132: }

 122:     /// Exact fine-depth query for an unshaded square splat.
 123:     ///
 124:     /// Traversal uses this immediately before fixture, hero, and flashlight
 125:     /// evaluation. A `false` result proves that `write_splat` would reject
 126:     /// every pixel, so skipping those radiance calculations cannot change the
 127:     /// framebuffer. A likely-visible splat returns at its first passing pixel.

 131:     }

 139:     fn request_may_contribute(&self, splat: &SquareSplat) -> bool {
 140:         let [x0, x1, y0, y1] = self.clipped_square_bounds(splat.center, splat.half);
 141:         for y in y0..y1 {
 142:             let row = y * self.width;
 143:             for x in x0..x1 {
 144:                 let Some(reciprocal_depth) = splat
 145:                     .depth
 146:                     .reciprocal_at_pixel([x as f32 + 0.5, y as f32 + 0.5])
 147:                 else {
 148:                     continue;
 149:                 };
 150:                 let stored = self.reciprocal_depth[row + x];
 151:                 if reciprocal_depth > stored
 152:                     || (splat.equal_depth_wins && reciprocal_depth == stored)
 153:                 {
 154:                     return true;
 155:                 }
 156:             }
 157:         }
 158:         false
 159:     }

 174:         // bounds their work without changing their requested coverage.
 175:         let [x0, x1, y0, y1] = self.clipped_square_bounds(splat.center, splat.half);

 149:     fn at_root(root: usize, world_size: f32) -> Self {
 150:         Self {
 151:             stack: [TraversalFrame {
 152:                 node_idx: 0,
 153:                 b_min: [0.0; 3],
 154:                 b_max: [0.0; 3],
 155:             }; 9],

 157:             node: root,
 158:             b_min: [0.0; 3],
 159:             b_max: [world_size; 3],
 160:         }
 161:     }
 162: 
 163:     fn push(&mut self) {
 164:         if self.stack_ptr < 8 {
 165:             self.stack[self.stack_ptr] = TraversalFrame {
 166:                 node_idx: self.node,
 167:                 b_min: self.b_min,
 168:                 b_max: self.b_max,
 169:             };
 170:             self.stack_ptr += 1;
 171:         }
 172:     }
 173: 
 174:     /// Pops EVERY level `p` exited: a skip through a plane shared by several
 175:     /// ancestor boxes leaves `p` outside more than one of them, and
 176:     /// descending from a box that no longer contains `p` corrupts the
 177:     /// traversal (wall cracks). Returns `false` when `p` left the chunk.
 178:     fn pop_exited(&mut self, p: [f32; 3]) -> bool {
 179:         while p[0] < self.b_min[0]
 180:             || p[0] > self.b_max[0]
 236:             continue;
 237:         }
 238:         let tie_tolerance = f32::EPSILON * 4.0 * t_exit.abs().max(axis_t.abs()).max(1.0);
 239:         if (t_exit - axis_t).abs() <= tie_tolerance {
 240:             let plane = exit_plane(axis);
 241:             p[axis] = if rd[axis] > 0.0 {
 242:                 plane.next_up()
 243:             } else {
 244:                 plane.next_down()
 245:             };
 246:         }
 247:     }
 248:     Some((t_exit, p))
 249: }

 251: /// Marches one chunk's SVO from `t_entry`. `ro`/`rd` are chunk-local.
 252: /// Distinguishes a proven-clear segment from an invalid or exhausted march.
 253: fn raymarch_svo_single(
 254:     atlas: &[u32],
 255:     ro: [f32; 3],

 261:     voxel_size: f32,
 262:     svo_depth: u8,
 263: ) -> ChunkMarch {
 264:     let mut t = t_entry;
 265:     let mut p = [ro[0] + t * rd[0], ro[1] + t * rd[1], ro[2] + t * rd[2]];
 266:     let mut cursor = TraversalCursor::at_root(chunk_root_idx, world_size);
 267: 
 268:     let max_steps = traversal_step_budget(world_size, voxel_size, svo_depth);
 269:     for _ in 0..max_steps {
 270:         // A finite visibility segment must not spend the rest of its step
 271:         // budget walking geometry beyond the receiver. Equality remains a
 272:         // valid endpoint hit, matching `trace_svo`'s public contract.
 273:         if t > max_t {
 274:             return ChunkMarch::Clear;
 275:         }
 276:         let Some(node) = decode_node(atlas, cursor.node) else {
 277:             return ChunkMarch::Indeterminate;
 278:         };
 279: 
 280:         if node.is_leaf {
 281:             if node.voxel_type != 0 {
 282:                 return ChunkMarch::Hit(RayHit {
 283:                     t,
 284:                     voxel_type: node.voxel_type,
 285:                 });
 286:             }
 287:             // TRAVERSAL: empty-space skip — one step to this empty
 288:             // leaf's exit plane instead of voxel-by-voxel stepping.
 289:             let Some(next) = skip_to_exit(ro, rd, cursor.b_min, cursor.b_max) else {
 290:                 return ChunkMarch::Indeterminate;
 291:             };
 292:             (t, p) = next;
 293:             if !cursor.pop_exited(p) {
 294:                 return ChunkMarch::Clear;
 295:             }
 296:             continue;
 297:         }
 298:

 300:             (cursor.b_min[0] + cursor.b_max[0]) * 0.5,

 249:     fn generate_payload(
 250:         &self,
 251:         origin_x: f32,
 252:         origin_z: f32,
 253:         level: u32,
 254:         lod: u8,
 255:         reality: &RealitySnapshot,
 256:         artifacts: RenderArtifactNeeds,
 257:     ) -> ChunkPayload {
 258:         let config = self.config.with_level(level).at_lod(lod);
 259:         let (halo_grid, halo_world_origin) =
 260:             self.halo_grid(origin_x, origin_z, &config, level, reality);
 261:         let grid = crop_lateral_halo(&halo_grid, 1);
 262:         let lights = collect_emissive_lights(&halo_grid, config.voxel_scale, halo_world_origin, 1);
 263:         let surface = if artifacts.needs_surface_extraction() {
 264:             build_surface_artifacts(
 265:                 &halo_grid,
 266:                 config.voxel_scale,
 267:                 lod,
 268:                 1,
 269:                 artifacts,
 270:                 self.surfel_density,
 271:             )
 272:         } else {
 273:             let mut empty = SurfaceMeshPayload::empty(lod);
 274:             empty.voxel_scale = config.voxel_scale;
 275:             empty
 276:         };
 277: 
 278:         let svo_depth = config.svo_depth();
 279:         let svo = BuildOctreeUseCase::new(&DEFAULT_MATERIAL_PALETTE).execute(
 280:             &grid,
 281:             svo_depth,
 282:             config.svo_world_size(),
 283:         );
 284: 
 285:         let (root, nodes, brick_voxels) = if artifacts.bricks() {
 286:             // Bricking folds the bottom two levels into dense blocks, so
 287:             // the ray stops pointer-chasing where the nodes actually are.
 288:             // Deliberately built from the tree, not the SVDAG: a brick is
 289:             // addressed by a word offset the parent hands out, and shared
 290:             // subtrees would have to agree on one, which is the DAG's whole
 291:             // point undone. Bricking already removes ~93% of the nodes.
 292:             let pool = build_brick_pool(&svo);
 293:             let gpu = BrickPoolGpuSerializer::serialize_to_gpu_data(&pool);
 294:             (gpu.root, gpu.node_data, gpu.voxel_data)
 295:         } else if artifacts.svo_nodes() {
 296:             // SVDAG upload: identical subtrees collapse to one shared block.
 297:             // Traversal-only consumers (the raymarch shader) are agnostic;
 298:             // the collision walk below deliberately keeps the tree because
 299:             // it visits arena nodes positionally.

 188: pub fn render_surfels(
 189:     clouds: &[(&[PackedSurfel], [f32; 3])],
 190:     camera: &SurfelCamera,
 191:     settings: &SurfelRenderSettings,
 192: ) -> SurfelImage {
 193:     let basis = Basis::new(camera, settings);
 194:     let pixels = settings.width * settings.height;
 195: 
 196:     // Per pixel: nearest depth seen, plus a weighted colour accumulator for
 197:     // every fragment inside that pixel's blending window.
 198:     let mut depth = vec![f32::INFINITY; pixels];
 199:     let mut accum = vec![[0.0f32; 3]; pixels];
 200:     let mut weight = vec![0.0f32; pixels];
 201:     let mut stats = SurfelStats::default();
 202: 
 203:     for (surfels, origin) in clouds {
 204:         for surfel in *surfels {
 205:             stats.submitted += 1;
 206:             let local = surfel.world_position();
 207:             let world = [
 208:                 local[0] + origin[0],
 209:                 local[1] + origin[1],
 210:                 local[2] + origin[2],
 280:     }
 281: 
 282:     let mut rgb = vec![0u8; pixels * 3];
 283:     let mut covered = 0usize;
 284:     for i in 0..pixels {
 285:         let color = if weight[i] > 0.0 {
 286:             covered += 1;
 287:             let inv = 1.0 / weight[i];
 288:             let lit = [accum[i][0] * inv, accum[i][1] * inv, accum[i][2] * inv];
 289:             fog_blend(lit, depth[i], &settings.fog)
 290:         } else {
 291:             settings.fog.color
 292:         };
 293:         for c in 0..3 {
 294:             rgb[i * 3 + c] = (color[c].clamp(0.0, 1.0).powf(1.0 / 2.2) * 255.0).round() as u8;
 295:         }
 296:     }
 297: 
 298:     SurfelImage {
 299:         width: settings.width,
 300:         height: settings.height,
 330: /// Jacobian at the surfel's centre, scaled by the radius.
 331: ///
 332: /// Both terms matter, and each of them alone is a distinct, believable
 333: /// failure:
 334: ///
 335: /// * Keep only `t·right / depth` — the naive "project the offset at the
 336: ///   centre's depth" — and any tangent pointing along the view direction
 337: ///   contributes nothing. A floor disc then flattens to a horizontal line,
 338: ///   and the floor renders as a radial spray of dashes from the vanishing
 339: ///   point.
 340: /// * Drop the Jacobian entirely and project the tangent's *endpoint*
 341: ///   exactly, and the secant explodes whenever the endpoint is much nearer
 342: ///   the eye than the centre. Every grazing disc smears across the frame.
 343: ///
 344: /// The second term, `-ndc * (t·forward)`, is the perspective stretch: how
 345: /// much the image slides because the offset changed the depth. With it, a
 346: /// disc lying along the view direction elongates toward the horizon, which
 347: /// is what a real floor tile does.
 348: fn screen_axis(
 349:     tangent: [f32; 3],
 350:     radius: f32,
 351:     ndc: [f32; 2],
 352:     depth: f32,
 353:     basis: &Basis,
 354: ) -> [f32; 2] {
 355:     let along_view = dot(tangent, basis.forward);
 356:     let scale = basis.focal_px * radius / depth;
 357:     [
 358:         scale * (dot(tangent, basis.right) - ndc[0] * along_view),
 359:         -scale * (dot(tangent, basis.up) - ndc[1] * along_view),
 360:     ]
 361: }
 362: 
 363: #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
 364: fn draw_ellipse(
 365:     centre: [f32; 2],
 366:     axis_a: [f32; 2],
 367:     axis_b: [f32; 2],
 368:     view_depth: f32,
 369:     surfel: &PackedSurfel,
 370:     settings: &SurfelRenderSettings,

 371:     depth: &mut [f32],
 372:     accum: &mut [[f32; 3]],
 373:     weight: &mut [f32],
 374:     stats: &mut SurfelStats,
 375: ) -> bool {
 376:     // Screen-space bound of the ellipse: the extent of |a| and |b| on each
 377:     // axis, floored so a surfel that has shrunk below a pixel still marks
 378:     // one. A surface that vanishes at range is worse than one that aliases.
 379:     let ext_x = (axis_a[0].abs() + axis_b[0].abs()).max(settings.min_radius_px);
 380:     let ext_y = (axis_a[1].abs() + axis_b[1].abs()).max(settings.min_radius_px);
 381:     let x0 = (centre[0] - ext_x).floor().max(0.0) as usize;
 382:     let x1 = ((centre[0] + ext_x).ceil() as isize).clamp(0, settings.width as isize) as usize;
 383:     let y0 = (centre[1] - ext_y).floor().max(0.0) as usize;
 384:     let y1 = ((centre[1] + ext_y).ceil() as isize).clamp(0, settings.height as isize) as usize;
 385:     if x0 >= x1 || y0 >= y1 {
 386:         return false;
 387:     }
 388: 
 389:     // Invert [a b] so a pixel offset can be expressed in disc coordinates.
 390:     // A degenerate matrix means the disc is exactly edge-on and covers no
 391:     // area; the min-radius path below still marks its centre pixel.
 392:     let det = axis_a[0] * axis_b[1] - axis_a[1] * axis_b[0];
 393:     let invertible = det.abs() > 1e-6;
 394:     let color = shade(surfel, settings);
 395: 
 396:     let mut touched = false;
 397:     for py in y0..y1 {
 398:         for px in x0..x1 {
 399:             let d = [px as f32 + 0.5 - centre[0], py as f32 + 0.5 - centre[1]];
 400:             let inside = if invertible {
 401:                 let u = (d[0] * axis_b[1] - d[1] * axis_b[0]) / det;
 402:                 let v = (axis_a[0] * d[1] - axis_a[1] * d[0]) / det;
 403:                 u * u + v * v <= 1.0
 404:             } else {
 405:                 d[0].abs() <= settings.min_radius_px && d[1].abs() <= settings.min_radius_px
 406:             };
 407:             if !inside {
 408:                 continue;
 409:             }
 410:             let i = py * settings.width + px;
 411:             stats.fragments += 1;
 412:             touched = true;
 413: 
 414:             if view_depth < depth[i] - settings.blend_window {
 415:                 // A genuinely nearer surface: the window moves to it and
 416:                 // everything accumulated behind is discarded.
 417:                 depth[i] = view_depth;
 418:                 accum[i] = [color[0], color[1], color[2]];
 419:                 weight[i] = 1.0;
 420:             } else if view_depth <= depth[i] + settings.blend_window {
 421:                 // Coplanar with what is already here: average, and keep the
 422:                 // nearest depth so the window tracks the front surface.
 423:                 depth[i] = depth[i].min(view_depth);
 424:                 for c in 0..3 {
 425:                     accum[i][c] += color[c];
 426:                 }
 427:                 weight[i] += 1.0;
 428:             }
 429:         }
 430:     }
 431:     touched
 432: }
 433: 
 434: /// Albedo, baked light, occlusion and emission. Deliberately the simplest
 435: /// shading that can be compared against the other renderers: any difference
 436: /// in the resulting image is then a difference in the *representation*, not
 437: /// in the lighting model.
 438: fn shade(surfel: &PackedSurfel, settings: &SurfelRenderSettings) -> [f32; 3] {
 439:     let packed = material_visual(surfel.material).color;
 440:     let albedo = [

/// Decides which chunks should be resident for a given player position.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub struct StreamingPolicy {
    /// World-space side length of a chunk.
    pub chunk_size: f32,
    /// Chebyshev radius in chunks: 1 -> 3x3 resident set, 2 -> 5x5.
    pub radius: i32,
    /// Chunks within this Chebyshev radius stay resident no matter where
    /// the player looks, so turning can never expose an unloaded chunk.
    /// Only chunks *beyond* it are subject to view culling.
    pub core_radius: i32,
}

impl StreamingPolicy {
    /// Omnidirectional policy: every chunk in the square is desired.
    ///
    /// This is what collision and simulation must use. Physics residency
    /// may never depend on view direction, or walking backwards into an
    /// unloaded chunk would drop the player through the floor.
    pub fn omnidirectional(chunk_size: f32, radius: i32) -> Self {
        Self {
            chunk_size,
            radius,
            core_radius: radius,
        }
    }

    /// Returns the desired chunk origins, sorted nearest-first so the load
    /// budget is always spent on the most urgent chunk.
    pub fn desired_origins(&self, player_x: f32, player_z: f32) -> Vec<(f32, f32)> {
        self.desired_origins_in_view(player_x, player_z, None)
    }

    /// Returns the desired chunk origins, optionally culled to a view cone
    /// beyond `core_radius`.
    ///
    /// The test is conservative: a chunk counts as visible when its
    /// bounding circle touches the cone, not merely when its centre is
    /// inside. A chunk straddling the frustum edge still streams.
    pub fn desired_origins_in_view(
        &self,
        player_x: f32,
        player_z: f32,
        view: Option<ViewCone>,
    ) -> Vec<(f32, f32)> {

Now — the last item: `collect_emissive_lights` worst-case complexity. Let me think again more carefully about the worst case, because I want to state it accurately.

The algorithm: for each unvisited exposed-underside cell, grow rectangle: x-run first (scan right while matches), then z-run (for each z row, scan the whole x-run). Cells are marked visited only when covered by a rectangle. The failing row cells are NOT marked. 

Worst case for repeated scans: consider a pattern of same-material exposed cells forming a "comb": rows 0..H-1, columns 0..W-1 all emissive except column W-1 has holes in every other row (holes at (W-1, 0), (W-1, 2), ...). Start cell (0,0): x-run scans to W-1, stops at hole in row 0. max_x_exclusive = W-1. z-run: rows 1, 2, ...: row 1 matches fully (W-1 cells scanned, none visited), extends; row 2 has hole at W-1 → fails → rectangle is rows 0-1 × cols 0..W-2, marked visited (2×(W-1) cells). Next start: (0,2)? It's unvisited, exposed. Its x-run stops at W-1 (hole). z-run: row 3 matches... etc. Each rectangle scans its own rows: 2(W-1) + 2(W-1)... total work = H×(W-1) scans over the whole grid plus holes tested once per rectangle start in that column... holes at (W-1, even rows) are each tested once by the rectangle starting at (0, even row). So total O(W×H) — linear. 

But here's a nastier case: start cell at (0,0) with x-run that extends over a region whose rows *mostly* match but each fails at a different far column, so the rectangle is small but the scans are wide: e.g., cells (x, z) exposed iff x == z (diagonal). Start (0,0): x-run = 1 (cell (1,0) is not exposed since x≠z). So rectangles are 1×1. Each cell's x-run scan is O(1) and z-run O(1). Total O(V). 

What if the first row is wide but every row below fails at the last column, and the failed rows are NOT marked, so for each start in row 0... they're marked (rows 0..k-1 covered). Only one start per component region... The pattern where a *single failing cell* is re-scanned many times: row 0 is fully exposed across W, rows 1..H-1 are fully exposed except column W-1 has a hole. Start (0,0): x-run reaches W (scans W cells), z-run: row 1: scans W-1 cells, matches; ... rows 1..H-1 all scan W-1 cells, match; final rectangle covers everything. Total O(W×H). Marked all. Done. 

Worst case: multiple rectangles each scanning the same unmarked cells — the only way a cell is scanned but not marked is when its row fails (hole at the far end). So a hole at column c in row r is scanned by every rectangle in row r whose x-run reaches c — but only the FIRST start in row r reaches c (subsequent starts begin after the previous rectangle's end... no wait — subsequent starts begin at the first unvisited exposed cell, which may be in the same row *after* the previous rectangle's x-range... hmm, rectangles in the same row: start at (a, r), covers cols a..b-1. Next unvisited in row r is at b (if exposed) or later. Its x-run extends from b rightward. So the hole at c gets scanned only by the rectangle whose start is left of c and whose x-run reaches c. Since after that rectangle, all cells up to c are visited... but the hole itself is never visited; a later rectangle starting at c+1 doesn't rescan c. So each hole is scanned at most O(1) times per... no wait, each hole is scanned once per rectangle start in its row that precedes it — but only the first such start (whose x-run reaches the hole). Actually multiple rectangles in the same row are disjoint in x, so only the one containing x-interval ending at c scans c. So each cell/hole is scanned O(1) times amortized. Total O(V). 

Wait, one more subtlety: rows are scanned in z order within a rectangle's z-run, and those row scans include cells that were already visited (marked by this same rectangle as it grows? No — cells are marked only after the full z-run completes; during the z-run, cells in earlier rows are NOT yet marked... they're marked after the loop finishes. Actually `for z in start_z..max_z_exclusive { for x ... visited = true }` happens after the z-run determination. So during the z-run, rows above are unvisited; the scan re-tests them — but they match (they're the same material, exposed, unvisited — that's why the rectangle grew). So each cell is scanned once per z-row test: the cells in row r are scanned for each z-extension test, i.e., (max_z - start_z) times? No — the z-run test for row z scans the x-range; so cell (x, r) in row r is scanned once when the z-run tests row r. Each row is tested once per rectangle. So each cell scanned O(1) times per rectangle that covers it, and each covered cell belongs to exactly one rectangle. The failing row cells: scanned once per rectangle that attempts to extend into that row — and each such rectangle is the one that "owns" the rows above it. Could two different rectangles attempt to extend into the same failing row? Rectangle A covers rows 0..k-1 cols 0..W-1 (failed at row k due to hole at W-1). Cells in row k are unmarked. Start (0,k): its x-run starts at col 0... scans cols 0..W-1 — the same cells scanned again by A's failed row test! So the cells of a failing row are scanned twice: once by the rectangle above, once by the rectangle starting in that row. If the row fails repeatedly... rectangle B starting at (0,k): x-run stops at hole → max_x = W-1; z-run: row k+1 matches... rows k+1..k+j match; rectangle B covers those; the hole row k+1's cells get scanned... each row below a failure is scanned by the rectangle above (once, failing) and again by the rectangle starting at it (matching). So each cell scanned ≤ 2× amortized... but what about a cell in a row that fails at a *different* column each time? E.g., row r fails at column r mod W — the failing cell is scanned by every rectangle start in rows above that attempts to extend past it... no — a rectangle starting at (0, r-1) scans row r's full x-range, hits the hole at (r mod W, r), fails. The cells in row r to the LEFT of the hole are scanned (unmarked). Then rectangle B starts at (0, r): x-run: scans cells 0..hole-1 again — they're unvisited, match — extends to hole; fails z-run at row r+1... The cells 0..hole-1 of row r get scanned: once by A's z-test of row r, once by B's x-run, and once by each of B's z-tests of subsequent rows?? No — B's z-run tests rows r+1, r+2, ..., each scans 0..hole-1 — those cells in row r+1 are scanned by A (row r+1 test) AND by B (row r+1 z-test) — but B covers them eventually if they match... they match so B extends over them, marks them. So cells in rows covered by B are scanned: once by A's failed z-test + once by B's z-test + ... hmm, B's z-run tests each row once, so cell (x, r+1) is scanned by: A's z-test of row r+1 (A's x-range = 0..W-1, so yes) and B's z-test of row r+1. 2 scans. Then B covers rows r+1..r+j. Cell in row r+j+1 (failing): scanned by B's z-test (fails) and then by C's x-run... so each cell is scanned O(1) times per "adjacent rectangle pair" — bounded by a small constant per cell? The x-run of C re-scans cells in the failing row r+j+1 from 0..hole-1; those cells were scanned by B's failed z-test. That's 2. But ALSO: B's z-run for rows r+1..r+j tests the x-range 0..hole-1 — cells in those rows were also scanned by A's failed z-test... 

OK in the pathological case where every row fails at a different column (holes at (c(r), r), c(r) decreasing), the rectangle above scans the full row (W cells) and fails; the next rectangle starts at (0, r) and its x-run scans to c(r) cells... So total work = sum over rows of W (from the rectangle above's failed test) = O(H×W) plus the successful runs. So worst case O(V) where V = W×H×D with a small constant (≤ ~3 per cell). I'll state it as O(V) amortized with each failing-row scan adding at most one extra full-row pass: worst case O(2V) per y-slab... Hmm, actually there's a subtle worst case that's worse: rows alternate between "fails at far right" and "matches": rectangle from row 0 covers rows 0..1 (row 1 matches fully, wait row 1 must fail too for the pattern...). Let me just say: amortized O(V) per y-layer, worst case each y-layer scanned twice (once by a failing z-test, once by the covering rectangle) → O(V) overall. Actually I realize the failed row's cells are scanned by the failed z-test of rectangle A (full x-range) — but only for the rows immediately below A's last matched row. Each row is the "failed row" of at most one rectangle (the rectangle covering the rows above it), and each row is covered by at most one rectangle. So per row: ≤ 1 full x-range scan (failed) + ≤ 1 full x-range scan (successful z-test) + 1 x-run scan (as start row) = O(W) per row → O(W×H) per y-slab. Total O(V). 

Good: collect_emissive_lights is O(V) amortized — I'll note the constant and the per-component allocations. Actually wait — one more: the x-run scan of the start row is part of the rectangle's own work, and the z-tests... the successful z-tests are O(area). So per row O(W) amortized. Fine: O(V).

Hmm wait, actually there IS a quadratic worst case: consider a "staircase" of holes: row r has its hole at column c(r) = r mod W... The rectangle above row r fails scanning W cells. Rectangle starting at row r scans c(r) cells in x-run, then its z-tests scan c(r) cells per row. If c(r) is small for many rows... no, then rectangle is small. The failed z-test scans W cells only for the rectangle that reaches row r's hole — and it fails at the FIRST hole in that row... it scans the full x-range (W cells) once per rectangle. Number of rectangles ≤ number of start cells ≤ V. So worst case: each of R rectangles does a full-width failed row scan → O(R×W). R ≤ number of rows that contain a hole... could be H. So O(H×W) per y-layer. Still O(V). Good — linear. Unless... R rectangles each scanning W cells where R = H → O(HW) = O(V). Fine.

## ALGORITHM FINDINGS

**F1 (High): Per-splat SVO segment occlusion queries in Full shadow mode are un-budgeted and multiply into O(S × L × 4 × C × B).**
- File: shading/shade_visible_splat.rs:89-95 + trace_direct_light_visibility.rs:59-72 + evaluate_fixture_irradiance.rs:159-200 (4 Gauss samples) + raycast.rs:444-493.
- Full shadow mode (Maximum preset default: quality_preset.rs:22 `CpuShadowMode::Full`) traces one SVO segment per point light and per 2x2 Gauss sample per rectangle light, for every shaded splat. Each query is O(C) chunk slab tests plus up to B=32,768-step march (raycast.rs:117 `MAX_TRAVERSAL_STEPS`, budget at raycast.rs:127-145). With S≈10^4-10^5 splats, L≈10^2 lights, C≈10^2 chunks this is 10^8-10^10 slab tests/frame. Hero mode has the 1.5M-pixel budget gate (resolve_hero_light_visibility.rs:64-65) and a 65k-entry cache, but Full has neither. 
- Fix: gate Full mode with the same pixel-write budget or a per-frame ray budget; add a chunk spatial index (grid/hash on chunk coords) so queries skip non-intersected chunks in O(1)-ish instead of O(C); consider the hero-style cache for Full mode too.

**F2 (High): Flashlight occlusion ray per splat per frame — no cache, O(S × C × B) with flashlight on.**
- flashlight.rs:158-181 (segment_is_occluded), called from beam_contribution flashlight.rs:186-203, per splat in shade_visible_splat.rs:97-109 when `cam.flashlight && rt_beam_occlusion`. Every frame, every lit splat issues a fresh SVO march. 
- Fix: reuse the HeroLightVisibilityCache pattern (key on receiver center+size+frame), or trace the beam once per coarse screen tile / per chunk; or reuse previous frame's beam via reprojection.

**F3 (Medium): Chunk list linear scan per visibility query — O(C) slab tests even for rays that miss everything.**
- raycast.rs:444-493 `svo_segment_is_occluded` iterates every resident chunk; with ~100-200 resident chunks (streaming.rs radius ~5 → 11×11) each query does C slab tests and C potential marches. Multiplied by F2/F1 this is the constant-factor killer.
- Fix: bucket chunks by coarse world grid cell (HashMap<(i64,i64,i64), Vec<idx>> or a uniform grid) and query only cells along the ray (Amanatides & Woo style).

**F4 (Medium): Hero-light visibility cache is never invalidated on atlas edits; stale shadows served up to RETRACE_PERIOD (4) frames after a chunk edit.**
- resolve_hero_light_visibility.rs:70-93. The cache is keyed on receiver+light only; `upload_atlas_rows` (rasterizer.rs:354-358) mutates the atlas and MIPs but doesn't touch hero_light_visibility. After editing geometry, a receiver in the edited chunk keeps its old visibility for up to 4 frames.
- Fix: bump an `atlas_epoch` on upload and clear (or age) cache entries when epoch changes; or fold epoch into query_key.

**F5 (Medium): Direct-mapped 65,536-entry hero cache with per-frame retrace scheduling; hash collisions cause full retrace storms in dense scenes.**
- resolve_hero_light_visibility.rs:41, 70-93. All receivers with query_key % 65536 == k share a slot; when two keys collide in the same frame, both trace (correct but doubles cost); with fine voxels many receivers map to the same slot... Actually correctness is maintained via query_key check. The should_trace stagger uses query_key+frame_index so retraces are spread. Collisions only waste work. Also the modulo-based indexing is a power-of-two-ish? 65536 = 2^16; the hash mixes, fine.
- It's more of a Low: work duplication on collision. I'll fold this into a Low or mention under hero cache.

**F6 (Medium): Per-frame chunk sort + Vec allocation: O(C log C) with a fresh Vec<&ChunkDraw> every frame.**
- render_cpu_frame.rs:80-92 (`chunks_in_draw_order`), called at render_cpu_frame.rs:30-34. Reallocates and re-sorts every frame even when camera hasn't moved.
- Fix: reuse a scratch Vec (like chunk_light_scratch), skip sort when front_to_back disabled (already), and skip sort if camera moved < epsilon or chunk set unchanged.

**F7 (Medium): select_lights_for_chunk scans ALL world fixtures per chunk per frame: O(C × L).**
- select_lights_for_chunk.rs:15-25, called render_cpu_frame.rs:60. With L in the hundreds and C in the hundreds, that's 10^4-10^5 distance tests/frame — not huge, but with the fixture scan happening even when the chunk has few splats... The fix: build a spatial hash of lights once per frame (or on edit), query per chunk O(1) amortized. Also note the `fixtures.iter().find(|light| light.id == id)` in traverse_voxel_scene/mod.rs:78 per chunk O(L).

**F8 (Medium): Defeated splat pre-check scans the full footprint twice (may_contribute + write_splat).**
- write_depth_tested_splats.rs:139-159 (request_may_contribute), 166-200 (write_splat). For a splat that passes the HZ test but fails fine depth entirely, the code scans all footprint pixels (up to (2·half_px+2)², half_px ≤ 16 → ~1024 px) once in may_contribute then again in write_splat. HZ only rejects fully-covered tiles.
- Fix: merge the two passes (probe while shading then write), or make write_splat return "first passing pixel" info... Actually simplest: in the deferred path, do the depth test pass and shading only for passing pixels — a fused loop. Low/Medium impact since HZ catches most occluded subtrees.

**F9 (Medium): `trace_svo` allocates + sorts per call — O(C log C) + O(C) allocation; fine for debug path but it's the public nearest-hit API.**
- raycast.rs:350-437, Vec::with_capacity at 363, sort at 389. Called only from tests currently. Low severity, note as API trap.

**F10 (Low/Medium): cpu_surfel::render_surfels allocates ~40 MB of buffers per frame (3 full-frame buffers + RGB).**
- cpu_surfel.rs:198-200, 282. depth 4B/px, accum 12B/px, weight 4B/px, rgb 3B/px → for 1080p ~43 MB per frame, zeroed each call. Also `draw_ellipse` (cpu_surfel.rs:364-432) scans the full ellipse bbox per surfel with no HZ/tile culling and no early-out, O(S × bbox).
- Fix: reuse buffers in the renderer struct (like DepthTestedSplatTarget), clear only touched tiles; add a coarse z-buffer (8px tiles) to skip occluded surfels' bbox scans.

**F11 (Low): `render_surfels` inner loop: per-pixel `powf(1/2.2)` gamma at 294 — fine (once per pixel). No issue.** Skip.

**F12 (Low): `build_mips` / `rebuild_mips_in_node_range` — O(N) and O(range) respectively; good. But `upload_atlas` does `texels.to_vec()` + full `build_mips` per full upload: O(N) memcpy + O(N) build — acceptable (once per upload). Note row updates are O(row) — good design.** No finding, but I can note that `validate_independent_node_block` scans the whole row update O(texels) — fine.

Wait — one important thing about `update_atlas_rows`: `rebuild_mips_in_node_range` uses done_scratch sized to the *node range* and `compute_mip` with done_base = node_range.start. But `compute_mip` recursion goes to children via child_base — the validation ensures children are within the global range (validate_independent_node_block checks child_base >= global_nodes.start && child_end <= global_nodes.end). So done_scratch indexing works. Good.

But hmm — there's a subtle bug-ish behavior: `rebuild_mips_in_node_range` fills the mips for the whole node range and recomputes each node; children within range get recomputed too (they're in the range). Total O(range × 8). Fine.

**F13 (Low): `collect_emissive_lights` — O(V) amortized with up to ~2-3 passes per cell; allocates a V-bool visited per chunk. Fine.** Maybe note `visited` allocation per generation call — one-time per chunk load, fine.

**F14 (Medium): `section_locator::describe` — regenerates region plan per region crossing; `generate_region_plan` is O(region area); fine because cached. But `describe` allocates a String per call — if called per frame it's per-frame allocation. Low.** Let me check who calls describe — HUD. Probably per frame or on change. Low.

**F15 (Low): `chunk_codec` — encode/decode O(bytes) with pre-sized capacity; decode bounds each count by remaining bytes — no OOM. The `pit_hazards` encode allocates a temp Vec per hazard (chunk_codec.rs:153) — negligible.** Good overall.

**F16: Recursion depth — `traverse_voxel_node` recursion depth ≤ SVO depth + max_virtual_depth (≤ 8+8=16... actually svo_depth up to 8 in settings? config.svo_depth() could be up to 10? MAX budget clamps svo_depth.min(20)). Depth ~20 + 8 = 28 frames — safe. `walk` in local_chunk_source — depth = SVO depth ≤ ~10 — safe. `compute_mip` — depth ≤ 20 — safe.** No finding; note in report.

**F17: `raycast` step budget — the budget is a safety valve; `ChunkMarch::Indeterminate` fails closed. But note: a *fully empty* chunk with a deep SVO costs up to B steps per query — with fine voxels B≈6000+ (test at raycast.rs:516-519 says fine > 6000). Each step is decode + skip_to_exit + pop_exited.** This is inherent to the design; flag as part of F1/F2.

**F18 (Medium): `write_depth_tested_splats::clear` — full-frame clears of rgba (4B), reciprocal (4B), coarse (2 arrays) each frame: O(W×H) memset — that's inherent and fast. Fine.**

**F19: HZ `coarse_occludes` is conservative-stale by design (recorded max never updated on nearer writes → miss culls). Fine.**

**F20 (Low): `frame_work_budget` recomputed per frame — O(1). Fine.**

## CONCURRENCY FINDINGS

The adapters layer is single-threaded by construction. Inventory:
- SoftwareRasterizer: all mutable state owned by main thread; no atomics.
- HeroLightVisibilityCache: inside rasterizer.
- LocalChunkSource: RefCell<BlockCache> — single-threaded borrow; not Send/Sync, so can't cross to workers (worker sources are constructed per worker presumably).
- ArchivedChunkSource: RefCell<ChunkArchive> + RefCell<Box<dyn ArchiveStorage>> — two simultaneous mutable borrows in fetch (lines ~127-131...). Actually let me get the exact lines. In fetch: `let mut storage = self.storage.borrow_mut(); let mut archive = self.archive.borrow_mut();` — held simultaneously; safe because no re-entrancy into self while held. But: is there re-entrancy risk if `ChunkArchive::clear`/`put` calls into storage which calls back? Storage is a trait (FileStorage/MemoryStorage) — no callback into the source. Safe.
- input.rs: reads crate-level atomics: MOUSE_SENSITIVITY_BITS (AtomicU32), INVERT_Y (AtomicBool), DOOM_CONTROLS (AtomicBool) with Relaxed ordering (input.rs:9-10, 57-60). These are set from JS via exported setters. Relaxed is sufficient for single-word loads (no torn reads on aligned atomics; no ordering constraints needed since these are independent settings). One caveat: if the f32 sensitivity is stored as bits and the writer is JS via `Atomics.store` — a u32 store is atomic anyway. Fine. Note: reading `f32::from_bits` of an AtomicU32 with Relaxed is a standard pattern; no torn read. No finding, but document.
- The `RefCell` in LocalChunkSource: `halo_grid` borrows `blocks.borrow_mut()` and then calls `generator.execute_from_plans` while holding the borrow — if the generator (or noise provider) called back into the source, it would panic (RefCell re-borrow). Let me check: `halo_grid` (lines 190-219) — `let mut blocks = self.blocks.borrow_mut(); let block = blocks.get_or_plan(...)` — the borrow is held across `generator.execute_from_plans(..., Some(block.plans()))` — wait, does the borrow end? `block` borrows from `blocks`; `blocks` is alive until end of halo_grid. `get_or_plan` returns &WorldBlock tied to the RefMut borrow. `generator.execute_from_plans` receives `Some(block.plans())` — a reference into the block — while `self.blocks` is mutably borrowed. If execute_from_plans → noise → ... no call path back into self.blocks (noise is a separate provider). But `preload_targets` also calls get_or_plan with `&mut |_, _| {}` — fine. So no re-entrancy panic. But this is a fragility worth noting: the RefCell borrow spans a long computation (generation!) — if any future code path in generation needs `self.blocks` (e.g., another chunk), it panics. Note as Low/fragility.
- Similarly `preload_targets` (local_chunk_source.rs:112-129) borrows mut and calls get_or_plan with a no-op progress callback — safe.
- Worker-related: chunk_codec is the worker↔main wire format; decode is allocation-bounded via `r.len(min_bytes)`. Workers are out of my scope (drivers), but codec correctness affects workers: any decode failure → chunk dropped → re-request. No races in codec itself.
- No data races, no deadlocks, no shared mutable statics in adapters. All memory ordering is Relaxed on independent single-word settings — correct.
- Determinism: generation is deterministic per seed; surfel/fixture collection is deterministic (explicit loops + sort + dedup). The only nondeterminism would be HashMap iteration — avoided via sort (collect_emissive_lights.rs:94). Good.

Concurrency inventory table:
1. crate::MOUSE_SENSITIVITY_BITS / INVERT_Y / DOOM_CONTROLS (lib.rs) — read in input.rs Relaxed; set by JS exports; single-word atomics, no torn reads.
2. RefCell<BlockCache> in LocalChunkSource — single-threaded; borrow held across generation call (fragility, no panic today).
3. RefCell<ChunkArchive> + RefCell<Box<dyn ArchiveStorage>> in ArchivedChunkSource — double mutable borrow in fetch; no re-entrancy; single-threaded.
4. HeroLightVisibilityCache / SoftwareRasterizer state — single-threaded main thread only.
5. chunk_codec — plain functions, no shared state; bounded allocations protect worker decode path.
6. RNG: none shared — noise provider per source; seeds deterministic. No static mut, no thread_local in adapters.

Now the Big-O table. Variables:
- C = resident chunk count (~10²)
- L = scene light count (active fixtures, ~10²)
- S = splats drawn per frame (≤ node budget; ~10⁴-10⁵)
- P = framebuffer pixels (≤ 2M at 1080p)
- B = ray-march step budget per chunk (160..32,768; fine voxels ~6,000+)
- N = atlas nodes (per chunk 10³-10⁵)
- V = voxel volume of one chunk grid
- Q = quads per chunk
- T = surfels per chunk
- R = bytes of one chunk payload

Table rows:
1. SVO traversal + splatting (traverse_voxel_scene) — O(nodes visited + Σ splat footprint px) per frame; bounded by node/pixel budget. Space O(1) working.
2. Secondary visibility ray (svo_segment_is_occluded) — O(C·B) worst per query (C slab tests + up to B steps in crossed chunks); amortized less. F1/F2 multiply: O(S·L·4·C·B) worst per frame with Full shadows + flashlight.
3. trace_svo — O(C log C + C·B) per query (sort + march).
4. build_mips — O(N) time, O(N) space per upload.
5. rebuild_mips_in_node_range — O(row nodes) per row update.
6. update_prepared_atlas_rows — O(row texels + row nodes) validated, no realloc.
7. write_splat / HZ — O(footprint px) per splat, O(P) state; coarse test O(tiles under rect).
8. request_may_contribute — O(footprint px) per splat (worst, fully occluded).
9. select_lights_for_chunk — O(L) per chunk → O(C·L) per frame.
10. chunks_in_draw_order — O(C log C) per frame + O(C) alloc.
11. resolve_hero_light_visibility — O(1) amortized per splat (cache); O(C·B) on retrace.
12. flashlight beam_contribution — O(1) irradiance + O(C·B) occlusion per splat.
13. render_surfels (cpu_surfel) — O(T·avg ellipse bbox) time, O(P) space + 40MB per-frame allocs.
14. build_surfel_cloud — O(T).
15. build_face_instances — O(Q log Q) (sort) — one-time per chunk.
16. collect_emissive_lights — O(V) amortized + O(V log V) worst-ish (sort of lights, usually tiny).
17. chunk_codec encode/decode — O(R), bounded allocations.
18. BlockCache — O(4) lookup; O(plan) on miss; bounded memory.
19. ArchivedChunkSource fetch — O(R) decode hit; O(generate) miss; archive clear O(archive) on budget.
20. crop_lateral_halo / neighbor_occlusion_mask — O(V).
21. extract_collision_boxes — O(nodes).
22. section_locator describe — O(1) cached + O(plan items) classify; O(region) on crossing.
23. raycast ray_box_interval — O(1).

Highest-leverage optimizations (ranked):
1. Chunk spatial index for visibility queries (kill O(C) per query; turns F1/F2 into O(crossed chunks)) — biggest multiplier since it's under S×L×4 queries.
2. Budget + cache the Full-shadow mode: per-frame ray budget like hero's SHADOW_PIXEL_BUDGET; reuse hero cache or a coarse screen-space shadow map for fixtures.
3. Cache flashlight occlusion (per-receiver cache like hero, or per-chunk beam queries).
4. Reuse per-frame scratch buffers: chunk order Vec, surfel render buffers (40MB/frame alloc).
5. Precompute light spatial index (one pass per frame, O(L)) so select_lights_for_chunk is O(1)-amortized instead of O(C×L).
6. Fuse may_contribute + write_splat double scan; optionally vectorize per-pixel loops (autovectorizable; note in report that write_splat loops are vectorization-friendly; wasm SIMD).

Also the HZ: fine.

710:    fn clearing_frees_the_log_but_keeps_it_usable() {



    /// Counts a generation that bypassed the archive, for the hit rate.
    pub fn note_miss(&mut self) {
        self.misses += 1;
    }

    /// Bytes the archive currently occupies.
    pub fn bytes(&self) -> u64 {
        self.end
    }

    /// Drops every record and starts the log again.
    ///
    /// An append-only log cannot free one record, so a bounded archive
    /// discards all of them at once. That is crude — it throws away chunks
    /// the player is standing next to along with ones they left an hour ago —
    /// but it is correct, it is O(1), and the cost of being wrong is one
    /// regeneration. A reuse-ordered eviction would need either a rewritten
    /// log or an index of holes, which is the complexity this format exists
    /// to avoid.
    pub fn clear(&mut self, storage: &mut dyn ArchiveStorage) {
        storage.reset();
        storage.append(&self.identity.to_bytes());
        self.index.clear();
        self.end = HEADER_BYTES as u64;
    }
}

//! Stack-based SVO ray traversal — the CPU twin of the GLSL `raymarchSVO`.
//!
//! Primary visibility comes from splatting, not marching. This module offers
//! two explicit query contracts: [`trace_svo`] returns the nearest hit for
//! reference/debug callers, while [`svo_segment_is_occluded`] is the
//! allocation-free early-out used by flashlight and hero visibility.
//! Both share the same bounded single-chunk marcher. Its work budget is
//! derived from the chunk's real voxel resolution, so selecting 0.05-unit
//! voxels cannot silently inherit the old 160-step coarse-scene limit:
//!
//! 1. Slab-test every chunk AABB and optionally sort hits near-to-far
//!    ([`trace_svo`], controlled by `rt_f2b`).
//! 2. Inside a chunk, descend toward the octant containing the current
//!    point; **empty-space skip** jumps a whole empty leaf/octant in one
//!    step via its AABB exit plane instead of voxel-by-voxel DDA.
//! 3. After a skip, pop every ancestor the point exited (a skip through a
//!    shared plane can leave several boxes at once).

use crate::application::ports::ChunkDraw;

use super::atlas::decode_node;
