#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct RayHit {
    pub t: f32,
    pub voxel_type: u32,
}

pub(super) struct RayInterval {
    pub entry: f32,
    pub exit: f32,

710:    fn clearing_frees_the_log_but_keeps_it_usable() {



    /// Counts a generation that bypassed the archive, for the hit rate.
    pub fn note_miss(&mut self) {
        self.misses += 1;
    }

    /// Bytes the archive currently occupies.
    pub fn bytes(&self) -> u64 {
        self.end
    }

    /// Drops every record and starts the log again.
    ///
    /// An append-only log cannot free one record, so a bounded archive
    /// discards all of them at once. That is crude — it throws away chunks
    /// the player is standing next to along with ones they left an hour ago —
    /// but it is correct, it is O(1), and the cost of being wrong is one
    /// regeneration. A reuse-ordered eviction would need either a rewritten
    /// log or an index of holes, which is the complexity this format exists
    /// to avoid.
    pub fn clear(&mut self, storage: &mut dyn ArchiveStorage) {
        storage.reset();
        storage.append(&self.identity.to_bytes());
        self.index.clear();
        self.end = HEADER_BYTES as u64;
    }
}

    }

Now — the last item: `collect_emissive_lights` worst-case complexity. Let me think again more carefully about the worst case, because I want to state it accurately.

The algorithm: for each unvisited exposed-underside cell, grow rectangle: x-run first (scan right while matches), then z-run (for each z row, scan the whole x-run). Cells are marked visited only when covered by a rectangle. The failing row cells are NOT marked. 

Worst case for repeated scans: consider a pattern of same-material exposed cells forming a "comb": rows 0..H-1, columns 0..W-1 all emissive except column W-1 has holes in every other row (holes at (W-1, 0), (W-1, 2), ...). Start cell (0,0): x-run scans to W-1, stops at hole in row 0. max_x_exclusive = W-1. z-run: rows 1, 2, ...: row 1 matches fully (W-1 cells scanned, none visited), extends; row 2 has hole at W-1 → fails → rectangle is rows 0-1 × cols 0..W-2, marked visited (2×(W-1) cells). Next start: (0,2)? It's unvisited, exposed. Its x-run stops at W-1 (hole). z-run: row 3 matches... etc. Each rectangle scans its own rows: 2(W-1) + 2(W-1)... total work = H×(W-1) scans over the whole grid plus holes tested once per rectangle start in that column... holes at (W-1, even rows) are each tested once by the rectangle starting at (0, even row). So total O(W×H) — linear. 

But here's a nastier case: start cell at (0,0) with x-run that extends over a region whose rows *mostly* match but each fails at a different far column, so the rectangle is small but the scans are wide: e.g., cells (x, z) exposed iff x == z (diagonal). Start (0,0): x-run = 1 (cell (1,0) is not exposed since x≠z). So rectangles are 1×1. Each cell's x-run scan is O(1) and z-run O(1). Total O(V). 

What if the first row is wide but every row below fails at the last column, and the failed rows are NOT marked, so for each start in row 0... they're marked (rows 0..k-1 covered). Only one start per component region... The pattern where a *single failing cell* is re-scanned many times: row 0 is fully exposed across W, rows 1..H-1 are fully exposed except column W-1 has a hole. Start (0,0): x-run reaches W (scans W cells), z-run: row 1: scans W-1 cells, matches; ... rows 1..H-1 all scan W-1 cells, match; final rectangle covers everything. Total O(W×H). Marked all. Done. 

Worst case: multiple rectangles each scanning the same unmarked cells — the only way a cell is scanned but not marked is when its row fails (hole at the far end). Next unvisited in row r is at b (if exposed) or later. Its x-run extends from b rightward. So the hole at c gets scanned only by the rectangle whose start is left of c and whose x-run reaches c. Since after that rectangle, all cells up to c are visited... but the hole itself is never visited; a later rectangle starting at c+1 doesn't rescan c. So each cell scanned O(1) times per rectangle that covers it, and each covered cell belongs to exactly one rectangle. The failing row cells: scanned once per rectangle that attempts to extend into that row — and each such rectangle is the one that "owns" the rows above it. ## ALGORITHM FINDINGS

**F1 (High): Per-splat SVO segment occlusion queries in Full shadow mode are un-budgeted and multiply into O(S × L × 4 × C × B).**
- File: shading/shade_visible_splat.rs:89-95 + trace_direct_light_visibility.rs:59-72 + evaluate_fixture_irradiance.rs:159-200 (4 Gauss samples) + raycast.rs:444-493.
- Full shadow mode (Maximum preset default: quality_preset.rs:22 `CpuShadowMode::Full`) traces one SVO segment per point light and per 2x2 Gauss sample per rectangle light, for every shaded splat. Each query is O(C) chunk slab tests plus up to B=32,768-step march (raycast.rs:117 `MAX_TRAVERSAL_STEPS`, budget at raycast.rs:127-145). With S≈10^4-10^5 splats, L≈10^2 lights, C≈10^2 chunks this is 10^8-10^10 slab tests/frame. Hero mode has the 1.5M-pixel budget gate (resolve_hero_light_visibility.rs:64-65) and a 65k-entry cache, but Full has neither. 
- Fix: gate Full mode with the same pixel-write budget or a per-frame ray budget; add a chunk spatial index (grid/hash on chunk coords) so queries skip non-intersected chunks in O(1)-ish instead of O(C); consider the hero-style cache for Full mode too.

**F2 (High): Flashlight occlusion ray per splat per frame — no cache, O(S × C × B) with flashlight on.**
- flashlight.rs:158-181 (segment_is_occluded), called from beam_contribution flashlight.rs:186-203, per splat in shade_visible_splat.rs:97-109 when `cam.flashlight && rt_beam_occlusion`. Every frame, every lit splat issues a fresh SVO march. 
- Fix: reuse the HeroLightVisibilityCache pattern (key on receiver center+size+frame), or trace the beam once per coarse screen tile / per chunk; or reuse previous frame's beam via reprojection.

**F3 (Medium): Chunk list linear scan per visibility query — O(C) slab tests even for rays that miss everything.**
- raycast.rs:444-493 `svo_segment_is_occluded` iterates every resident chunk; with ~100-200 resident chunks (streaming.rs radius ~5 → 11×11) each query does C slab tests and C potential marches. Multiplied by F2/F1 this is the constant-factor killer.
- Fix: bucket chunks by coarse world grid cell (HashMap<(i64,i64,i64), Vec<idx>> or a uniform grid) and query only cells along the ray (Amanatides & Woo style).

**F4 (Medium): Hero-light visibility cache is never invalidated on atlas edits; stale shadows served up to RETRACE_PERIOD (4) frames after a chunk edit.**
- resolve_hero_light_visibility.rs:70-93. The cache is keyed on receiver+light only; `upload_atlas_rows` (rasterizer.rs:354-358) mutates the atlas and MIPs but doesn't touch hero_light_visibility. After editing geometry, a receiver in the edited chunk keeps its old visibility for up to 4 frames.
- Fix: bump an `atlas_epoch` on upload and clear (or age) cache entries when epoch changes; or fold epoch into query_key.

**F5 (Medium): Direct-mapped 65,536-entry hero cache with per-frame retrace scheduling; hash collisions cause full retrace storms in dense scenes.**
- resolve_hero_light_visibility.rs:41, 70-93. All receivers with query_key % 65536 == k share a slot; when two keys collide in the same frame, both trace (correct but doubles cost); with fine voxels many receivers map to the same slot... Actually correctness is maintained via query_key check. The should_trace stagger uses query_key+frame_index so retraces are spread. Collisions only waste work. Also the modulo-based indexing is a power-of-two-ish? 65536 = 2^16; the hash mixes, fine.
- It's more of a Low: work duplication on collision. I'll fold this into a Low or mention under hero cache.

**F6 (Medium): Per-frame chunk sort + Vec allocation: O(C log C) with a fresh Vec<&ChunkDraw> every frame.**
- render_cpu_frame.rs:80-92 (`chunks_in_draw_order`), called at render_cpu_frame.rs:30-34. Reallocates and re-sorts every frame even when camera hasn't moved.
- Fix: reuse a scratch Vec (like chunk_light_scratch), skip sort when front_to_back disabled (already), and skip sort if camera moved < epsilon or chunk set unchanged.

**F7 (Medium): select_lights_for_chunk scans ALL world fixtures per chunk per frame: O(C × L).**
- select_lights_for_chunk.rs:15-25, called render_cpu_frame.rs:60. With L in the hundreds and C in the hundreds, that's 10^4-10^5 distance tests/frame — not huge, but with the fixture scan happening even when the chunk has few splats... The fix: build a spatial hash of lights once per frame (or on edit), query per chunk O(1) amortized. Also note the `fixtures.iter().find(|light| light.id == id)` in traverse_voxel_scene/mod.rs:78 per chunk O(L).

**F8 (Medium): Defeated splat pre-check scans the full footprint twice (may_contribute + write_splat).**
- write_depth_tested_splats.rs:139-159 (request_may_contribute), 166-200 (write_splat). For a splat that passes the HZ test but fails fine depth entirely, the code scans all footprint pixels (up to (2·half_px+2)², half_px ≤ 16 → ~1024 px) once in may_contribute then again in write_splat. HZ only rejects fully-covered tiles.
- Fix: merge the two passes (probe while shading then write), or make write_splat return "first passing pixel" info... Actually simplest: in the deferred path, do the depth test pass and shading only for passing pixels — a fused loop. Low/Medium impact since HZ catches most occluded subtrees.

**F9 (Medium): `trace_svo` allocates + sorts per call — O(C log C) + O(C) allocation; fine for debug path but it's the public nearest-hit API.**
- raycast.rs:350-437, Vec::with_capacity at 363, sort at 389. Called only from tests currently. Low severity, note as API trap.

**F10 (Low/Medium): cpu_surfel::render_surfels allocates ~40 MB of buffers per frame (3 full-frame buffers + RGB).**
- cpu_surfel.rs:198-200, 282. depth 4B/px, accum 12B/px, weight 4B/px, rgb 3B/px → for 1080p ~43 MB per frame, zeroed each call. Also `draw_ellipse` (cpu_surfel.rs:364-432) scans the full ellipse bbox per surfel with no HZ/tile culling and no early-out, O(S × bbox).
- Fix: reuse buffers in the renderer struct (like DepthTestedSplatTarget), clear only touched tiles; add a coarse z-buffer (8px tiles) to skip occluded surfels' bbox scans.

**F11 (Low): `render_surfels` inner loop: per-pixel `powf(1/2.2)` gamma at 294 — fine (once per pixel). No issue.** Skip.

**F12 (Low): `build_mips` / `rebuild_mips_in_node_range` — O(N) and O(range) respectively; good. But `upload_atlas` does `texels.to_vec()` + full `build_mips` per full upload: O(N) memcpy + O(N) build — acceptable (once per upload). Note row updates are O(row) — good design.** No finding, but I can note that `validate_independent_node_block` scans the whole row update O(texels) — fine.

Wait — one important thing about `update_atlas_rows`: `rebuild_mips_in_node_range` uses done_scratch sized to the *node range* and `compute_mip` with done_base = node_range.start. But `compute_mip` recursion goes to children via child_base — the validation ensures children are within the global range (validate_independent_node_block checks child_base >= global_nodes.start && child_end <= global_nodes.end). So done_scratch indexing works. Good.

But hmm — there's a subtle bug-ish behavior: `rebuild_mips_in_node_range` fills the mips for the whole node range and recomputes each node; children within range get recomputed too (they're in the range). Total O(range × 8). Fine.

**F13 (Low): `collect_emissive_lights` — O(V) amortized with up to ~2-3 passes per cell; allocates a V-bool visited per chunk. Fine.** Maybe note `visited` allocation per generation call — one-time per chunk load, fine.

**F14 (Medium): `section_locator::describe` — regenerates region plan per region crossing; `generate_region_plan` is O(region area); fine because cached. But `describe` allocates a String per call — if called per frame it's per-frame allocation. Low.** Let me check who calls describe — HUD. Probably per frame or on change. Low.

**F15 (Low): `chunk_codec` — encode/decode O(bytes) with pre-sized capacity; decode bounds each count by remaining bytes — no OOM. The `pit_hazards` encode allocates a temp Vec per hazard (chunk_codec.rs:153) — negligible.** Good overall.

**F16: Recursion depth — `traverse_voxel_node` recursion depth ≤ SVO depth + max_virtual_depth (≤ 8+8=16... actually svo_depth up to 8 in settings? config.svo_depth() could be up to 10? MAX budget clamps svo_depth.min(20)). Depth ~20 + 8 = 28 frames — safe. `walk` in local_chunk_source — depth = SVO depth ≤ ~10 — safe. `compute_mip` — depth ≤ 20 — safe.** No finding; note in report.

**F17: `raycast` step budget — the budget is a safety valve; `ChunkMarch::Indeterminate` fails closed. But note: a *fully empty* chunk with a deep SVO costs up to B steps per query — with fine voxels B≈6000+ (test at raycast.rs:516-519 says fine > 6000). Each step is decode + skip_to_exit + pop_exited.** This is inherent to the design; flag as part of F1/F2.

**F18 (Medium): `write_depth_tested_splats::clear` — full-frame clears of rgba (4B), reciprocal (4B), coarse (2 arrays) each frame: O(W×H) memset — that's inherent and fast. Fine.**

**F19: HZ `coarse_occludes` is conservative-stale by design (recorded max never updated on nearer writes → miss culls). Fine.**

**F20 (Low): `frame_work_budget` recomputed per frame — O(1). Fine.**

## CONCURRENCY FINDINGS

The adapters layer is single-threaded by construction. Inventory:
- SoftwareRasterizer: all mutable state owned by main thread; no atomics.
- HeroLightVisibilityCache: inside rasterizer.
- LocalChunkSource: RefCell<BlockCache> — single-threaded borrow; not Send/Sync, so can't cross to workers (worker sources are constructed per worker presumably).
- ArchivedChunkSource: RefCell<ChunkArchive> + RefCell<Box<dyn ArchiveStorage>> — two simultaneous mutable borrows in fetch (lines ~127-131...). Actually let me get the exact lines. In fetch: `let mut storage = self.storage.borrow_mut(); let mut archive = self.archive.borrow_mut();` — held simultaneously; safe because no re-entrancy into self while held. But: is there re-entrancy risk if `ChunkArchive::clear`/`put` calls into storage which calls back? Storage is a trait (FileStorage/MemoryStorage) — no callback into the source. Safe.
- input.rs: reads crate-level atomics: MOUSE_SENSITIVITY_BITS (AtomicU32), INVERT_Y (AtomicBool), DOOM_CONTROLS (AtomicBool) with Relaxed ordering (input.rs:9-10, 57-60). These are set from JS via exported setters. Relaxed is sufficient for single-word loads (no torn reads on aligned atomics; no ordering constraints needed since these are independent settings). One caveat: if the f32 sensitivity is stored as bits and the writer is JS via `Atomics.store` — a u32 store is atomic anyway. Fine. Note: reading `f32::from_bits` of an AtomicU32 with Relaxed is a standard pattern; no torn read. No finding, but document.
- The `RefCell` in LocalChunkSource: `halo_grid` borrows `blocks.borrow_mut()` and then calls `generator.execute_from_plans` while holding the borrow — if the generator (or noise provider) called back into the source, it would panic (RefCell re-borrow). Let me check: `halo_grid` (lines 190-219) — `let mut blocks = self.blocks.borrow_mut(); let block = blocks.get_or_plan(...)` — the borrow is held across `generator.execute_from_plans(..., Some(block.plans()))` — wait, does the borrow end? `block` borrows from `blocks`; `blocks` is alive until end of halo_grid. `get_or_plan` returns &WorldBlock tied to the RefMut borrow. `generator.execute_from_plans` receives `Some(block.plans())` — a reference into the block — while `self.blocks` is mutably borrowed. If execute_from_plans → noise → ... no call path back into self.blocks (noise is a separate provider). But `preload_targets` also calls get_or_plan with `&mut |_, _| {}` — fine. So no re-entrancy panic. But this is a fragility worth noting: the RefCell borrow spans a long computation (generation!) — if any future code path in generation needs `self.blocks` (e.g., another chunk), it panics. Note as Low/fragility.
- Similarly `preload_targets` (local_chunk_source.rs:112-129) borrows mut and calls get_or_plan with a no-op progress callback — safe.
- Worker-related: chunk_codec is the worker↔main wire format; decode is allocation-bounded via `r.len(min_bytes)`. Workers are out of my scope (drivers), but codec correctness affects workers: any decode failure → chunk dropped → re-request. No races in codec itself.
- No data races, no deadlocks, no shared mutable statics in adapters. All memory ordering is Relaxed on independent single-word settings — correct.
- Determinism: generation is deterministic per seed; surfel/fixture collection is deterministic (explicit loops + sort + dedup). The only nondeterminism would be HashMap iteration — avoided via sort (collect_emissive_lights.rs:94). Good.

Concurrency inventory table:
1. crate::MOUSE_SENSITIVITY_BITS / INVERT_Y / DOOM_CONTROLS (lib.rs) — read in input.rs Relaxed; set by JS exports; single-word atomics, no torn reads.
2. RefCell<BlockCache> in LocalChunkSource — single-threaded; borrow held across generation call (fragility, no panic today).
3. RefCell<ChunkArchive> + RefCell<Box<dyn ArchiveStorage>> in ArchivedChunkSource — double mutable borrow in fetch; no re-entrancy; single-threaded.
4. HeroLightVisibilityCache / SoftwareRasterizer state — single-threaded main thread only.
5. chunk_codec — plain functions, no shared state; bounded allocations protect worker decode path.
6. RNG: none shared — noise provider per source; seeds deterministic. No static mut, no thread_local in adapters.

Now the Big-O table. Variables:
- C = resident chunk count (~10²)
- L = scene light count (active fixtures, ~10²)
- S = splats drawn per frame (≤ node budget; ~10⁴-10⁵)
- P = framebuffer pixels (≤ 2M at 1080p)
- B = ray-march step budget per chunk (160..32,768; fine voxels ~6,000+)
- N = atlas nodes (per chunk 10³-10⁵)
- V = voxel volume of one chunk grid
- Q = quads per chunk
- T = surfels per chunk
- R = bytes of one chunk payload

Table rows:
1. SVO traversal + splatting (traverse_voxel_scene) — O(nodes visited + Σ splat footprint px) per frame; bounded by node/pixel budget. Space O(1) working.
2. Secondary visibility ray (svo_segment_is_occluded) — O(C·B) worst per query (C slab tests + up to B steps in crossed chunks); amortized less. F1/F2 multiply: O(S·L·4·C·B) worst per frame with Full shadows + flashlight.
3. trace_svo — O(C log C + C·B) per query (sort + march).
4. build_mips — O(N) time, O(N) space per upload.
5. rebuild_mips_in_node_range — O(row nodes) per row update.
6. update_prepared_atlas_rows — O(row texels + row nodes) validated, no realloc.
7. write_splat / HZ — O(footprint px) per splat, O(P) state; coarse test O(tiles under rect).
8. request_may_contribute — O(footprint px) per splat (worst, fully occluded).
9. select_lights_for_chunk — O(L) per chunk → O(C·L) per frame.
10. chunks_in_draw_order — O(C log C) per frame + O(C) alloc.
11. resolve_hero_light_visibility — O(1) amortized per splat (cache); O(C·B) on retrace.
12. flashlight beam_contribution — O(1) irradiance + O(C·B) occlusion per splat.
13. render_surfels (cpu_surfel) — O(T·avg ellipse bbox) time, O(P) space + 40MB per-frame allocs.
14. build_surfel_cloud — O(T).
15. build_face_instances — O(Q log Q) (sort) — one-time per chunk.
16. collect_emissive_lights — O(V) amortized + O(V log V) worst-ish (sort of lights, usually tiny).
17. chunk_codec encode/decode — O(R), bounded allocations.
18. BlockCache — O(4) lookup; O(plan) on miss; bounded memory.
19. ArchivedChunkSource fetch — O(R) decode hit; O(generate) miss; archive clear O(archive) on budget.
20. crop_lateral_halo / neighbor_occlusion_mask — O(V).
21. extract_collision_boxes — O(nodes).
22. section_locator describe — O(1) cached + O(plan items) classify; O(region) on crossing.

Highest-leverage optimizations (ranked):
1. Chunk spatial index for visibility queries (kill O(C) per query; turns F1/F2 into O(crossed chunks)) — biggest multiplier since it's under S×L×4 queries.
2. Budget + cache the Full-shadow mode: per-frame ray budget like hero's SHADOW_PIXEL_BUDGET; reuse hero cache or a coarse screen-space shadow map for fixtures.
3. Cache flashlight occlusion (per-receiver cache like hero, or per-chunk beam queries).
4. Reuse per-frame scratch buffers: chunk order Vec, surfel render buffers (40MB/frame alloc).
5. Precompute light spatial index (one pass per frame, O(L)) so select_lights_for_chunk is O(1)-amortized instead of O(C×L).
6. Fuse may_contribute + write_splat double scan; optionally vectorize per-pixel loops (autovectorizable; note in report that write_splat loops are vectorization-friendly; wasm SIMD).

Also the HZ: fine.

Now, an important check — the "accidental O(n^2)" hunt: 
- `fixtures.iter().find(...)` per chunk O(L) — fine.
- `EMISSIVE_MATERIALS.contains(&material)` in atlas.rs:25 and face_instances.rs:146 — EMISSIVE_MATERIALS is presumably a small array; contains on a slice of 3 — O(3). Fine.
- `SOLID_TYPES.contains(&voxel_type)` — local_chunk_source.rs:482 — per leaf in collision walk; SOLID_MATERIALS is small (maybe ~10) — O(k) per leaf; fine, but could be a bitmask. Low.
- `material_emission_strength` per splat — small match. Fine.
- `block_cache` linear scans — capacity 4, deliberate.
- No string concat in loops, no char scanning in hot loops.

One more potential issue: `decode_node` is called per traversal node (visit) — the atlas is a Vec<u32>; decode_node does bounds checks — fine.

Also worth noting: `mips.get(visit.node_idx)` per node — Vec index — fine.

Also `visit_children` — `voxel_child_visits` returns `[Option<NodeVisit>; 8]` then `.into_iter().flatten()` — stack array, no alloc. Good.

Also — `traverse_voxel_node` — recursion. Rust default stack 8MB; depth ≤ ~30 — fine.

Also `render_cpu_frame` calls `FrameWorkBudget::for_target` per frame — cheap.

One more: `upload_atlas_rows` validation `mips.len().checked_mul(WORDS_PER_NODE)? != atlas.len()` — O(1). Fine.

Also `chunk_can_contribute` — calls `project_screen_footprint` (8 corners + 12 edges) per chunk per frame — O(C × 20 projections). Fine.

Also, worth noting the per-frame `clear()`: fills rgba + reciprocal_depth + coarse arrays: O(P). Fine.

Also, `hero cache` — the CachedVisibility is 16 bytes (u64 + f32 + bool) × 65536 = 1MB. Fine.

Now — is there anything about `request_may_contribute` being called BEFORE `resolve_hero_light_visibility` and shading — yes, in draw_leaf_splat: surface_splat_may_contribute check (mod.rs traverse: draw_leaf_splat lines ~180-200). Let me get the line numbers for draw_leaf_splat in traverse_voxel_scene/mod.rs to cite the double-scan path: the `surface_splat_may_contribute` call at mod.rs:197-204 (I saw earlier: "if self.settings.toggles.deferred_shading && !self.surface_splat_may_contribute(...)").  185:     /// Safety-cap behavior: retain the current fidelity and stop descending.
 186:     fn render_budget_fallback(
 187:         &mut self,
 188:         cam: &Camera,
 189:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 190:         lighting: ChunkLighting<'_>,
 191:         visit: NodeVisit,
 192:         projected: &ProjectedVoxelNode,
 193:         kind: NodeKind,
 194:     ) {
 195:         match kind {
 196:             NodeKind::Leaf { payload, exposure } if payload.voxel_type != VOXEL_AIR => {
 197:                 let visit = NodeVisit { exposure, ..visit };
 198:                 self.draw_leaf_splat(cam, chunks, lighting, visit, projected, payload);
 199:             }
 200:             NodeKind::Leaf { .. } => {}
 201:             NodeKind::Interior { .. } => {
 202:                 self.draw_mip_splat(cam, chunks, lighting, visit, projected)
 203:             }
 204:         }
 205:     }
 206: 
 207:     fn render_leaf(
 208:         &mut self,
 209:         cam: &Camera,
 210:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 211:         lighting: ChunkLighting<'_>,
 212:         visit: NodeVisit,
 213:         projected: &ProjectedVoxelNode,
 214:         payload: LeafPayload,
 215:     ) {
 216:         if payload.voxel_type == VOXEL_AIR {
 217:             return;
 218:         }
 219:         self.max_virtual_depth_reached = self.max_virtual_depth_reached.max(visit.virtual_depth);
 220: 
 221:         let should_subdivide = should_subdivide_leaf(&self.settings, visit, projected);
 222:         if should_subdivide {
 223:             self.visit_children(
 224:                 cam,

710:    fn clearing_frees_the_log_but_keeps_it_usable() {



    /// Counts a generation that bypassed the archive, for the hit rate.
    pub fn note_miss(&mut self) {
        self.misses += 1;
    }

    /// Bytes the archive currently occupies.
    pub fn bytes(&self) -> u64 {
        self.end
    }

    /// Drops every record and starts the log again.
    ///
    /// An append-only log cannot free one record, so a bounded archive
    /// discards all of them at once. That is crude — it throws away chunks
    /// the player is standing next to along with ones they left an hour ago —
    /// but it is correct, it is O(1), and the cost of being wrong is one
    /// regeneration. A reuse-ordered eviction would need either a rewritten
    /// log or an index of holes, which is the complexity this format exists
    /// to avoid.
    pub fn clear(&mut self, storage: &mut dyn ArchiveStorage) {
        storage.reset();
        storage.append(&self.identity.to_bytes());
        self.index.clear();
        self.end = HEADER_BYTES as u64;
    }
}

7. `build_mips` — per atlas upload: O(nodes × 8)? Actually each node computed once via memoization; each interior node visits up to 8 children. So O(N) total (each node visited once, up to 8 child lookups per interior). Fine.

But `rebuild_mips_in_node_range` — for a row update, does `compute_mip` on each node in the range; done_scratch sized to range. Each interior node recurses to children in range... but children within range. Since the row range is a complete slot, children are inside. O(range) per update. Good.

Wait — there's a subtlety: `rebuild_mips_in_node_range` fills mips[node_range].fill(default) then computes each. If the range includes internal nodes whose children are in the range, fine. But what about an internal node in the range with children NOT in the range (validated to not happen by validate_independent_node_block). OK.

But note: `mips[node_range.clone()].fill(MipNode::default())` — node_range could be huge (whole atlas row update). fill is O(range). Fine.

That's the deferred shading path: `surface_splat_may_contribute` before shading. Hmm, that's by design (skip shading cost). But for a splat that's fully occluded, the scan is 2x. Each step: decode_node (4 u32 reads), skip_to_exit with next_up per axis. This is per secondary ray. In Full shadow mode with 4 Gauss samples per rectangle light × many lights × many splats, this can explode. The budget is the safety, but worst-case Indeterminate returns true (occluded) — fail-closed.

17. `ArchivedChunkSource::fetch` — on budget exceed, `archive.clear(storage)` clears the whole archive (not per-record eviction) — a thundering-herd design but documented. Note: it holds `storage.borrow_mut()` and `archive.borrow_mut()` simultaneously — two RefCells borrowed in one scope; not a deadlock risk (no other thread). But wait — the borrows: `let mut storage = self.storage.borrow_mut(); let mut archive = self.archive.borrow_mut();` — both are RefCell borrows; if `fetch` is re-entered (e.g., via `inner.load_with_artifacts` calling back into the same source? Then later `self.archive.borrow_mut().note_miss()`. Then `let mut storage = self.storage.borrow_mut(); let mut archive = self.archive.borrow_mut();` — these two are held simultaneously; `archive.clear(storage.as_mut())` and `archive.put(storage.as_mut(), key, &encoded)` — no re-entry into self. 20. `collect_emissive_lights` — O(W×H×D) with rectangle grow. The `collect_owned_rectangle` grows x then z; each row check is O(row width). Each visited cell is checked... The inner loop `while max_z_exclusive < ... && (start_x..max_x_exclusive).all(...)` — for each z row it scans the row. Cells visited once each (visited flag). But note: `rectangle_cell_matches` checks the cell only; the visited flag prevents re-processing. So total O(volume) amortized. But wait — there's a subtle: a row check scans cells that may already be visited by a previous rectangle? They can't be — visited cells are skipped in the outer loop, and rectangle_cell_matches requires !visited. But could a cell be checked twice across different rectangles? Cell is marked visited when covered. A cell not visited but part of another component won't match (material same? if same material and exposed, it would have been covered by the earlier rectangle...). Actually the greedy algorithm marks only the chosen rectangle; a leftover cell of the same material could be re-examined by a neighboring rectangle's row scan and found matching — then it would extend into that cell and later be visited. Each cell's rectangle_cell_matches can be evaluated multiple times: once per candidate rectangle row-scan that includes it. In the worst case (e.g., checkerboard of same material where every cell starts a component that fails after 1 row), each cell could be scanned O(width) times. Hmm, worst case: alternating pattern where each start_x finds max_x_exclusive = 1, then z grows... For each start cell, the z-loop scans rows of 1 cell each: O(1). But if a start cell has a wide x-run (say W), then for each z it scans W cells; if it extends down Z rows, total W*Z cells marked visited. Adjacent start cells are visited already. So each cell is visited once when covered, but the *row-scan* may re-test cells that fail the match (e.g., a gap cell) repeatedly. Consider a grid full of emissive cells with a vertical wall of holes at column x0 spanning all z. Start cells to the left: each row scan tests the hole cell once per start cell column... Actually for a start at (x1, z1), the x-grow stops at the hole column, so max_x_exclusive = x0. Then the z-loop scans rows, testing x1..x0-1. For each subsequent row it re-tests cells x1..x0-1, all visited (marked). Wait, they're marked visited as it grows; but the row scan for z+1 re-tests them — each cell is re-tested once per z row of the same rectangle! That's O(area) per rectangle — but cells are only re-tested while they remain in the same component's row scan. Each cell gets re-tested O(depth_of_rectangle) times... no wait: the row scan for row z+1 tests x1..x0-1 (all visited already — but `rectangle_cell_matches` returns false for visited). So for each row the scan is over the full width of the rectangle. Total work = width × height of rectangle. That's O(area), fine.

Worst-case overall: the outer loop skips visited. Each rectangle's work is its area. But a *failing* row scan (when a row doesn't fully match) still scans the full width — and that row's cells get scanned once per rectangle that reaches it. If rectangles of the same material don't touch... e.g., staggered L-shapes: rectangle A (width 100) fails at row 50 (one cell mismatch), so work = 50 rows × 100 = 5000, marking 5000 cells... wait no — the rectangle marks only rows that fully matched. The failing row's cells are NOT marked. So the same failing row cells could be re-scanned by a later start cell in that row. Consider an L-shape: row 0-49 full, row 50 has 1 hole at the far right. Start at (0,0): scans 50 rows × 100 cols = 5000 tests, marks 5000... no — marks rows 0..49 × cols 0..99 = 5000 cells. Row 50's cells (except hole) unvisited. Then start at (0,50)? Actually start cells are found by scanning x from left; the first unvisited exposed cell in row 50 is (0,50). It extends x to 99 (all match), then z from 51... each row scan tests 100 cells. Fine. Total O(W×H) amortized per component, and the total across components is bounded by... each test either marks a cell or fails at a hole. Hmm, a hole cell can be re-tested many times: in row 50, the hole at col 99 is tested by every start cell in that row that extends to 99 — no, the first start cell in row 50 extends x to 99 and hits the hole → stops at 99, so max_x_exclusive = 99, hole tested once. For a *later* start in the same row (after the hole, col 100+), it tests its own region. So each cell is tested O(1) times amortized per rectangle growth... Roughly O(volume × small constant). I'd call it O(W·H·D) — linear in volume — with a small constant. The worst case is a pathological pattern where a single failing cell is re-tested by many rectangles — e.g., a vertical line of holes at the far right of every row, and rows that start components: each row's first component tests the hole once. Still O(volume). OK, fine.

26. `traverse_voxel_chunk` → `ChunkLighting::with_global_hero` — `fixtures.iter().find(|light| light.id == id)` — O(F) per chunk. Fine.

27. In `draw_leaf_splat`: `emission_strength(payload.voxel_type)` — palette lookup each splat; it's a match over materials, O(1).

28. The `mips.get(visit.node_idx)` — HashMap? No, it's Vec<MipNode>, indexed. O(1). Good.

29. Note in `resolve_node`: `decode_node` reads atlas at node_idx. Each node visited decodes. Fine.

30. `visit_children` — iterates 8 fixed entries. Good, no allocation.

31. In `write_depth_tested_splats::write_splat` — per-pixel `reciprocal_at_pixel` — mul_add, fine.

32. `surface_splat_may_contribute` — creates a SquareSplat with depth and scans all pixels until first pass. If nothing passes (fully occluded), it scans the full footprint: O(area). Then write_splat also scans full footprint. So an occluded splat does 2× area scan. But note the HZ cull in project_voxel_node would have rejected many fully-occluded nodes before this. The `coarse_rect_occludes` only rejects when fully covered tiles are nearer. Partial overlaps still get through. Medium finding.

33. `project_surface_depth` per splat — O(1).

34. `draw_visible_flare_cores` — loops dynamic lights, O(L). Fine.

35. In `flashlight::beam_contribution` — per splat when flashlight on: unoccluded_irradiance O(1); if visibility TraceScene: segment_is_occluded → svo_segment_is_occluded per splat: O(C × steps). With a flashlight on and rt_beam_occlusion, per-splat occlusion queries could dominate. The hero visibility cache amortizes hero rays but the flashlight has no cache! Flashlight rays are per-splat per frame, each a full SVO march. High-impact optimization: cache flashlight visibility per receiver (e.g., reuse the same 65k cache with a flashlight key), or trace at lower frequency.

Wait — actually the flashlight query is per splat per frame; each is a march over the beam segment only (max_t limited), but chunk slab tests are per chunk. This is the "per-frame recomputation" hot path. Medium/High finding.

36. `trace_direct_light_visibility` in Full shadow mode: per splat per light sample → svo_segment_is_occluded. There's no cache for Full mode. This is by design (correctness reference). Note budget gating: hero mode gates on pixel_writes < 1,500,000, but Full mode has no budget gate! Full mode is Maximum preset default. That's a perf risk: shadows=Full with many lights and fine voxels → catastrophic. Actually, is there any gating for Full mode? In shade_predecoded_with_frame_lighting, DirectLightVisibility::trace_scene is created unconditionally when shadows == Full. No budget check. Medium/High finding.

37. `update_atlas_rows` — note validate_row_update requires atlas.len() % WORDS_PER_ROW == 0; atlas from upload_atlas may not be row-multiple... upload_atlas_rows returns false in that case — graceful.

38. `raycast::traversal_step_budget` — fine.

39. `svo_segment_is_occluded` treats `ChunkMarch::Indeterminate` as occluded — fail-closed, fine.

40. Also `raymarch_svo_single` — `cursor.push()` — bounded stack 9, fine.

One correctness-ish subtlety in raycast: `pop_exited` uses >= vs > comparisons on float positions with next_up nudges. Documented. Not our concern for complexity audit.

41. `atlas::build_mips` recursion — recursion depth = SVO depth ≤ 20ish? depth_cells clamp svo_depth.min(20). Recursion depth ~20, fine. But note: `compute_mip` marks done before descending, and skips children `idx >= mips.len()`. If atlas has a cycle (malformed), done flag prevents infinite recursion. Good.

But there's a real algorithmic subtlety: build_mips is O(N) per upload, but `compute_mip` is called for every node index i in 0..node_count — each call recurses into children, but the `done` flag means each node is computed once total. However the *outer loop* still iterates N and the recursion revisits parent chains... `compute_mip(atlas, i, ...)` — each distinct node does O(1) work + recursion into children that are marked done. Total O(N). Good.

42. `surfel_cloud::build_surfel_cloud` — O(total surfels). `emit_quad` — per quad O(u_count × v_count). Total O(surfels). Fine.

43. `cpu_surfel::render_surfels` — per surfel: project + ellipse bounding box; per-pixel loop over the ellipse's bbox: O(surfel count × footprint area). Footprint area bounded by min_radius_px and screen. Worst case: a surfel near the camera can have a huge footprint (ext_x can be large — clamped by frustum cull with margin, but margin = radius/view_depth, so a huge disc right in front can still have a large footprint; the ellipse bbox could cover much of the screen). The depth/accum/weight are 3 full-frame vec allocations per frame: 3 × W×H × (4+12+4) bytes — for 1920×1080: 3×2M×20 = 120MB? depth f32 8MB, accum [f32;3] 24MB, weight 8MB = 40MB per frame, allocated fresh each render_surfels call (vec! macro). Per-frame allocations of ~40MB — Medium finding (reuse buffers). Also RGB output vec another 6MB. And `stats.fragments` per pixel inner loop — fine.

The ellipse pixel test per surfel: O(footprint) — no HZ culling, no per-pixel early-out; every surfel scans its whole bbox. Could early-exit or use tile culling. Also depth window blending: fragments accumulate without limit — weight can grow unbounded? weight[i] += 1.0 per coplanar fragment; number of coplanar fragments per pixel is bounded by surfel count. Not unbounded growth per se.

44. `face_instances` sort — one-time per chunk build. Fine.

45. `collect_emissive_lights`: `visited` vec allocation per chunk generation. Fine.

46. `section_locator::zone_name` — regenerates plan per region crossing: `generate_region_plan` cost is region-sized. Cached. But `describe` builds a String per call — per frame HUD. Fine.

47. `query_config::query_param` — O(query length). Called multiple times per parse. parse_generation_params is O(n) single pass. Fine.

Now concurrency: within my scope, concurrency surface is minimal:
- input.rs: reads AtomicU32 MOUSE_SENSITIVITY_BITS, AtomicBool INVERT_Y, DOOM_CONTROLS — Relaxed loads. Where are they set? In wasm_frontend/src/lib.rs (out of scope but mentioned in the task: AtomicU32/AtomicBool statics in wasm_frontend/src/lib.rs). The input adapter reads them Relaxed. The concern: MOUSE_SENSITIVITY_BITS is a bit pattern (f32) written by JS; Relaxed single-word atomic store/load is torn-free. Fine.
- LocalChunkSource/ArchivedChunkSource use RefCell — single-threaded; workers get their own sources? Generation workers presumably instantiate their own ArchivedChunkSource or LocalChunkSource. RefCell is not Send — so they're per-thread. No data races (they can't cross threads). Not an issue.
- The hero cache, SoftwareRasterizer — all single-threaded, owned by main thread.

Since the task says the concurrency surface in this repo is small, my area is mostly single-threaded. I should still inventory: RefCells (main-thread only), statics read via Relaxed (input.rs), and note the absence of synchronization issues in adapters. Also worker-related: archived_chunk_source is used in workers? The worker source file is out of my scope (worker_source.rs is in drivers). But ArchivedChunkSource with FileStorage could be used from workers. If two workers shared one ArchivedChunkSource... they don't (each worker has its own). Also the fetch path holds two RefCell borrows — if FileStorage or ChunkArchive::get internally calls into... not re-entrant. OK.

One more: `block_cache::BlockCache` — RefCell<BlockCache> in LocalChunkSource. `preload_targets` borrows mut and calls `cache.get_or_plan(...)` — fine.

Re-entrancy risk: `ArchivedChunkSource::fetch` holds `self.storage.borrow_mut()` and `self.archive.borrow_mut()` while calling `self.inner.load_with_artifacts` — no, the inner load happens BEFORE those borrows. Good. Actually wait, re-check the order:

```
let stored = { let storage = self.storage.borrow(); self.archive.borrow_mut().get(storage.as_ref(), key) };
if let Some(bytes) = stored && let Some(payload) = decode... { return payload; }
self.archive.borrow_mut().note_miss();
let payload = self.inner.load_with_artifacts(...);   // no borrows held here
let encoded = encode_chunk_payload(&payload);
let mut storage = self.storage.borrow_mut();
let mut archive = self.archive.borrow_mut();
if archive.bytes() + encoded.len() > max_bytes { archive.clear(storage.as_mut()); ... }
archive.put(storage.as_mut(), key, &encoded);
drop(archive);
payload
```

Fine. No re-entrancy.

Hmm — one potential issue: `archive.borrow_mut().note_miss()` — note_miss is called on every miss, including corrupt records. Fine.

 105: /// Result of marching one intersected chunk.
 106: ///
 107: /// `Indeterminate` is deliberately distinct from a clear segment. A corrupt
 108: /// atlas or exhausted safety budget must not become a light leak: visibility
 109: /// queries treat it as blocked, while the nearest-hit diagnostic API (which
 110: /// cannot represent an unknown material) simply continues to another chunk.
 111: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
 112: enum ChunkMarch {
 113:     Hit(RayHit),
 114:     Clear,
 115:     Indeterminate,
 116: }
 117: 
 118: const MIN_TRAVERSAL_STEPS: usize = 160;
 119: const MAX_TRAVERSAL_STEPS: usize = 32_768;
 120: 
 121: /// Conservative upper work bound for a valid octree traversal.
 122: ///
 123: /// A line crosses at most roughly three times the leaf count of a cubic grid.
 124: /// In the pessimistic sparse-tree case each crossing can descend every SVO
 125: /// level again. The absolute ceiling still protects a frame from malformed or
 126: /// hostile metadata; reaching it produces [`ChunkMarch::Indeterminate`].
 127: fn traversal_step_budget(world_size: f32, voxel_size: f32, svo_depth: u8) -> usize {
 128:     let geometry_cells =
 129:         if world_size.is_finite() && voxel_size.is_finite() && world_size > 0.0 && voxel_size > 0.0
 130:         {
 131:             (world_size / voxel_size).ceil().clamp(1.0, 1_048_576.0) as u64
 132:         } else {
 133:             1
 134:         };
 135:     let depth_cells = 1_u64
 136:         .checked_shl(u32::from(svo_depth.min(20)))
 137:         .unwrap_or(1_048_576);
 138:     let cells_per_axis = geometry_cells.max(depth_cells);
 139:     let levels = u64::from(svo_depth).saturating_add(1);
 140:     let estimated = cells_per_axis
 141:         .saturating_mul(3)
 142:         .saturating_add(1)
 143:         .saturating_mul(levels)
 144:         .saturating_add(16);
 145:     estimated.clamp(MIN_TRAVERSAL_STEPS as u64, MAX_TRAVERSAL_STEPS as u64) as usize
 146: }
 147:

 149:     fn at_root(root: usize, world_size: f32) -> Self {

 152:                 node_idx: 0,

 154:                 b_max: [0.0; 3],
 155:             }; 9],
 156:             stack_ptr: 0,
 157:             node: root,
 158:             b_min: [0.0; 3],
 159:             b_max: [world_size; 3],
 160:         }

 348: /// Traces a world-space ray through every resident chunk. Returns the first
 349: /// solid hit within `max_t`.
 350: pub fn trace_svo(
 351:     atlas: &[u32],
 352:     chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 353:     origin: [f32; 3],
 354:     direction: [f32; 3],
 355:     max_t: f32,
 356:     front_to_back: bool,
 357: ) -> Option<RayHit> {
 358:     struct ChunkHit {
 359:         idx: usize,
 360:         t_min: f32,
 361:     }
 362: 
 363:     let mut hits = Vec::with_capacity(chunks.len());
 364: 
 365:     for (i, chunk) in chunks.iter().enumerate() {
 366:         let local_ro = [
 367:             origin[0] - chunk.origin[0],
 368:             origin[1] - chunk.origin[1],
 369:             origin[2] - chunk.origin[2],
 370:         ];
 371: 
 372:         let Some(interval) = ray_box_interval(local_ro, direction, [0.0; 3], [chunk.world_size; 3])
 373:         else {
 374:             continue;
 375:         };
 376: 
 377:         if interval.entry < interval.exit && interval.exit > 0.0 && interval.entry < max_t {
 378:             hits.push(ChunkHit {
 379:                 idx: i,
 380:                 t_min: interval.entry.max(0.0),
 381:             });
 382:         }
 383:     }
 384: 
 385:     // OPTIMIZATION (rt_f2b): nearest chunk first permits the first valid hit
 386:     // to return immediately. The disabled reference path visits input order
 387:     // and explicitly retains the nearest hit, producing the same answer.
 388:     if front_to_back {
 389:         hits.sort_by(|a, b| a.t_min.total_cmp(&b.t_min));
 390:     }
 391: 
 392:     let mut closest = None;
 393:     for hit in hits {
 394:         // OPTIMIZATION (rt_f2b): sorting permits a safe early exit once the
 395:         // next chunk does not begin before the closest solid already found.
 396:         // Returning after the first intersected chunk is not equivalent:
 397:         // padded chunk AABBs overlap, so a nearer box entry can contain a
 398:         // farther solid than the following box.
 399:         if front_to_back && closest.is_some_and(|current: RayHit| current.t <= hit.t_min) {
 400:             break;
 401:         }
 402: 
 403:         let chunk = &chunks[hit.idx];
 404:         let local_ro = [
 405:             origin[0] - chunk.origin[0],
 406:             origin[1] - chunk.origin[1],
 407:             origin[2] - chunk.origin[2],
 408:         ];
 409: 
 410:         if let ChunkMarch::Hit(ray_hit) = raymarch_svo_single(
 411:             atlas,
 412:             local_ro,
 413:             direction,
 414:             chunk.root_index as usize,
 415:             hit.t_min,
 416:             max_t,
 417:             chunk.world_size,
 418:             chunk.voxel_size,
 419:             chunk.svo_depth,
 420:         ) {
 421:             // BUG FIX (cone light): the old code ignored `max_t` once inside
 422:             // a chunk, so a wall *behind* the lit surface could "occlude"
 423:             // the flashlight ray and randomly black out beam splats.
 424:             if ray_hit.t <= max_t {
 425:                 if closest.is_none_or(|current: RayHit| ray_hit.t < current.t) {
 426:                     closest = Some(ray_hit);
 427:                 }
 428:             }
 429:         }
 430:     }
 431: 
 432:     closest
 433: }
 434: 
 435: /// Returns `true` as soon as any solid voxel intersects the finite ray
 436: /// segment `[origin, origin + direction * max_t]`.
 437: ///
 438: /// Visibility queries do not need the nearest material hit. Keeping that
 439: /// contract separate from [`trace_svo`] avoids its per-query chunk-hit
 440: /// allocation and sort: each resident chunk is slab-tested in place and the
 441: /// first solid hit inside the segment terminates the scan. The SVO march is
 442: /// still resolution-bounded and absolutely capped, so malformed atlas data
 443: /// cannot hang a flashlight or fixture-shadow query.
 444: pub fn svo_segment_is_occluded(
 445:     atlas: &[u32],
 446:     chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 447:     origin: [f32; 3],
 448:     direction: [f32; 3],
 449:     max_t: f32,
 450: ) -> bool {
 451:     if !max_t.is_finite() || max_t <= 0.0 {
 452:         return false;
 453:     }
 454: 
 455:     for chunk in chunks {
 456:         let local_origin = [
 457:             origin[0] - chunk.origin[0],
 458:             origin[1] - chunk.origin[1],
 459:             origin[2] - chunk.origin[2],
 460:         ];
 461:         let Some(interval) =
 462:             ray_box_interval(local_origin, direction, [0.0; 3], [chunk.world_size; 3])
 463:         else {
 464:             continue;
 465:         };
 466:         if interval.entry >= interval.exit || interval.exit <= 0.0 || interval.entry >= max_t {
 467:             continue;
 468:         }
 469: 
 470:         let entry = interval.entry.max(0.0);
 471:         match raymarch_svo_single(
 472:             atlas,
 473:             local_origin,
 474:             direction,
 475:             chunk.root_index as usize,
 476:             entry,
 477:             max_t,
 478:             chunk.world_size,
 479:             chunk.voxel_size,
 480:             chunk.svo_depth,
 481:         ) {
 482:             ChunkMarch::Hit(hit) if hit.t <= max_t => return true,
 483:             // Unknown traversal is conservatively shadowed. Treating it as
 484:             // clear created bright leaks exactly where fine-voxel scenes
 485:             // exceeded the former fixed step budget.
 486:             ChunkMarch::Indeterminate => return true,
 487:             ChunkMarch::Hit(_) | ChunkMarch::Clear => {}
 488:         }
 489:     }
 490: 
 491:     false
 492: }

resolve_hero_light_visibility.rs: 180
   1: //! Resolve the CPU renderer's optional hero-light visibility term.
   2: //!
   3: //! Hero rays are intentionally a policy layer around the exact SVO raycast:
   4: //! they are limited to nearby, non-emissive receivers, stopped when the frame
   5: //! is already expensive, and amortized through a position-hashed cache. The
   6: //! traversal only asks for a visibility factor; it does not know how that
   7: //! factor is budgeted or cached.
   8: 
   9: use crate::application::ports::{ChunkDraw, LightSource};
  10: 
  11: use super::super::camera::dot;
  12: use super::super::raycast::{ray_box_interval, svo_segment_is_occluded};
  13: use super::super::settings::CpuShadowMode;
  14: use super::super::shading::HeroLightVisibility;
  15: use super::SoftwareRasterizer;
  16: 
  17: const CACHE_ENTRIES: usize = 65_536;
  18: const RETRACE_PERIOD_FRAMES: usize = 4;
  19: const MAX_RECEIVER_DISTANCE: f32 = 24.0;
  20: const RECEIVER_EXIT_BIAS: f32 = 0.02;
  21: const EMITTER_ENDPOINT_BIAS: f32 = 0.02;
  22: /// Above this write level, hero shadow rays are no longer affordable.
  23: const SHADOW_PIXEL_BUDGET: usize = 1_500_000;
  24: 
  25: /// Persistent visibility samples and the staggered refresh clock.
  26: pub(super) struct HeroLightVisibilityCache {
  27:     samples: Vec<CachedVisibility>,
  28:     frame_index: usize,
  29: }
  30: 
  31: #[derive(Clone, Copy, Default)]
  32: struct CachedVisibility {
  33:     query_key: u64,
  34:     visibility: f32,
  35:     valid: bool,
  36: }
  37: 
  38: impl HeroLightVisibilityCache {
  39:     pub(super) fn new() -> Self {
  40:         Self {
  41:             samples: vec![CachedVisibility::default(); CACHE_ENTRIES],
  42:             frame_index: 0,
  43:         }
  44:     }
  45: 
  46:     pub(super) fn advance_frame(&mut self) {
  47:         self.frame_index = self.frame_index.wrapping_add(1);
  48:     }
  49: }
  50: 
  51: impl SoftwareRasterizer {
  52:     /// Returns no override for the reference/no-shadow path, otherwise an
  53:     /// id-matched binary SVO visibility sample for one real scene fixture.
  54:     pub(super) fn resolve_hero_light_visibility(
  55:         &mut self,
  56:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
  57:         center: [f32; 3],
  58:         receiver_world_size: f32,
  59:         is_emissive: bool,
  60:         receiver_distance: f32,
  61:         hero: Option<&LightSource>,
  62:     ) -> Option<HeroLightVisibility> {
  63:         let hero = hero?;
  64:         let affordable =
  65:             receiver_distance <= MAX_RECEIVER_DISTANCE && self.pixel_writes < SHADOW_PIXEL_BUDGET;
  66:         if self.settings.shadows != CpuShadowMode::Hero || is_emissive || !affordable {
  67:             return None;
  68:         }
  69: 
  70:         let query_key = hash_receiver_and_light(center, receiver_world_size, hero.id);
  71:         let cache_index = query_key as usize % self.hero_light_visibility.samples.len();
  72:         let should_trace = (query_key as usize + self.hero_light_visibility.frame_index)
  73:             .is_multiple_of(RETRACE_PERIOD_FRAMES);
  74:         let cached = self.hero_light_visibility.samples[cache_index];
  75:         if !should_trace && cached.valid && cached.query_key == query_key {
  76:             return Some(HeroLightVisibility {
  77:                 light_id: hero.id,
  78:                 visibility: cached.visibility,
  79:             });
  80:         }
  81: 
  82:         let visibility = trace_hero_fixture(
  83:             &self.atlas,
  84:             chunks,
  85:             center,
  86:             receiver_world_size,
  87:             hero.position,
  88:         );
  89:         self.hero_light_visibility.samples[cache_index] = CachedVisibility {
  90:             query_key,
  91:             visibility,
  92:             valid: true,
  93:         };
  94:         Some(HeroLightVisibility {
  95:             light_id: hero.id,
  96:             visibility,
  97:         })
  98:     }
  99: }
 100: 
 101: fn hash_receiver_and_light(center: [f32; 3], world_size: f32, light_id: u64) -> u64 {
 102:     let mut key = light_id ^ 0xcbf2_9ce4_8422_2325;
 103:     for value in [center[0], center[1], center[2], world_size] {
 104:         let bits = if value == 0.0 { 0 } else { value.to_bits() };
 105:         key ^= u64::from(bits);
 106:         key = key.wrapping_mul(0x0000_0100_0000_01b3);
 107:     }
 108:     key ^= key >> 33;
 109:     key = key.wrapping_mul(0xff51_afd7_ed55_8ccd);
 110:     key ^ (key >> 33)
 111: }
 112: 
 113: fn trace_hero_fixture(
 114:     atlas: &[u32],
 115:     chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 116:     center: [f32; 3],
 117:     receiver_world_size: f32,
 118:     light_position: [f32; 3],
 119: ) -> f32 {
 120:     let to_light = [
 121:         light_position[0] - center[0],
 122:         light_position[1] - center[1],
 123:         light_position[2] - center[2],
 124:     ];
 125:     let distance_to_light = dot(to_light, to_light).sqrt();
 126:     if distance_to_light <= 0.001 {
 127:         return 1.0;
 128:     }
 129: 
 130:     let inverse_distance = 1.0 / distance_to_light;
 131:     let direction = to_light.map(|component| component * inverse_distance);
 132:     let receiver_half = receiver_world_size * 0.5;
 133:     let receiver_min = center.map(|coordinate| coordinate - receiver_half);
 134:     let receiver_max = center.map(|coordinate| coordinate + receiver_half);
 135:     let Some(receiver_interval) = ray_box_interval(center, direction, receiver_min, receiver_max)
 136:     else {
 137:         return 1.0;
 138:     };
 139:     let origin_offset = receiver_interval.exit.max(0.0) + RECEIVER_EXIT_BIAS;
 140:     let trace_length = distance_to_light - origin_offset - EMITTER_ENDPOINT_BIAS;
 141:     if trace_length <= 0.0 {
 142:         return 1.0;
 143:     }
 144:     let origin = [
 145:         center[0] + direction[0] * origin_offset,
 146:         center[1] + direction[1] * origin_offset,
 147:         center[2] + direction[2] * origin_offset,
 148:     ];
 149:     if svo_segment_is_occluded(atlas, chunks, origin, direction, trace_length) {
 150:         0.0
 151:     } else {
 152:         1.0
 153:     }
 154: }
 155:

Confirmed: the hero cache is never invalidated on `upload_atlas`/`upload_atlas_rows` (rasterizer.rs:504, 510). Stale visibility served up to RETRACE_PERIOD frames after edits. Since the cache is keyed on receiver position + light id, geometry edits don't change the key, and the sample stays valid. This is a real (if minor) correctness staleness finding.

Now let me read render_cpu_frame.rs (132 lines) fully, write_depth_tested_splats.rs key parts, and flashlight.rs.
   1: //! Orchestrate one complete CPU-splat frame.
   2: //!
   3: //! The sequence is intentionally visible in [`SoftwareRasterizer::render_cpu_frame`]:
   4: //! capture atmosphere, clear reusable buffers, build the camera, choose chunk
   5: //! order, reject non-contributing chunks, traverse, then draw flare cores.
   6: //! Individual rendering algorithms live behind those named operations.
   7: 
   8: use crate::application::ports::{ChunkDraw, FrameParams};
   9: 
  10: use super::super::camera::{Camera, dot};
  11: use super::SoftwareRasterizer;
  12: use super::frame_work_budget::FrameWorkBudget;
  13: use super::project_aabb_footprint::{
  14:     NEAR_PLANE_DEPTH, aabb_camera_depth_interval, project_screen_footprint,
  15: };
  16: use super::select_lights_for_chunk::select_lights_for_chunk;
  17: 
  18: impl SoftwareRasterizer {
  19:     pub(super) fn render_cpu_frame(&mut self, frame: &FrameParams, chunks: &[ChunkDraw]) {
  20:         self.environment = frame.environment;
  21:         self.frame_work_budget =
  22:             FrameWorkBudget::for_target(&self.settings, self.width(), self.height());
  23:         self.clear();
  24:         self.reset_frame_telemetry();
  25:         if self.atlas.is_empty() {
  26:             return;
  27:         }
  28: 
  29:         let camera = Camera::new(frame, self.width(), self.height(), &self.settings);
  30:         let ordered_chunks = chunks_in_draw_order(
  31:             chunks,
  32:             frame.camera_pos,
  33:             self.settings.toggles.front_to_back,
  34:         );
  35:         // Allowances are fixed before anything is drawn, from the camera and
  36:         // chunk list alone. Nothing below can change what another chunk or
  37:         // another band is allowed to spend.
  38:         let plan = FrameWorkPlan::build(
  39:             self.frame_work_budget,
  40:             SHADOW_PIXEL_BUDGET,
  41:             &camera,
  42:             &ordered_chunks,
  43:             self.width(),
  44:             self.height(),
  45:         );
  46:         // Reused across chunks: one world-fixture scan per chunk, no
  47:         // per-splat allocation and no truncation of overlapping lights.
  48:         let mut chunk_scene_lights = std::mem::take(&mut self.chunk_light_scratch);
  49:         let global_hero_id = frame.active_scene_lights().first().map(|light| light.id);
  50:         let frame_budget = self.frame_work_budget;
  51: 
  52:         let mut visited_nodes = 0;
  53:         let mut pixel_writes = 0;
  54:         for band_index in 0..self.bands.len() {
  55:             self.activate_band(band_index);
  56:             for (chunk_index, chunk) in ordered_chunks.iter().enumerate() {
  57:                 // Culling a whole chunk is safe because a chunk's allowance
  58:                 // is its own. Culling individual *nodes* against the band
  59:                 // would not be: it would change the node count and so move
  60:                 // where a node-starved traversal stops.
  61:                 if self.settings.toggles.distance_cull
  62:                     && !chunk_can_contribute(
  63:                         chunk,
  64:                         &camera,
  65:                         self.width(),
  66:                         self.height(),
  67:                         self.settings.max_draw_distance,
  68:                     )
  69:                 {
  70:                     continue;
  71:                 }
  72:                 self.begin_chunk(&plan, chunk_index);
  73:                 select_lights_for_chunk(
  74:                     frame.active_scene_lights(),
  75:                     chunk,
  76:                     &mut chunk_scene_lights,
  77:                 );
  78:                 self.traverse_voxel_chunk(
  79:                     &camera,
  80:                     chunks,
  81:                     chunk,
  82:                     &chunk_scene_lights,
  83:                     global_hero_id,
  84:                 );
  85:                 visited_nodes += self.visited_nodes;
  86:                 pixel_writes += self.pixel_writes;
  87:             }
  88:             self.draw_visible_flare_cores(frame, &camera);
  89:         }
  90:         self.finish_bands();
  91: 
  92:         self.frame_work_budget = frame_budget;
  93:         self.visited_nodes = visited_nodes;
  94:         self.pixel_writes = pixel_writes;
  95:         self.chunk_light_scratch = chunk_scene_lights;
  96:         self.hero_light_visibility.advance_frame();
  97:     }
  98: 
  99:     fn reset_frame_telemetry(&mut self) {
 100:         self.visited_nodes = 0;
 101:         self.budget_exhausted = false;
 102:         self.max_virtual_depth_reached = 0;
 103:         self.splat_count = 0;
 104:         self.pixel_writes = 0;
 105:     }
 106: }
 107: 
 108: /// Front-to-back ordering is an optional performance policy; fine depth
 109: /// testing makes the original chunk order the output-equivalent reference.
 110: fn chunks_in_draw_order(
 111:     chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 112:     camera_position: [f32; 3],
 113:     front_to_back: bool,
 114: ) -> Vec<&ChunkDraw> {
 115:     let mut ordered: Vec<_> = chunks.iter().collect();
 116:     if front_to_back {
 117:         ordered.sort_by(|a, b| {
 118:             chunk_distance_sq(a, camera_position).total_cmp(&chunk_distance_sq(b, camera_position))
 119:         });
 120:     }
 121:     ordered
 122: }
 123: 
 124: /// Conservative chunk-level distance and screen-bounds rejection.
 125: fn chunk_can_contribute(
 126:     chunk: &ChunkDraw,
 127:     camera: &Camera,
 128:     width: usize,
 129:     height: usize,
 130:     max_draw_distance: f32,
 131: ) -> bool {
 132:     let half_size = chunk.world_size * 0.5;
 133:     let center = [
 134:         chunk.origin[0] + half_size,
 135:         chunk.origin[1] + half_size,
 136:         chunk.origin[2] + half_size,
 137:     ];
 138:     let radius = chunk.world_size * 0.5 * 3.0_f32.sqrt();
 139:     let relative_to_camera = [
 140:         center[0] - camera.pos[0],
 141:         center[1] - camera.pos[1],
 142:         center[2] - camera.pos[2],
 143:     ];
 144: 
 145:     if dot(relative_to_camera, relative_to_camera).sqrt() - radius > max_draw_distance {
 146:         return false;
 147:     }
 148:     let depth_interval = aabb_camera_depth_interval(camera, relative_to_camera, half_size);
 149:     if depth_interval[1] <= NEAR_PLANE_DEPTH {
 150:         return false;
 151:     }
 152: 
 153:     project_screen_footprint(camera, chunk.origin, chunk.world_size, depth_interval[0])
 154:         .is_some_and(|footprint| !footprint.outside_framebuffer(width, height))
 155: }
 156: 
 157: fn chunk_distance_sq(chunk: &ChunkDraw, position: [f32; 3]) -> f32 {
 158:     let half = chunk.world_size * 0.5;
 159:     let dx = chunk.origin[0] + half - position[0];
 160:     let dz = chunk.origin[2] + half - position[2];
 161:     dx * dx + dz * dz
 162: }

Confirmed render_cpu_frame.rs:
- `chunks_in_draw_order` at 110-122: allocates a fresh `Vec<&ChunkDraw>` per frame (line 115), sorts O(C log C) per frame (117-119). No reuse. Every frame, even when camera and chunk set are unchanged. Medium/Low.
- `select_lights_for_chunk` at 73-77: per chunk per band, scans all active scene lights O(L) — but note it's inside the band loop: `for band_index in 0..self.bands.len() { for chunk in ordered_chunks }`. Wait — it's per band × chunk? Look: select_lights_for_chunk is called inside the band loop (73-77) for each chunk. So it's O(bands × C × L) per frame! How many bands? Let me check self.bands.len(). That could multiply. Let me look at bands in rasterizer.rs.
   1: //! Persistent state and public facade for CPU voxel splatting.
   2: //!
   3: //! This file deliberately contains no frame algorithm. It owns allocations
   4: //! and the stable [`RendererPort`] API; purpose-named child modules explain
   5: //! what happens to a frame:
   6: //!
   7: //! * [`render_cpu_frame`] orchestrates one complete frame;
   8: //! * [`traverse_voxel_scene`] projects and walks the SVO;
   9: //! * [`resolve_hero_light_visibility`] amortizes secondary visibility rays;
  10: //! * [`select_lights_for_chunk`] builds exact finite-support light clusters;
  11: //! * [`draw_visible_flare_cores`] draws depth-tested dynamic-light cores;
  12: //! * [`write_depth_tested_splats`] owns framebuffer and hierarchical Z data.
  13: //!
  14: //! Color remains in [`super::shading`], secondary rays in
  15: //! [`super::raycast`], and per-frame camera math in [`super::camera`].
  16:

  18: mod draw_visible_flare_cores;

  20: mod frame_work_plan;
  21: mod project_aabb_footprint;

  23: mod resolve_hero_light_visibility;
  24: mod select_lights_for_chunk;
  25: mod simd_row_ops;
  26: mod traverse_voxel_scene;
  27: mod write_depth_tested_splats;

  44: /// Per-frame counters for the HUD; tests also use them to prove termination
  45: /// and safety-cap behavior.
  46: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default)]
  47: pub struct SoftwareRasterizerTelemetry {
  48:     pub visited_nodes: usize,
  49:     pub node_soft_limit: usize,
  50:     pub node_visit_limit: usize,
  51:     pub node_budget_limited: bool,
  52:     pub budget_exhausted: bool,
  53:     pub max_virtual_depth: usize,
  54:     pub splat_count: usize,
  55:     pub pixel_writes: usize,
  56:     pub pixel_write_limit: usize,
  57:     pub pixel_budget_limited: bool,
  58: }
  59: 
  60: /// Persistent state for the CPU renderer. The struct is intentionally the
  61: /// allocation-owning facade; rendering algorithms live in focused modules.
  62: pub struct SoftwareRasterizer {
  63:     /// The band currently being rendered. Between bands its storage is
  64:     /// swapped out to `band_targets`, so every operation below sees one
  65:     /// ordinary target and needs no knowledge of the partitioning.
  66:     target: DepthTestedSplatTarget,
  67:     /// Parked storage for the bands that are not currently active. Empty
  68:     /// when the frame is rendered unpartitioned.
  69:     band_targets: Vec<DepthTestedSplatTarget>,
  70:     bands: Vec<BandLayout>,
  71:     /// Requested band count. One reproduces the unpartitioned renderer.
  72:     band_count: usize,
  73:     /// Remaining pixel writes for the current chunk, indexed by absolute
  74:     /// coarse-tile row. Sized once per frame and refilled per chunk.
  75:     zone_budgets: Vec<usize>,
  76:     /// Zones owned by the band being rendered, as `first..first + count`.
  77:     band_zones: (usize, usize),
  78:     /// SVO atlas texels: four `u32`s per node.
  79:     atlas: Vec<u32>,
  80:     mips: Vec<MipNode>,
  81:     /// Reused visitation bits for one streamed pool-slot MIP rebuild.
  82:     mip_update_scratch: Vec<bool>,
  83:     /// Reused finite-support fixture cluster; populated once per drawn chunk.
  84:     chunk_light_scratch: Vec<LightSource>,
  85: 
  86:     // Public counters are retained for compatibility; `telemetry()` is the
  87:     // stable snapshot API used by the browser driver and tests.
  88:     pub visited_nodes: usize,
  89:     pub budget_exhausted: bool,
  90:     pub max_virtual_depth_reached: usize,
  91:     pub splat_count: usize,
  92:     pub pixel_writes: usize,
  93:     frame_work_budget: FrameWorkBudget,
  94: 
  95:     pub settings: CpuRenderSettings,
  96:     hero_light_visibility: HeroLightVisibilityCache,
  97:     /// Current level atmosphere, captured once at the start of `draw`.
  98:     environment: Environment,
  99: }
 100: 
 101: impl SoftwareRasterizer {
 102:     pub fn new(width: usize, height: usize) -> Self {
 103:         let settings = CpuRenderSettings::default();
 104:         let frame_work_budget = FrameWorkBudget::for_target(&settings, width, height);
 105:         Self {
 106:             target: DepthTestedSplatTarget::new(width, height),
 107:             band_targets: Vec::new(),
 108:             bands: Vec::new(),
 109:             band_count: 1,
 110:             zone_budgets: Vec::new(),
 111:             band_zones: (0, 0),
 112:             atlas: Vec::new(),
 113:             mips: Vec::new(),
 114:             mip_update_scratch: Vec::new(),
 115:             chunk_light_scratch: Vec::new(),
 116:             visited_nodes: 0,
 117:             budget_exhausted: false,
 118:             max_virtual_depth_reached: 0,
 119:             splat_count: 0,
 120:             pixel_writes: 0,
 121:             frame_work_budget,
 122:             settings,
 123:             hero_light_visibility: HeroLightVisibilityCache::new(),
 124:             environment: Environment::default(),
 125:         }
 126:     }
 127: 
 128:     pub fn resize(&mut self, width: usize, height: usize) {
 129:         self.rebuild_bands(width.max(1), height.max(1));
 130:     }
 131: 
 132:     /// Splits subsequent frames across `band_count` horizontal bands.
 133:     ///
 134:     /// Bands are the unit of parallelism, but they are also meaningful with
 135:     /// one thread: rendering N bands sequentially must produce exactly the
 136:     /// frame that one band does, which is what the equivalence test pins.
 137:     pub fn set_band_count(&mut self, band_count: usize) {
 138:         let band_count = band_count.max(1);
 139:         if band_count == self.band_count {
 140:             return;
 141:         }
 142:         self.band_count = band_count;
 143:         self.rebuild_bands(self.width(), self.total_height());
 144:     }
 145: 
 146:     fn rebuild_bands(&mut self, width: usize, total_height: usize) {
 147:         self.bands = partition_bands(total_height, self.band_count);
 148:         self.zone_budgets = vec![0; total_height.div_ceil(COARSE_TILE)];
 149:         // The first band lives in `target`; the rest are parked.
 150:         self.target
 151:             .resize_band(width, total_height, self.bands[0].row_offset, self.bands[0].height);
 152:         self.band_targets = self.bands[1..]
 153:             .iter()
 154:             .map(|band| {
 155:                 let mut target = DepthTestedSplatTarget::new(width, total_height);
 156:                 target.resize_band(width, total_height, band.row_offset, band.height);
 157:                 target
 158:             })
 159:             .collect();
 160:     }
 161: 
 162:     pub fn width(&self) -> usize {
 163:         self.target.width()
 164:     }
 165: 
 166:     /// Height of the whole framebuffer, not of the active band.
 167:     pub fn height(&self) -> usize {
 168:         self.total_height()
 169:     }
 170: 
 171:     fn total_height(&self) -> usize {
 172:         self.bands
 173:             .last()
 174:             .map_or_else(|| self.target.height(), |band| band.row_end())
 175:     }
 176: 
 177:     /// The finished frame as top-down RGBA8 rows.
 178:     ///
 179:     /// Contiguous when unpartitioned; otherwise the bands are concatenated
 180:     /// into a reusable buffer, since they own separate allocations. Callers
 181:     /// that can present per band should use [`Self::bands_rgba`] instead and
 182:     /// skip the copy — that is what the worker present path does.
 183:     pub fn framebuffer(&self) -> &[u8] {
 184:         self.target.rgba()
 185:     }
 186: 
 187:     /// Copies every band's rows into `out` in framebuffer order.
 188:     pub fn compose_into(&self, out: &mut Vec<u8>) {
 189:         let stride = self.width() * 4;
 190:         out.clear();
 191:         out.resize(stride * self.total_height(), 0);
 192:         for (band, rgba) in self.bands_rgba() {
 193:             let start = band.row_offset * stride;
 194:             out[start..start + rgba.len()].copy_from_slice(rgba);
 195:         }
 196:     }
 197: 
 198:     /// Each band's layout paired with its own RGBA rows, in frame order.
 199:     pub(super) fn bands_rgba(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (BandLayout, &[u8])> {
 200:         std::iter::once((self.bands[0], self.target.rgba())).chain(
 201:             self.bands[1..]
 202:                 .iter()
 203:                 .zip(self.band_targets.iter())
 204:                 .map(|(band, target)| (*band, target.rgba())),
 205:         )
 206:     }
 207: 
 208:     pub fn telemetry(&self) -> SoftwareRasterizerTelemetry {
 209:         let node_budget_limited = self.budget_exhausted
 210:             && self
 211:                 .frame_work_budget
 212:                 .outside_focus_reserve(self.visited_nodes);
 213:         let pixel_budget_limited =
 214:             self.budget_exhausted && self.frame_work_budget.writes_exhausted(self.pixel_writes);
 215:         SoftwareRasterizerTelemetry {
 216:             visited_nodes: self.visited_nodes,
 217:             node_soft_limit: self.frame_work_budget.soft_node_visit_limit(),
 218:             node_visit_limit: self.frame_work_budget.node_visit_limit(),
 219:             node_budget_limited,
 220:             budget_exhausted: self.budget_exhausted,
 221:             max_virtual_depth: self.max_virtual_depth_reached,
 222:             splat_count: self.splat_count,
 223:             pixel_writes: self.pixel_writes,
 224:             pixel_write_limit: self.frame_work_budget.pixel_write_limit(),
 225:             pixel_budget_limited,
 226:         }
 227:     }
 228: 
 229:     /// Test seam and frame clear: atmosphere chooses the background; target
 230:     /// storage owns color/depth/HZ reset mechanics.
 231:     pub(super) fn clear(&mut self) {
 232:         let background_radiance = if self.environment.outdoor {
 233:             self.environment.sky_color
 234:         } else {
 235:             self.environment.fog_color
 236:         };
 237:         let background = encode_display_color(background_radiance)
 238:             .map(|channel| (channel * 255.0).round().clamp(0.0, 255.0) as u8);
 239:         self.target.clear(background);
 240:     }
 241: 
 242:     /// Frame-policy wrapper around the pixel-only target operation. It keeps
 243:     /// the exact in-loop write cap and renderer telemetry out of `target`.
 244:     fn splat(&mut self, cx: f32, cy: f32, half: f32, z: f32, rgb: [u8; 3]) {
 245:         self.write_splat_request(SquareSplat::new(cx, cy, half, z, rgb));
 246:     }
 247: 
 248:     /// Writes a shaded voxel surface using its exact ray/plane depth. The
 249:     /// emissive flag affects only exact coplanar reconstruction ties.
 250:     fn surface_splat(
 251:         &mut self,
 252:         cx: f32,
 253:         cy: f32,
 254:         half: f32,
 255:         depth: ProjectedSurfaceDepth,
 256:         rgb: [u8; 3],
 257:         emissive: bool,
 258:     ) {
 259:         let mut splat = SquareSplat::new(cx, cy, half, depth.representative_depth(), rgb)
 260:             .with_surface_depth(depth);
 261:         if emissive {
 262:             splat = splat.with_equal_depth_priority();
 263:         }
 264:         self.write_splat_request(splat);
 265:     }
 266: 
 267:     fn write_splat_request(&mut self, splat: SquareSplat) {
 268:         if self.band_zone_budgets_spent() {
 269:             self.budget_exhausted = true;
 270:             return;
 271:         }
 272:         self.splat_count += 1;
 273: 
 274:         let result = self.target.write_splat(
 275:             splat,
 276:             &mut self.zone_budgets,
 277:             self.settings.toggles.hierarchical_z,
 278:         );
 279:         self.pixel_writes += result.pixel_writes;
 280:         self.budget_exhausted |= result.budget_exhausted;
 281:     }
 282: 
 283:     /// True once every zone the active band owns has spent this chunk's
 284:     /// write allowance, so no further splat from it can change a pixel.
 285:     ///
 286:     /// This is scoped to the band's own zones rather than the whole frame on
 287:     /// purpose: a zone outside this band still having allowance says nothing
 288:     /// about whether this band can write, and consulting it would make the
 289:     /// early-out depend on how the frame happens to be partitioned.
 290:     fn band_zone_budgets_spent(&self) -> bool {
 291:         let (first, count) = self.band_zones;
 292:         self.zone_budgets
 293:             .iter()
 294:             .skip(first)
 295:             .take(count)
 296:             .all(|remaining| *remaining == 0)
 297:     }
 298: 
 299:     /// Exact pre-shading fine-depth rejection. This deliberately lives at the
 300:     /// renderer boundary: traversal can avoid radiance and visibility work,
 301:     /// while the pixel target remains the single owner of clipping/depth math.
 302:     fn surface_splat_may_contribute(
 303:         &self,
 304:         cx: f32,
 305:         cy: f32,
 306:         half: f32,
 307:         depth: ProjectedSurfaceDepth,
 308:         emissive: bool,
 309:     ) -> bool {
 310:         if self.band_zone_budgets_spent() {
 311:             return false;
 312:         }
 313:         let mut splat = SquareSplat::new(cx, cy, half, depth.representative_depth(), [0; 3])
 314:             .with_surface_depth(depth);
 315:         if emissive {
 316:             splat = splat.with_equal_depth_priority();
 317:         }
 318:         self.target.surface_splat_may_contribute(&splat)
 319:     }
 320: 
 321:     /// Shades one exact box and writes its square splat. Retained as a narrow
 322:     /// test seam; lighting itself remains in `shading`.
 323:     #[cfg(test)]
 324:     #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
 325:     pub(super) fn shade_and_splat(
 326:         &mut self,
 327:         cam: &Camera,
 328:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],

 332:         py: f32,
 333:         half_px: f32,

 335:         base_color: [f32; 3],

 339:     ) {
 340:         self.shade_and_splat_with_frame_lighting(

 343:             FrameLighting::unshadowed(&[]),
 344:             center,

 346:             px,

 348:             half_px,
 349:             dist,
 350:             base_color,
 351:             light_level,
 352:             is_emissive,
 353:             crowded_siblings,
 354:         );
 355:     }
 356: 
 357:     /// Production shading path with the exact per-chunk fixture cluster and
 358:     /// optional id-matched hero visibility context.
 359:     #[cfg(test)]
 360:     #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
 361:     pub(super) fn shade_and_splat_with_frame_lighting(
 362:         &mut self,
 363:         cam: &Camera,
 364:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 365:         lighting: FrameLighting<'_>,
 366:         center: [f32; 3],
 367:         world_size: f32,
 368:         px: f32,
 369:         py: f32,
 370:         half_px: f32,
 371:         dist: f32,
 372:         base_color: [f32; 3],
 373:         light_level: f32,
 374:         is_emissive: bool,
 375:         crowded_siblings: u32,
 376:     ) {
 377:         let linear_albedo = super::decode_srgb_albedo::decode_albedo(base_color);
 378:         self.shade_predecoded_and_splat_with_frame_lighting(
 379:             cam,

 382:             center,
 383:             world_size,
 384:             px,
 385:             py,
 386:             half_px,
 387:             ProjectedSurfaceDepth::constant(dist),
 388:             linear_albedo,
 389:             [light_level; 3],
