 391:             1,
 392:             SurfaceExposure::ALL_FACES,
 393:             crowded_siblings,
 394:         );
 395:     }
 396: 
 397:     #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
 398:     pub(super) fn shade_predecoded_and_splat_with_frame_lighting(
 399:         &mut self,
 400:         cam: &Camera,
 401:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 402:         lighting: FrameLighting<'_>,
 403:         center: [f32; 3],
 404:         world_size: f32,
 405:         px: f32,
 406:         py: f32,
 407:         half_px: f32,
 408:         surface_depth: ProjectedSurfaceDepth,
 409:         linear_albedo: [f32; 3],
 410:         baked_irradiance: [f32; 3],
 411:         emission_strength: Option<f32>,
 412:         voxel_type: u32,
 413:         exposure: SurfaceExposure,
 414:         crowded_siblings: u32,
 415:     ) {
 416:         let surface = SplatSurface {
 417:             center,
 418:             world_size,
 419:             dist: surface_depth.representative_depth(),
 420:             base_color: [0.0; 3],
 421:             baked_irradiance,
 422:             voxel_type,
 423:             is_emissive: emission_strength.is_some(),
 424:             exposure,
 425:             crowded_siblings,
 426:         };
 427:         let rgb = shading::shade_predecoded_with_frame_lighting(
 428:             cam,
 429:             &self.environment,
 430:             &self.settings,
 431:             &self.atlas,
 432:             chunks,
 433:             lighting,
 434:             &surface,
 435:             linear_albedo,
 436:             emission_strength,
 437:         );
 438:         self.surface_splat(
 439:             px,
 440:             py,
 441:             half_px,
 442:             surface_depth,
 443:             rgb,
 444:             emission_strength.is_some(),
 445:         );
 446:     }
 447: }
 448:

 450:     fn upload_atlas(&mut self, texels: &[u32]) {
 451:         self.atlas = texels.to_vec();
 452:         // MIP attributes are rebuilt on upload, never per frame.
 453:         self.mips = build_mips(&self.atlas);
 454:     }

 461:             first_row,
 462:             texels,
 463:         )
 464:     }
 465:

 467:         self.render_cpu_frame(frame, chunks);
 468:     }

Important: `select_lights_for_chunk` in render_cpu_frame.rs:73-77 is inside the band loop (54-89) AND chunk loop. So O(bands × C × L). Bands: how many? band_count default 1. Now read write_depth_tested_splats.rs (key parts: request_may_contribute, write_splat, clear, coarse_rect_occludes, record_first_coverage).
   1: //! Write square splats into a reusable, depth-tested CPU framebuffer.
   2: //!
   3: //! This module knows only pixels. Traversal decides *what* to draw and owns
   4: //! frame policy; the target clips a square splat, performs fine depth tests,
   5: //! enforces the caller's remaining write budget exactly, and maintains the
   6: //! coarse occlusion summary when requested.
   7: 
   8: use super::super::surface_geometry::ProjectedSurfaceDepth;
   9: use super::simd_row_ops::{LANES, depth_pass_mask};
  10: 
  11: /// Coarse hierarchical-Z tile edge in pixels.
  12: pub(super) const COARSE_TILE: usize = 8;
  13: 
  14: /// One depth-tested square request in target coordinates.
  15: pub(super) struct SquareSplat {
  16:     center: [f32; 2],
  17:     half: f32,
  18:     depth: ProjectedSurfaceDepth,
  19:     color: [u8; 3],
  20:     /// Overlapping square reconstruction kernels can cover the same physical
  21:     /// coplanar face. An emitter owns that exact material tie, regardless of
  22:     /// traversal order; ordinary and genuinely farther samples never do.
  23:     equal_depth_wins: bool,
  24: }
  25: 
  26: impl SquareSplat {
  27:     pub(super) fn new(cx: f32, cy: f32, half: f32, depth: f32, color: [u8; 3]) -> Self {
  28:         Self {
  29:             center: [cx, cy],
  30:             half,
  31:             depth: ProjectedSurfaceDepth::constant(depth),
  32:             color,
  33:             equal_depth_wins: false,
  34:         }
  35:     }
  36: 
  37:     pub(super) fn with_surface_depth(mut self, depth: ProjectedSurfaceDepth) -> Self {
  38:         self.depth = depth;
  39:         self
  40:     }
  41: 
  42:     pub(super) fn with_equal_depth_priority(mut self) -> Self {
  43:         self.equal_depth_wins = true;
  44:         self
  45:     }
  46: }
  47: 
  48: /// Result of one square-splat write.
  49: pub(super) struct SplatWrite {
  50:     /// Fine-depth-tested pixels whose color and depth changed.
  51:     pub(super) pixel_writes: usize,
  52:     /// A further depth-passing pixel existed after the write budget ran out.
  53:     pub(super) budget_exhausted: bool,
  54: }
  55: 
  56: /// Reusable CPU render target. Reciprocal depth is affine under perspective,
  57: /// so zero means uncovered and larger finite values are nearer. A zero coarse
  58: /// value likewise means that the tile is not yet fully covered.
  59: ///
  60: /// A target may back either the whole framebuffer or one horizontal band of
  61: /// it. Splats are always submitted in absolute framebuffer coordinates and
  62: /// clipped against the full height, so a band clips a footprint exactly as
  63: /// the unpartitioned target would; only the rows it actually stores differ.
  64: /// `row_offset == 0 && height == total_height` is the unpartitioned case and
  65: /// behaves identically to the pre-band code.
  66: pub(super) struct DepthTestedSplatTarget {
  67:     width: usize,
  68:     /// Rows this target stores.
  69:     height: usize,
  70:     /// Rows in the whole framebuffer, which is what footprints clip against.
  71:     total_height: usize,
  72:     /// First absolute framebuffer row backed by this target's buffers.
  73:     row_offset: usize,
  74:     rgba: Vec<u8>,
  75:     reciprocal_depth: Vec<f32>,
  76:     /// Smallest (farthest) reciprocal fine depth in each fully covered tile.
  77:     coarse_reciprocal_depth: Vec<f32>,
  78:     /// Number of pixels that have transitioned from uncovered to covered.
  79:     coarse_filled: Vec<usize>,
  80:     coarse_width: usize,
  81:     coarse_height: usize,
  82: }
  83: 
  84: impl DepthTestedSplatTarget {
  85:     pub(super) fn new(width: usize, height: usize) -> Self {
  86:         let mut target = Self {
  87:             width: 0,
  88:             height: 0,
  89:             total_height: 0,
  90:             row_offset: 0,
  91:             rgba: Vec::new(),
  92:             reciprocal_depth: Vec::new(),
  93:             coarse_reciprocal_depth: Vec::new(),
  94:             coarse_filled: Vec::new(),
  95:             coarse_width: 0,
  96:             coarse_height: 0,
  97:         };
  98:         target.resize(width, height);
  99:         target
 100:     }
 101: 
 102:     pub(super) fn resize(&mut self, width: usize, height: usize) {
 103:         let height = height.max(1);
 104:         self.resize_band(width, height, 0, height);
 105:     }
 106: 
 107:     /// Resizes to back `band_height` rows starting at absolute `row_offset`
 108:     /// of a `total_height`-row framebuffer.
 109:     pub(super) fn resize_band(
 110:         &mut self,
 111:         width: usize,
 112:         total_height: usize,
 113:         row_offset: usize,
 114:         band_height: usize,
 115:     ) {
 116:         self.width = width.max(1);
 117:         self.total_height = total_height.max(1);
 118:         self.row_offset = row_offset.min(self.total_height - 1);
 119:         self.height = band_height.max(1).min(self.total_height - self.row_offset);
 120:         self.rgba = vec![0; self.width * self.height * 4];
 121:         self.reciprocal_depth = vec![0.0; self.width * self.height];
 122:         self.coarse_width = self.width.div_ceil(COARSE_TILE);
 123:         self.coarse_height = self.height.div_ceil(COARSE_TILE);
 124:         self.coarse_reciprocal_depth = vec![0.0; self.coarse_width * self.coarse_height];
 125:         self.coarse_filled = vec![0; self.coarse_width * self.coarse_height];
 126:     }
 127: 
 128:     pub(super) fn width(&self) -> usize {
 129:         self.width
 130:     }
 131: 
 132:     /// Rows this target stores, which for a band is the band height.
 133:     pub(super) fn height(&self) -> usize {
 134:         self.height
 135:     }
 136: 
 137:     pub(super) fn row_offset(&self) -> usize {
 138:         self.row_offset
 139:     }
 140: 
 141:     pub(super) fn rgba(&self) -> &[u8] {
 142:         &self.rgba
 143:     }
 144: 
 145:     pub(super) fn clear(&mut self, background: [u8; 3]) {
 146:         for pixel in self.rgba.chunks_exact_mut(4) {
 147:             pixel[0] = background[0];
 148:             pixel[1] = background[1];
 149:             pixel[2] = background[2];
 150:             pixel[3] = 255;
 151:         }
 152:         self.reciprocal_depth.fill(0.0);
 153:         self.coarse_reciprocal_depth.fill(0.0);
 154:         self.coarse_filled.fill(0);
 155:     }
 156: 
 157:     /// Exact fine-depth query for an unshaded square splat.
 158:     ///
 159:     /// Traversal uses this immediately before fixture, hero, and flashlight
 160:     /// evaluation. A `false` result proves that `write_splat` would reject
 161:     /// every pixel, so skipping those radiance calculations cannot change the
 162:     /// framebuffer. A likely-visible splat returns at its first passing pixel.
 163:     #[cfg(test)]
 164:     pub(super) fn splat_may_contribute(&self, center: [f32; 2], half: f32, depth: f32) -> bool {
 165:         self.request_may_contribute(&SquareSplat::new(center[0], center[1], half, depth, [0; 3]))
 166:     }
 167: 
 168:     /// Fine-depth query for a projected physical surface. The exact same
 169:     /// per-pixel depth and tie policy is consumed by [`Self::write_splat`].
 170:     pub(super) fn surface_splat_may_contribute(&self, splat: &SquareSplat) -> bool {
 171:         self.request_may_contribute(splat)
 172:     }
 173: 
 174:     fn request_may_contribute(&self, splat: &SquareSplat) -> bool {
 175:         let [x0, x1, y0, y1] = self.clipped_square_bounds(splat.center, splat.half);
 176:         for y in y0..y1 {
 177:             let row = y * self.width;
 178:             // The plane is expressed in absolute framebuffer coordinates, so
 179:             // a band evaluates the same depth for a pixel as the whole-frame
 180:             // target would, even though it stores that pixel at a lower row.
 181:             let row_bias = splat.depth.row_bias(self.absolute_row(y) as f32 + 0.5);
 182:             let mut x = x0;
 183:             while x < x1 {
 184:                 let lanes = LANES.min(x1 - x);
 185:                 let (candidate, valid) = self.evaluate_lanes(splat, x, lanes, row_bias);
 186:                 let stored = self.load_stored_lanes(row + x, lanes);
 187:                 let mask = depth_pass_mask(stored, candidate, valid, splat.equal_depth_wins);
 188:                 if mask[..lanes].iter().any(|passed| *passed) {
 189:                     return true;
 190:                 }
 191:                 x += lanes;
 192:             }
 193:         }
 194:         false
 195:     }
 196: 
 197:     /// Evaluates up to [`LANES`] consecutive candidate depths on one scanline.
 198:     ///
 199:     /// Lanes past `lanes` are filled with a rejected sentinel so a short tail
 200:     /// batch cannot read or write outside the requested span.
 201:     fn evaluate_lanes(
 202:         &self,
 203:         splat: &SquareSplat,
 204:         x: usize,
 205:         lanes: usize,
 206:         row_bias: f32,
 207:     ) -> ([f32; LANES], [bool; LANES]) {
 208:         let mut candidate = [0.0; LANES];
 209:         let mut valid = [false; LANES];
 210:         for lane in 0..lanes {
 211:             if let Some(depth) = splat
 212:                 .depth
 213:                 .reciprocal_at_row_pixel((x + lane) as f32 + 0.5, row_bias)
 214:             {
 215:                 candidate[lane] = depth;
 216:                 valid[lane] = true;
 217:             }
 218:         }
 219:         (candidate, valid)
 220:     }
 221: 
 222:     fn load_stored_lanes(&self, idx: usize, lanes: usize) -> [f32; LANES] {
 223:         let mut stored = [0.0; LANES];
 224:         stored[..lanes].copy_from_slice(&self.reciprocal_depth[idx..idx + lanes]);
 225:         stored
 226:     }
 227: 
 228:     /// Fills a depth-tested square. `half` is its half-extent in pixels.
 229:     ///
 230:     /// The budget check lives inside the depth-passing branch: rejected
 231:     /// pixels cost no writes, while a visible pixel with no allowance left
 232:     /// is skipped and reported as exhaustion.
 233:     ///
 234:     /// `zone_budgets` is indexed by **absolute** coarse-tile row, so a zone's
 235:     /// allowance is the same value however the framebuffer is split into
 236:     /// bands. Exhausting one zone skips that zone's rows and lets the fill
 237:     /// continue into the next — the alternative, aborting the whole splat,
 238:     /// would make one zone's spending decide another zone's contents.
 239:     pub(super) fn write_splat(
 240:         &mut self,
 241:         splat: SquareSplat,
 242:         zone_budgets: &mut [usize],
 243:         maintain_coarse: bool,
 244:     ) -> SplatWrite {
 245:         // Traversal normally subdivides large footprints. Camera-plane and
 246:         // budget fallbacks remain legal here; clipping plus `write_budget`
 247:         // bounds their work without changing their requested coverage.
 248:         let [x0, x1, y0, y1] = self.clipped_square_bounds(splat.center, splat.half);
 249: 
 250:         let mut pixel_writes = 0;
 251:         let mut budget_exhausted = false;
 252:         for y in y0..y1 {
 253:             let row = y * self.width;
 254:             let absolute_row = self.absolute_row(y);
 255:             let zone = absolute_row / COARSE_TILE;
 256:             let Some(zone_budget) = zone_budgets.get(zone).copied() else {
 257:                 continue;
 258:             };
 259:             if zone_budget == 0 {
 260:                 budget_exhausted = true;
 261:                 continue;
 262:             }
 263:             // The plane is expressed in absolute framebuffer coordinates, so
 264:             // a band evaluates the same depth for a pixel as the whole-frame
 265:             // target would, even though it stores that pixel at a lower row.
 266:             let row_bias = splat.depth.row_bias(absolute_row as f32 + 0.5);
 267:             let mut x = x0;
 268:             'row: while x < x1 {
 269:                 let lanes = LANES.min(x1 - x);
 270:                 let (candidate, valid) = self.evaluate_lanes(&splat, x, lanes, row_bias);
 271:                 let stored = self.load_stored_lanes(row + x, lanes);
 272:                 let mask = depth_pass_mask(stored, candidate, valid, splat.equal_depth_wins);
 273: 
 274:                 // Everything past the verdict stays scalar and in lane order,
 275:                 // so the pixel a budget-exhausted splat tears at is exactly
 276:                 // the one it tore at before batching.
 277:                 for lane in 0..lanes {
 278:                     if !mask[lane] {
 279:                         continue;
 280:                     }
 281:                     if zone_budgets[zone] == 0 {
 282:                         budget_exhausted = true;
 283:                         break 'row;
 284:                     }
 285:                     let idx = row + x + lane;
 286:                     let was_uncovered = stored[lane] == 0.0;
 287:                     self.reciprocal_depth[idx] = candidate[lane];
 288:                     let rgba_index = idx * 4;
 289:                     self.rgba[rgba_index] = splat.color[0];
 290:                     self.rgba[rgba_index + 1] = splat.color[1];
 291:                     self.rgba[rgba_index + 2] = splat.color[2];
 292:                     pixel_writes += 1;
 293:                     zone_budgets[zone] -= 1;
 294:                     if maintain_coarse && was_uncovered {
 295:                         self.record_first_coverage(x + lane, y);
 296:                     }
 297:                 }
 298:                 x += lanes;
 299:             }
 300:         }
 301: 
 302:         SplatWrite {
 303:             pixel_writes,
 304:             budget_exhausted,
 305:         }
 306:     }
 307: 
 308:     /// Clips a splat to this target, returning `[x0, x1, y0, y1]` where the
 309:     /// y pair is **local** to the stored rows and the x pair is absolute.
 310:     ///
 311:     /// Clipping happens against the full framebuffer height first, so a band
 312:     /// selects exactly the subset of the rows the unpartitioned target would
 313:     /// have written. Translating afterwards can leave an empty range, which
 314:     /// is the cheap early-out for a splat that misses this band entirely.
 315:     fn clipped_square_bounds(&self, center: [f32; 2], half: f32) -> [usize; 4] {
 316:         // Traversal normally subdivides to the configured maximum radius,
 317:         // but camera-plane intersections and explicit budget fallbacks can
 318:         // legitimately cover more than 32 pixels.  The framebuffer is
 319:         // already a finite clip bound and the caller supplies an exact write
 320:         // budget, so silently shrinking the requested footprint would only
 321:         // create holes.  A malformed infinity conservatively covers the
 322:         // finite target instead of entering float-to-integer edge cases.
 323:         let half = if half.is_finite() {
 324:             half.max(0.0)
 325:         } else {
 326:             self.width.max(self.total_height) as f32
 327:         };
 328:         let y0_absolute = (center[1] - half).floor().max(0.0) as usize;
 329:         let y1_absolute = ((center[1] + half).ceil() as usize).min(self.total_height);
 330:         let y0 = y0_absolute.max(self.row_offset) - self.row_offset;

Confirmed write_depth_tested_splats.rs:
- `request_may_contribute` 174-195: full footprint scan, early-out on first passing pixel, but worst-case full scan for occluded splat.
- `write_splat` 239-306: full footprint scan with SIMD lanes; per-lane scalar writes.
- `clear` 145-155: O(P) per frame.
- `record_first_coverage` — need to see it (line ~340-370?).

Let me see the rest of this file (330-500) to check record_first_coverage and coarse_rect_occludes.
 331:         let y1 = y1_absolute
 332:             .min(self.row_offset + self.height)
 333:             .saturating_sub(self.row_offset);
 334:         [
 335:             (center[0] - half).floor().max(0.0) as usize,
 336:             ((center[0] + half).ceil() as usize).min(self.width),
 337:             y0.min(y1),
 338:             y1,
 339:         ]
 340:     }
 341: 
 342:     /// Absolute framebuffer row of a locally-indexed row, for the coarse
 343:     /// bookkeeping that reports in band-local space.
 344:     fn absolute_row(&self, local_row: usize) -> usize {
 345:         self.row_offset + local_row
 346:     }
 347: 
 348:     /// True only when every coarse tile under the screen rect is fully
 349:     /// covered and its farthest stored pixel is nearer than `z_near`.
 350:     #[cfg(test)]
 351:     pub(super) fn coarse_occludes(&self, px: f32, py: f32, proj_radius: f32, z_near: f32) -> bool {
 352:         self.coarse_rect_occludes([px, py], [proj_radius, proj_radius], z_near)
 353:     }
 354: 
 355:     /// Rectangular form used by the exact projected AABB footprint. Keeping
 356:     /// X/Y extents separate avoids turning a wide wall or shallow floor into
 357:     /// an unnecessarily large square HZ query.
 358:     pub(super) fn coarse_rect_occludes(
 359:         &self,
 360:         center: [f32; 2],
 361:         half_extent: [f32; 2],
 362:         z_near: f32,
 363:     ) -> bool {
 364:         let lower_tile = |value: f32, tiles: usize| {
 365:             ((value.max(0.0) as usize) / COARSE_TILE).min(tiles.saturating_sub(1))
 366:         };
 367:         // Upper bounds are exclusive after `ceil`, matching the fine
 368:         // `x0..x1`/`y0..y1` loops. Dividing that exclusive coordinate
 369:         // directly includes the next tile at every exact 8-pixel boundary.
 370:         let upper_tile = |value: f32, tiles: usize| {
 371:             ((value.ceil().max(0.0) as usize).saturating_sub(1) / COARSE_TILE)
 372:                 .min(tiles.saturating_sub(1))
 373:         };
 374:         let x0 = lower_tile((center[0] - half_extent[0]).floor(), self.coarse_width);
 375:         let x1 = upper_tile(center[0] + half_extent[0], self.coarse_width);
 376:         // Rows arrive absolute; this target only knows about its own band.
 377:         // A query reaching outside the band is answered by the band's edge
 378:         // tiles, which is conservative in the same direction as the existing
 379:         // clamp to the framebuffer edge: it can only refuse to occlude.
 380:         let row_offset = self.row_offset as f32;
 381:         let y0 = lower_tile((center[1] - half_extent[1]).floor() - row_offset, self.coarse_height);
 382:         let y1 = upper_tile(center[1] + half_extent[1] - row_offset, self.coarse_height);
 383:         if !z_near.is_finite() || z_near <= 0.0 {
 384:             return false;
 385:         }
 386:         let reciprocal_near = z_near.recip();
 387: 
 388:         for tile_y in y0..=y1 {
 389:             for tile_x in x0..=x1 {
 390:                 if reciprocal_near
 391:                     >= self.coarse_reciprocal_depth[tile_y * self.coarse_width + tile_x]
 392:                 {
 393:                     return false;
 394:                 }
 395:             }
 396:         }
 397:         true
 398:     }
 399: 
 400:     /// Records an infinity-to-finite transition. A tile is scanned exactly
 401:     /// once, when its final uncovered pixel becomes covered. Later depth
 402:     /// writes only decrease values, so the stored maximum becomes a stale
 403:     /// upper bound: conservative for rejection and free to maintain.
 404:     fn record_first_coverage(&mut self, x: usize, y: usize) {
 405:         let tile_x = x / COARSE_TILE;
 406:         let tile_y = y / COARSE_TILE;
 407:         let tile_index = tile_y * self.coarse_width + tile_x;
 408:         self.coarse_filled[tile_index] += 1;
 409:         if self.coarse_filled[tile_index] == self.tile_pixel_count(tile_x, tile_y) {
 410:             self.coarse_reciprocal_depth[tile_index] =
 411:                 self.scan_tile_farthest_reciprocal_depth(tile_x, tile_y);
 412:         }
 413:     }
 414: 
 415:     fn tile_pixel_count(&self, tile_x: usize, tile_y: usize) -> usize {
 416:         let width = ((tile_x + 1) * COARSE_TILE).min(self.width) - tile_x * COARSE_TILE;
 417:         let height = ((tile_y + 1) * COARSE_TILE).min(self.height) - tile_y * COARSE_TILE;
 418:         width * height
 419:     }
 420: 
 421:     fn scan_tile_farthest_reciprocal_depth(&self, tile_x: usize, tile_y: usize) -> f32 {
 422:         let x0 = tile_x * COARSE_TILE;
 423:         let x1 = (x0 + COARSE_TILE).min(self.width);
 424:         let y0 = tile_y * COARSE_TILE;
 425:         let y1 = (y0 + COARSE_TILE).min(self.height);
 426:         let mut farthest = f32::INFINITY;
 427:         for y in y0..y1 {
 428:             for x in x0..x1 {
 429:                 farthest = farthest.min(self.reciprocal_depth[y * self.width + x]);
 430:             }
 431:         }
 432:         farthest
 433:     }
 434: }
 435:

Confirmed. Now let me read flashlight.rs (the per-splat occlusion), trace_direct_light_visibility.rs, shade_visible_splat.rs, and evaluate_fixture_irradiance.rs.
   1: //! A finite, one-sided spotlight used by the CPU splatter.
   2: //!
   3: //! The module keeps the four independent responsibilities visible:
   4: //!
   5: //! 1. [`cone_falloff`] describes the lamp's angular intensity profile;
   6: //! 2. inverse-square attenuation describes transport through distance;
   7: //! 3. a Lambert cosine describes how the receiver faces the lamp;
   8: //! 4. [`segment_is_occluded`] optionally tests geometric visibility.
   9: //!
  10: //! Occlusion is deliberately not folded into the light equation. Disabling
  11: //! `rt_beam_occlusion` removes only the visibility query; it cannot change
  12: //! the cone, range, color, or receiver response.
  13: 
  14: use crate::application::ports::ChunkDraw;
  15: #[cfg(test)]
  16: use crate::application::rendering::compact_support_window;
  17: use crate::application::rendering::{
  18:     FLASHLIGHT_SPEC, finite_range_inverse_square_attenuation, lambertian_receiver_cosine,
  19: };
  20: 
  21: use super::camera::{Camera, dot};
  22: use super::raycast::{ray_box_interval, svo_segment_is_occluded};
  23: use super::settings::CpuFlashlightVisibility;
  24: 
  25: /// Full-strength core and soft outer edge of the angular profile.
  26: pub const INNER_DEG: f32 = FLASHLIGHT_SPEC.inner_degrees;
  27: pub const OUTER_DEG: f32 = FLASHLIGHT_SPEC.outer_degrees;
  28: /// Finite support of the lamp in world units.
  29: pub const RANGE_END: f32 = FLASHLIGHT_SPEC.range;
  30: /// Radiometric scale before inverse-square transport. It is intentionally a
  31: /// named calibration constant rather than a hidden post-lighting boost.
  32: pub const INTENSITY: f32 = FLASHLIGHT_SPEC.intensity;
  33: /// Finite source-radius clamp, preventing a singularity against the lamp.
  34: pub const MINIMUM_DISTANCE: f32 = FLASHLIGHT_SPEC.minimum_distance;
  35: /// Lamp offset from the eye: a small hand-held parallax, not an eye glow.
  36: pub const LAMP_FORWARD: f32 = FLASHLIGHT_SPEC.forward_offset;
  37: pub const LAMP_DOWN: f32 = FLASHLIGHT_SPEC.downward_offset;
  38: /// Warm-white spectral tint in linear RGB.
  39: pub const TINT: [f32; 3] = FLASHLIGHT_SPEC.tint;
  40: 
  41: /// Fixed world-space endpoint tolerances for the visibility segment.
  42: const OCCLUSION_ORIGIN_BIAS: f32 = 0.02;
  43: const OCCLUSION_RECEIVER_BIAS: f32 = 0.02;
  44: 
  45: fn smoothstep01(t: f32) -> f32 {
  46:     let t = t.clamp(0.0, 1.0);
  47:     t * t * (3.0 - 2.0 * t)
  48: }
  49: 
  50: /// Angular intensity: one in the core, zero outside the outer cone, and a
  51: /// smooth transition between them. `alignment` is `cos(theta)`.
  52: pub fn cone_falloff(alignment: f32) -> f32 {
  53:     let inner_cos = INNER_DEG.to_radians().cos();
  54:     let outer_cos = OUTER_DEG.to_radians().cos();
  55:     if !alignment.is_finite() || alignment <= outer_cos {
  56:         return 0.0;
  57:     }
  58:     smoothstep01((alignment - outer_cos) / (inner_cos - outer_cos))
  59: }
  60: 
  61: /// Compact-support window used by the inverse-square term. Exposing it
  62: /// separately makes range behavior easy to inspect in diagnostics/tests.
  63: #[cfg(test)]
  64: pub fn range_falloff(distance: f32) -> f32 {
  65:     compact_support_window(distance, RANGE_END)
  66: }
  67: 
  68: fn lamp_position(cam: &Camera) -> [f32; 3] {
  69:     [
  70:         cam.pos[0] + cam.forward[0] * LAMP_FORWARD,
  71:         cam.pos[1] + cam.forward[1] * LAMP_FORWARD - LAMP_DOWN,
  72:         cam.pos[2] + cam.forward[2] * LAMP_FORWARD,
  73:     ]
  74: }
  75: 
  76: fn normalized_direction(from: [f32; 3], to: [f32; 3]) -> Option<([f32; 3], f32)> {
  77:     let delta = [to[0] - from[0], to[1] - from[1], to[2] - from[2]];
  78:     let distance = dot(delta, delta).sqrt();
  79:     if !distance.is_finite() || distance <= 1.0e-4 {
  80:         return None;
  81:     }
  82:     Some((delta.map(|component| component / distance), distance))
  83: }
  84: 
  85: /// The splatter shades an axis-aligned cube at one representative surface
  86: /// point and normal. Full bounds stop the visibility ray at the physical
  87: /// near face; transport is evaluated at `sample_position`.
  88: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
  89: pub struct BeamReceiver {
  90:     center: [f32; 3],
  91:     sample_position: [f32; 3],
  92:     half_extent: f32,
  93:     normal: [f32; 3],
  94: }
  95: 
  96: impl BeamReceiver {
  97:     #[cfg(test)]
  98:     pub fn cube(center: [f32; 3], world_size: f32, normal: [f32; 3]) -> Self {
  99:         Self {
 100:             center,
 101:             sample_position: center,
 102:             half_extent: (world_size * 0.5).max(0.0),
 103:             normal,
 104:         }
 105:     }
 106: 
 107:     /// Uses an explicit representative point on the cube's visible surface
 108:     /// while retaining the full bounds for the occlusion endpoint.
 109:     pub fn surface_sample(
 110:         center: [f32; 3],
 111:         world_size: f32,
 112:         sample_position: [f32; 3],
 113:         normal: [f32; 3],
 114:     ) -> Self {
 115:         Self {
 116:             center,
 117:             sample_position,
 118:             half_extent: (world_size * 0.5).max(0.0),
 119:             normal,
 120:         }
 121:     }
 122: 
 123:     fn bounds(self) -> ([f32; 3], [f32; 3]) {
 124:         (
 125:             self.center.map(|value| value - self.half_extent),
 126:             self.center.map(|value| value + self.half_extent),
 127:         )
 128:     }
 129: }
 130: 
 131: /// Pure unoccluded spotlight irradiance at one representative receiver.
 132: ///
 133: /// ```text
 134: /// E = tint * I * cone(theta) * window(d, R) / max(d, d_min)^2
 135: ///          * max(dot(n, omega_to_lamp), 0)
 136: /// ```
 137: pub fn unoccluded_irradiance(cam: &Camera, receiver: BeamReceiver) -> [f32; 3] {
 138:     let lamp = lamp_position(cam);
 139:     let Some((lamp_to_receiver, distance)) = normalized_direction(lamp, receiver.sample_position)
 140:     else {
 141:         return [0.0; 3];
 142:     };
 143: 
 144:     let cone = cone_falloff(dot(lamp_to_receiver, cam.forward));
 145:     let facing = lambertian_receiver_cosine(receiver.normal, lamp_to_receiver.map(|v| -v));
 146:     let attenuation =
 147:         finite_range_inverse_square_attenuation(distance, RANGE_END, MINIMUM_DISTANCE);
 148:     let scalar = INTENSITY * cone * facing * attenuation;
 149:     TINT.map(|channel| channel * scalar)
 150: }
 151: 
 152: fn occlusion_trace_length(lamp_to_receiver_face: f32) -> Option<f32> {
 153:     let length = lamp_to_receiver_face - OCCLUSION_ORIGIN_BIAS - OCCLUSION_RECEIVER_BIAS;
 154:     (length > 0.0).then_some(length)
 155: }
 156: 
 157: /// Tests only visibility of the finite lamp-to-receiver segment.
 158: fn segment_is_occluded(
 159:     cam: &Camera,
 160:     atlas: &[u32],
 161:     chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 162:     receiver: BeamReceiver,
 163: ) -> bool {
 164:     let lamp = lamp_position(cam);
 165:     let Some((direction, _)) = normalized_direction(lamp, receiver.sample_position) else {
 166:         return false;
 167:     };
 168:     let (bounds_min, bounds_max) = receiver.bounds();
 169:     let Some(interval) = ray_box_interval(lamp, direction, bounds_min, bounds_max) else {
 170:         return false;
 171:     };
 172:     let receiver_face = interval.entry.max(0.0);
 173:     let Some(max_trace) = occlusion_trace_length(receiver_face) else {
 174:         return false;
 175:     };
 176:     let origin = [
 177:         lamp[0] + direction[0] * OCCLUSION_ORIGIN_BIAS,
 178:         lamp[1] + direction[1] * OCCLUSION_ORIGIN_BIAS,
 179:         lamp[2] + direction[2] * OCCLUSION_ORIGIN_BIAS,
 180:     ];
 181:     svo_segment_is_occluded(atlas, chunks, origin, direction, max_trace)
 182: }
 183: 
 184: /// Spotlight irradiance with optional SVO visibility. The toggle is an
 185: /// independent cost/quality switch: off is exactly the pure unoccluded term.
 186: pub fn beam_contribution(
 187:     cam: &Camera,
 188:     atlas: &[u32],
 189:     chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 190:     receiver: BeamReceiver,
 191:     visibility: CpuFlashlightVisibility,
 192: ) -> [f32; 3] {
 193:     let irradiance = unoccluded_irradiance(cam, receiver);
 194:     if irradiance == [0.0; 3] {
 195:         return irradiance;
 196:     }
 197:     if visibility == CpuFlashlightVisibility::TraceScene
 198:         && segment_is_occluded(cam, atlas, chunks, receiver)
 199:     {
 200:         [0.0; 3]
 201:     } else {
 202:         irradiance
 203:     }
 204: }

 237:         let mid = cone_falloff(((INNER_DEG + OUTER_DEG) * 0.5).to_radians().cos());

   1: //! Facade for one complete visible-splat shading evaluation.

  20: use super::{FrameLighting, SplatSurface};

  22: /// Correctness path with the complete analytic fixture contract.
  23: pub fn shade_with_frame_lighting(
  24:     cam: &Camera,
  25:     environment: &Environment,
  26:     settings: &CpuRenderSettings,
  27:     atlas: &[u32],
  28:     chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
  29:     lighting: FrameLighting<'_>,
  30:     surface: &SplatSurface,
  31: ) -> [u8; 3] {
  32:     shade_predecoded_with_frame_lighting(
  33:         cam,
  34:         environment,
  35:         settings,
  36:         atlas,
  37:         chunks,
  38:         lighting,
  39:         surface,
  40:         decode_albedo(surface.base_color),
  41:         surface
  42:             .is_emissive
  43:             .then(|| emission_strength(surface.base_color)),
  44:     )
  45: }
  46: 
  47: /// Hot production path. Atlas upload already decoded authored sRGB and MIP
  48: /// filtering already averaged in linear space, so shading consumes the
  49: /// prepared albedo without repeating transfer-function powers per splat.
  50: pub(crate) fn shade_predecoded_with_frame_lighting(
  51:     cam: &Camera,
  52:     environment: &Environment,
  53:     settings: &CpuRenderSettings,
  54:     atlas: &[u32],
  55:     chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
  56:     lighting: FrameLighting<'_>,
  57:     surface: &SplatSurface,
  58:     albedo: LinearRgb,
  59:     emission_strength: Option<f32>,
  60: ) -> [u8; 3] {
  61:     let camera_to_surface = [
  62:         surface.center[0] - cam.pos[0],
  63:         surface.center[1] - cam.pos[1],
  64:         surface.center[2] - cam.pos[2],
  65:     ];
  66:     let reconstructed_normal = representative_normal(surface, camera_to_surface);
  67:     let normal = reconstructed_normal.unwrap_or([0.0, 1.0, 0.0]);
  68:     let surface_position =
  69:         representative_surface_position(surface.center, surface.world_size, normal);
  70:     let distance = view_distance(cam, surface, surface_position);
  71: 
  72:     if let Some(emission) =
  73:         emitted_radiance_with_strength(surface, albedo, normal, emission_strength)
  74:     {
  75:         return present_radiance(emission, distance, environment);
  76:     }
  77: 
  78:     let ambient_visibility = if settings.toggles.ambient_occlusion {
  79:         ambient_visibility(surface.crowded_siblings)
  80:     } else {
  81:         1.0
  82:     };
  83:     let mut irradiance =
  84:         ambient_irradiance(environment).map(|channel| channel * ambient_visibility);
  85:     // AO approximates only open environment visibility. The cached field
  86:     // already represents transported diffuse fill and must not be darkened a
  87:     // second time.
  88:     let cached = cached_fill_irradiance(surface, settings);
  89:     let direct_visibility = if settings.shadows == CpuShadowMode::Full {
  90:         DirectLightVisibility::trace_scene(atlas, chunks, surface.world_size)
  91:     } else {
  92:         DirectLightVisibility::unoccluded()
  93:     };
  94:     let fixtures =
  95:         fixture_irradiance_with_visibility(lighting, surface_position, normal, direct_visibility);
  96:     let dynamics = dynamic_irradiance(cam, surface_position, normal);
  97:     let flashlight = if cam.flashlight && reconstructed_normal.is_some() {
  98:         flashlight::beam_contribution(
  99:             cam,
 100:             atlas,
 101:             chunks,
 102:             flashlight::BeamReceiver::surface_sample(
 103:                 surface.center,
 104:                 surface.world_size,
 105:                 surface_position,
 106:                 normal,
 107:             ),
 108:             settings.flashlight_visibility(),
 109:         )
 110:     } else {
 111:         [0.0; 3]
 112:     };
 113: 
 114:     for channel in 0..3 {
 115:         irradiance[channel] +=
 116:             cached[channel] + fixtures[channel] + dynamics[channel] + flashlight[channel];
 117:     }
 118:     let reflected = [
 119:         albedo[0] * irradiance[0] * DIFFUSE_REFLECTANCE_INV_PI,
 120:         albedo[1] * irradiance[1] * DIFFUSE_REFLECTANCE_INV_PI,
 121:         albedo[2] * irradiance[2] * DIFFUSE_REFLECTANCE_INV_PI,
 122:     ];
 123:     present_radiance(reflected, distance, environment)
 124: }
============================== cpu_splatter/shading/trace_direct_light_visibility.rs 98
   1: //! Exact finite SVO visibility segments for analytic fixture samples.
   2: //!
   3: //! The expensive CPU `Full` shadow policy calls this once for a point light
   4: //! and once for each of a rectangle's four Gauss samples. Receiver and
   5: //! emitter biases mirror the strict GPU raymarch reference so neither the
   6: //! receiver voxel nor the luminous endpoint shadows itself.
   7: 
   8: use crate::application::ports::ChunkDraw;
   9: 
  10: use super::super::camera::dot;
  11: use super::super::raycast::svo_segment_is_occluded;
  12: 
  13: #[derive(Clone, Copy)]
  14: pub(super) struct DirectLightVisibility<'a> {
  15:     atlas: &'a [u32],
  16:     chunks: &'a [ChunkDraw],
  17:     receiver_world_size: f32,
  18:     trace_scene: bool,
  19: }
  20: 
  21: impl<'a> DirectLightVisibility<'a> {
  22:     pub(super) fn unoccluded() -> Self {
  23:         Self {
  24:             atlas: &[],
  25:             chunks: &[],
  26:             receiver_world_size: 0.0,
  27:             trace_scene: false,
  28:         }
  29:     }
  30: 
  31:     pub(super) fn trace_scene(
  32:         atlas: &'a [u32],
  33:         chunks: &'a [ChunkDraw],
  34:         receiver_world_size: f32,
  35:     ) -> Self {
  36:         Self {
  37:             atlas,
  38:             chunks,
  39:             receiver_world_size,
  40:             trace_scene: true,
  41:         }
  42:     }
  43: 
  44:     pub(super) fn sample_is_visible(
  45:         self,
  46:         surface_position: [f32; 3],
  47:         surface_normal: [f32; 3],
  48:         sample_position: [f32; 3],
  49:     ) -> bool {
  50:         if !self.trace_scene {
  51:             return true;
  52:         }
  53: 
  54:         let bias = (self.receiver_world_size.abs() * 1.0e-3).max(1.0e-5);
  55:         let origin = [
  56:             surface_position[0] + surface_normal[0] * bias,
  57:             surface_position[1] + surface_normal[1] * bias,
  58:             surface_position[2] + surface_normal[2] * bias,
  59:         ];
  60:         let segment = [
  61:             sample_position[0] - origin[0],
  62:             sample_position[1] - origin[1],
  63:             sample_position[2] - origin[2],
  64:         ];
  65:         let segment_length = dot(segment, segment).sqrt();
  66:         if !segment_length.is_finite() || segment_length <= bias * 2.0 {
  67:             return true;
  68:         }
  69:         let inverse_length = 1.0 / segment_length;
  70:         let direction = segment.map(|component| component * inverse_length);
  71:         let blocker_limit = segment_length - bias * 2.0;
  72:         !svo_segment_is_occluded(self.atlas, self.chunks, origin, direction, blocker_limit)
  73:     }
  74: }
  75: 
  76: #[cfg(test)]
  77: mod tests {
  78:     use super::DirectLightVisibility;
  79:     use crate::application::ports::ChunkDraw;
  80:

  82:     fn finite_sample_segment_detects_only_blockers_before_the_endpoint() {

  84:         let chunks = [ChunkDraw {
  85:             origin: [0.0; 3],

  91:         let visibility = DirectLightVisibility::trace_scene(&atlas, &chunks, 0.5);
  92: 
  93:         assert!(!visibility.sample_is_visible([0.5, 0.5, -1.0], [0.0, 0.0, 1.0], [0.5, 0.5, 2.0],));
  94:         assert!(
  95:             visibility.sample_is_visible([0.5, 0.5, -1.0], [0.0, 0.0, 1.0], [0.5, 0.5, -0.25],)
  96:         );
  97:     }

Confirmed:
- shade_visible_splat.rs:89-93: `DirectLightVisibility::trace_scene` created per splat when shadows == Full — no budget gate.
- shade_visible_splat.rs:97-109: flashlight beam_contribution per splat.
- trace_direct_light_visibility.rs:44-73: sample_is_visible calls svo_segment_is_occluded per sample.

Now evaluate_fixture_irradiance.rs to confirm 4 Gauss samples per rectangle light.
   1: //! Analytic static-fixture irradiance. Point emitters and finite rectangular
   2: //! ceiling emitters share one sanitized radiometric boundary.
   3: 
   4: use crate::application::ports::{LightKind, LightSource};
   5: use crate::application::rendering::{LinearRgb, finite_range_inverse_square_attenuation};
   6: 
   7: use super::super::camera::dot;
   8: use super::FrameLighting;
   9: use super::trace_direct_light_visibility::DirectLightVisibility;
  10: 
  11: const GAUSS_ABSCISSA: f32 = 0.577_350_26;
  12: const MINIMUM_LIGHT_DISTANCE: f32 = 0.2;
  13: 
  14: /// Exact zero test for the four quadrature samples. This is not a lighting
  15: /// approximation: it rejects work only when every sample is outside the
  16: /// finite support, behind the one-sided emitter, or behind the receiver.
  17: fn rectangle_can_contribute(
  18:     surface_position: [f32; 3],
  19:     normal: [f32; 3],
  20:     light: &LightSource,
  21: ) -> bool {
  22:     if !light.radius.is_finite() || light.radius <= 0.0 {
  23:         return false;
  24:     }
  25:     let center_delta = [
  26:         light.position[0] - surface_position[0],
  27:         light.position[1] - surface_position[1],
  28:         light.position[2] - surface_position[2],
  29:     ];
  30:     if center_delta
  31:         .into_iter()
  32:         .chain(normal)
  33:         .any(|component| !component.is_finite())
  34:     {
  35:         return false;
  36:     }
  37: 
  38:     // Every sample shares the emitter height. A horizontal panel emits only
  39:     // downward, so receivers level with or above it get exactly zero.
  40:     if center_delta[1] <= 0.0 {
  41:         return false;
  42:     }
  43: 
  44:     let sample_extent_x = GAUSS_ABSCISSA * light.half_size[0];
  45:     let sample_extent_z = GAUSS_ABSCISSA * light.half_size[1];
  46:     let nearest_x = (center_delta[0].abs() - sample_extent_x).max(0.0);
  47:     let nearest_z = (center_delta[2].abs() - sample_extent_z).max(0.0);
  48:     let nearest_squared =
  49:         nearest_x * nearest_x + center_delta[1] * center_delta[1] + nearest_z * nearest_z;
  50:     if nearest_squared >= light.radius * light.radius {
  51:         return false;
  52:     }
  53: 
  54:     // Dot products are affine over the sample rectangle, so this bound is
  55:     // the exact maximum receiver cosine numerator among its four corners.
  56:     let maximum_receiver_dot = dot(normal, center_delta)
  57:         + normal[0].abs() * sample_extent_x
  58:         + normal[2].abs() * sample_extent_z;
  59:     maximum_receiver_dot > 0.0
  60: }
  61: 
  62: fn sanitized_light_scale(color: LinearRgb, intensity: f32) -> LinearRgb {
  63:     if !intensity.is_finite() || intensity <= 0.0 {
  64:         return [0.0; 3];
  65:     }
  66:     color.map(|channel| {
  67:         if channel.is_finite() {
  68:             channel.max(0.0) * intensity
  69:         } else {
  70:             0.0
  71:         }
  72:     })
  73: }
  74: 
  75: fn normalized(vector: [f32; 3]) -> Option<[f32; 3]> {
  76:     if vector.into_iter().any(|component| !component.is_finite()) {
  77:         return None;
  78:     }
  79:     let scale = vector
  80:         .into_iter()
  81:         .fold(0.0_f32, |largest, component| largest.max(component.abs()));
  82:     if scale == 0.0 {
  83:         return None;
  84:     }
  85:     let scaled = vector.map(|component| component / scale);
  86:     let length = dot(scaled, scaled).sqrt();
  87:     Some(scaled.map(|component| component / length))
  88: }
  89: 
  90: /// Reuses the one surface-to-light normalization for every geometric term.
  91: /// The generic rendering helpers normalize each argument independently;
  92: /// doing that twice per Gauss sample is mathematically redundant and was the
  93: /// dominant arithmetic cost in fixture-heavy CPU frames.
  94: pub(super) fn sample_geometry(
  95:     receiver_normal: [f32; 3],
  96:     surface_to_light: [f32; 3],
  97: ) -> Option<(f32, f32, f32)> {
  98:     let distance = dot(surface_to_light, surface_to_light).sqrt();
  99:     if !distance.is_finite() || distance <= 0.0 {
 100:         return None;
 101:     }
 102:     let inverse_distance = 1.0 / distance;
 103:     let direction = surface_to_light.map(|component| component * inverse_distance);
 104:     let receiver_cosine = dot(receiver_normal, direction).clamp(0.0, 1.0);
 105:     // Horizontal rectangular fixtures point down. For direction L from the
 106:     // receiver to the light, dot(-Y, direction_light_to_receiver) == L.y.
 107:     let downward_emitter_cosine = direction[1].clamp(0.0, 1.0);
 108:     Some((distance, receiver_cosine, downward_emitter_cosine))
 109: }
 110: 
 111: pub(super) fn point_light_irradiance(
 112:     surface_position: [f32; 3],
 113:     normal: [f32; 3],
 114:     light_position: [f32; 3],
 115:     color: LinearRgb,
 116:     range: f32,
 117:     intensity: f32,
 118: ) -> LinearRgb {
 119:     let delta = [
 120:         light_position[0] - surface_position[0],
 121:         light_position[1] - surface_position[1],
 122:         light_position[2] - surface_position[2],
 123:     ];
 124:     let distance_squared = dot(delta, delta);
 125:     if !range.is_finite()
 126:         || range <= 0.0
 127:         || !distance_squared.is_finite()
 128:         || distance_squared >= range * range
 129:     {
 130:         return [0.0; 3];
 131:     }
 132:     let Some(normal) = normalized(normal) else {
 133:         return [0.0; 3];
 134:     };
 135:     let Some((distance, geometry, _)) = sample_geometry(normal, delta) else {
 136:         return [0.0; 3];
 137:     };
 138:     let attenuation =
 139:         finite_range_inverse_square_attenuation(distance, range, MINIMUM_LIGHT_DISTANCE);
 140:     let scale = sanitized_light_scale(color, intensity);
 141:     scale.map(|channel| channel * geometry * attenuation)
 142: }
 143: 
 144: /// Deterministic 2x2 Gauss quadrature over a physical XZ rectangle.
 145: #[cfg(test)]
 146: pub(super) fn rectangle_light_irradiance(
 147:     surface_position: [f32; 3],
 148:     normal: [f32; 3],
 149:     light: &LightSource,
 150: ) -> LinearRgb {
 151:     rectangle_light_irradiance_with_visibility(
 152:         surface_position,
 153:         normal,
 154:         light,
 155:         DirectLightVisibility::unoccluded(),
 156:     )
 157: }
 158: 
 159: fn rectangle_light_irradiance_with_visibility(
 160:     surface_position: [f32; 3],
 161:     normal: [f32; 3],
 162:     light: &LightSource,
 163:     visibility: DirectLightVisibility<'_>,
 164: ) -> LinearRgb {
 165:     let [half_x, half_z] = light.half_size;
 166:     if !half_x.is_finite() || !half_z.is_finite() || half_x <= 0.0 || half_z <= 0.0 {
 167:         return [0.0; 3];
 168:     }
 169:     if !rectangle_can_contribute(surface_position, normal, light) {
 170:         return [0.0; 3];
 171:     }
 172:     let mut integral = 0.0;
 173:     for x in [-GAUSS_ABSCISSA, GAUSS_ABSCISSA] {
 174:         for z in [-GAUSS_ABSCISSA, GAUSS_ABSCISSA] {
 175:             let sample = [
 176:                 light.position[0] + x * half_x,
 177:                 light.position[1],
 178:                 light.position[2] + z * half_z,
 179:             ];
 180:             let surface_to_light = [
 181:                 sample[0] - surface_position[0],
 182:                 sample[1] - surface_position[1],
 183:                 sample[2] - surface_position[2],
 184:             ];
 185:             let Some((distance, receiver_cosine, emitter_cosine)) =
 186:                 sample_geometry(normal, surface_to_light)
 187:             else {
 188:                 continue;
 189:             };
 190:             if receiver_cosine <= 0.0
 191:                 || emitter_cosine <= 0.0
 192:                 || !visibility.sample_is_visible(surface_position, normal, sample)
 193:             {
 194:                 continue;
 195:             }
 196:             integral += receiver_cosine
 197:                 * emitter_cosine
 198:                 * finite_range_inverse_square_attenuation(
 199:                     distance,
 200:                     light.radius,
 201:                     MINIMUM_LIGHT_DISTANCE,
 202:                 );
 203:         }
 204:     }
 205:     let area = 4.0 * half_x * half_z;
 206:     let scale = sanitized_light_scale(light.color, light.intensity);
 207:     scale.map(|channel| channel * area * integral * 0.25)
 208: }
 209: 
 210: fn scene_light_irradiance(
 211:     light: &LightSource,
 212:     surface_position: [f32; 3],
 213:     normal: [f32; 3],
 214:     visibility: DirectLightVisibility<'_>,
 215: ) -> LinearRgb {
 216:     if !light.enabled {
 217:         return [0.0; 3];
 218:     }
 219:     match light.kind {
 220:         LightKind::Point => {
 221:             let contribution = point_light_irradiance(
 222:                 surface_position,
 223:                 normal,
 224:                 light.position,
 225:                 light.color,
 226:                 light.radius,
 227:                 light.intensity,
 228:             );
 229:             if contribution != [0.0; 3]
 230:                 && !visibility.sample_is_visible(surface_position, normal, light.position)
 231:             {
 232:                 [0.0; 3]
 233:             } else {
 234:                 contribution
 235:             }
 236:         }
 237:         LightKind::CeilingPanel | LightKind::Strip | LightKind::Emergency => {
 238:             rectangle_light_irradiance_with_visibility(surface_position, normal, light, visibility)
 239:         }
 240:     }
 241: }
 242: 
 243: #[cfg(test)]
 244: pub(super) fn fixture_irradiance(
 245:     lighting: FrameLighting<'_>,
 246:     surface_position: [f32; 3],
 247:     normal: [f32; 3],
 248: ) -> LinearRgb {
 249:     fixture_irradiance_with_visibility(
 250:         lighting,
 251:         surface_position,
 252:         normal,
 253:         DirectLightVisibility::unoccluded(),
 254:     )
 255: }
 256: 
 257: pub(super) fn fixture_irradiance_with_visibility(
 258:     lighting: FrameLighting<'_>,
 259:     surface_position: [f32; 3],
 260:     normal: [f32; 3],
 261:     direct_visibility: DirectLightVisibility<'_>,
 262: ) -> LinearRgb {
 263:     let mut sum = [0.0; 3];
 264:     for light in lighting.scene_lights {
 265:         let visibility = lighting
 266:             .hero_visibility
 267:             .filter(|hero| hero.light_id == light.id)
 268:             .map_or(1.0, |hero| hero.visibility.clamp(0.0, 1.0));
 269:         let contribution =
 270:             scene_light_irradiance(light, surface_position, normal, direct_visibility);
 271:         for channel in 0..3 {
 272:             sum[channel] += contribution[channel] * visibility;
 273:         }
 274:     }
 275:     sum
 276: }

 294:             let normal = normalized(normal).unwrap();

 297:                 let reference_receiver = lambertian_receiver_cosine(normal, delta);

 344:     #[test]
 345:     fn full_visibility_traces_each_rectangle_quadrature_endpoint() {
 346:         let mut light = panel([0.0, 4.0, 0.0], 10.0);
 347:         light.half_size = [2.0, 2.0];
 348:         let receiver = [0.0, 0.0, 0.0];

 350:         let unoccluded = rectangle_light_irradiance(receiver, normal, &light);
 351:

 353:         // A center-only visibility query would miss it and return the full
 354:         // integral; endpoint visibility removes only that sample.

 365:             normal,

 368:         );

 371:         assert!(partial[0] < unoccluded[0]);
 372:     }
 373: }

Confirmed: `fixture_irradiance_with_visibility` at 257-276 loops over `lighting.scene_lights` per splat (O(L_splat)); `scene_light_irradiance` at 210-241; rectangle 4 Gauss samples each calling `sample_is_visible` (line 192) which does svo_segment_is_occluded.    1: //! Traverse the voxel scene and turn selected SVO nodes into shaded splats.
   2: //!
   3: //! `render_node` is deliberately a short dispatcher. Projection, atlas
   4: //! decoding, budget fallback, leaf handling, interior handling, MIP drawing,
   5: //! and child ordering each live in a small helper with an explicit data type.
   6: 
   7: use crate::application::ports::{ChunkDraw, LightSource};
   8: 
   9: mod project_voxel_node;
  10: mod select_voxel_detail;
  11: mod visit_voxel_octants;

  22: /// Attributes carried by a real atlas leaf or a virtually subdivided leaf.
  23: #[derive(Clone, Copy)]
  24: pub(super) struct LeafPayload {
  25:     voxel_type: u32,
  26:     linear_albedo: [f32; 3],
  27:     baked_rgb: [u8; 3],
  28: }
  29: 
  30: /// Everything needed to visit one real or virtual node.
  31: #[derive(Clone, Copy)]
  32: pub(super) struct NodeVisit {
  33:     node_idx: usize,
  34:     min: [f32; 3],
  35:     size: f32,
  36:     crowded_siblings: u32,
  37:     virtual_leaf: Option<LeafPayload>,
  38:     virtual_depth: usize,
  39:     exposure: SurfaceExposure,
  40: }
  41: 
  42: impl NodeVisit {
  43:     fn root(chunk: &ChunkDraw) -> Self {
  44:         Self {
  45:             node_idx: chunk.root_index as usize,
  46:             min: chunk.origin,
  47:             size: chunk.world_size,
  48:             crowded_siblings: 1,
  49:             virtual_leaf: None,
  50:             virtual_depth: 0,
  51:             exposure: SurfaceExposure::ALL_FACES,
  52:         }
  53:     }
  54: }
  55: 
  56: #[derive(Clone, Copy)]
  57: enum NodeKind {
  58:     Leaf {
  59:         payload: LeafPayload,
  60:         exposure: SurfaceExposure,
  61:     },
  62:     Interior {
  63:         child_base: usize,
  64:         child_mask: u32,
  65:     },
  66: }
  67: 
  68: /// The exact finite-support cluster for one chunk plus the frame-global hero
  69: /// when (and only when) that same fixture belongs to the cluster.
  70: #[derive(Clone, Copy)]
  71: struct ChunkLighting<'a> {
  72:     fixtures: &'a [LightSource],
  73:     hero: Option<&'a LightSource>,
  74: }
  75: 
  76: impl<'a> ChunkLighting<'a> {
  77:     fn with_global_hero(fixtures: &'a [LightSource], global_hero_id: Option<u64>) -> Self {
  78:         let hero = global_hero_id.and_then(|id| fixtures.iter().find(|light| light.id == id));
  79:         Self { fixtures, hero }
  80:     }
  81: }
  82: 
  83: impl SoftwareRasterizer {
  84:     /// Traversal entry point kept narrow for the frame orchestrator.
  85:     pub(super) fn traverse_voxel_chunk(
  86:         &mut self,
  87:         cam: &Camera,
  88:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
  89:         chunk: &ChunkDraw,
  90:         scene_lights: &[LightSource],
  91:         global_hero_id: Option<u64>,
  92:     ) {
  93:         let lighting = ChunkLighting::with_global_hero(scene_lights, global_hero_id);
  94:         self.traverse_voxel_node(cam, chunks, lighting, NodeVisit::root(chunk));
  95:     }
  96: 
  97:     /// Projects, resolves, and dispatches one node. All branch-specific work
  98:     /// is delegated so the recursive control flow remains readable.
  99:     fn traverse_voxel_node(
 100:         &mut self,
 101:         cam: &Camera,
 102:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 103:         lighting: ChunkLighting<'_>,
 104:         visit: NodeVisit,
 105:     ) {
 106:         // These are absolute frame limits, checked before projection, atlas
 107:         // decode, lighting, or a secondary visibility ray. In particular,
 108:         // reaching the pixel-write cap must not leave an invisible tail of
 109:         // expensive shading work in the recursion.
 110:         if self.frame_work_budget.writes_exhausted(self.pixel_writes)
 111:             || self.frame_work_budget.nodes_exhausted(self.visited_nodes)
 112:         {
 113:             self.budget_exhausted = true;
 114:             return;
 115:         }
 116: 
 117:         let Some(projected) = project_voxel_node(self, cam, visit) else {
 118:             return;
 119:         };
 120: 
 121:         // Preserve telemetry semantics: a projected malformed atlas node is
 122:         // counted before its failed decode, just as the original traversal.
 123:         self.visited_nodes += 1;
 124:         let Some(kind) = self.resolve_node(visit) else {
 125:             return;
 126:         };
 127: 
 128:         let at_hard_cap = self.frame_work_budget.nodes_exhausted(self.visited_nodes);
 129:         let outside_focus_reserve = self
 130:             .frame_work_budget
 131:             .outside_focus_reserve(self.visited_nodes)
 132:             && !node_is_in_flashlight_focus(cam, &projected);
 133:         if at_hard_cap || outside_focus_reserve {
 134:             self.budget_exhausted = true;
 135:             self.render_budget_fallback(cam, chunks, lighting, visit, &projected, kind);
 136:             return;
 137:         }
 138: 
 139:         match kind {
 140:             NodeKind::Leaf { payload, exposure } => {
 141:                 let visit = NodeVisit { exposure, ..visit };
 142:                 self.render_leaf(cam, chunks, lighting, visit, &projected, payload)
 143:             }
 144:             NodeKind::Interior {
 145:                 child_base,
 146:                 child_mask,
 147:             } => {
 148:                 self.render_interior(
 149:                     cam, chunks, lighting, visit, &projected, child_base, child_mask,
 150:                 );
 151:             }
 152:         }
 153:     }
 154: 
 155:     fn resolve_node(&self, visit: NodeVisit) -> Option<NodeKind> {
 156:         if let Some(payload) = visit.virtual_leaf {
 157:             return Some(NodeKind::Leaf {
 158:                 payload,
 159:                 exposure: visit.exposure,
 160:             });
 161:         }
 162: 
 163:         let node = decode_node(&self.atlas, visit.node_idx)?;
 164:         if node.is_leaf {
 165:             let linear_albedo = self
 166:                 .mips
 167:                 .get(visit.node_idx)
 168:                 .map_or([0.0; 3], |mip| mip.linear_albedo);
 169:             Some(NodeKind::Leaf {
 170:                 payload: LeafPayload {
 171:                     voxel_type: node.voxel_type,
 172:                     linear_albedo,
 173:                     baked_rgb: node.baked_rgb,
 174:                 },
 175:                 exposure: node.exposure,
 176:             })
 177:         } else {
 178:             Some(NodeKind::Interior {
 179:                 child_base: node.child_base,
 180:                 child_mask: node.child_mask,
 181:             })
 182:         }
 183:     }
 184: 
 185:     /// Safety-cap behavior: retain the current fidelity and stop descending.
 186:     fn render_budget_fallback(
 187:         &mut self,
 188:         cam: &Camera,
 189:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 190:         lighting: ChunkLighting<'_>,
 191:         visit: NodeVisit,
 192:         projected: &ProjectedVoxelNode,
 193:         kind: NodeKind,
 194:     ) {
 195:         match kind {
 196:             NodeKind::Leaf { payload, exposure } if payload.voxel_type != VOXEL_AIR => {
 197:                 let visit = NodeVisit { exposure, ..visit };
 198:                 self.draw_leaf_splat(cam, chunks, lighting, visit, projected, payload);
 199:             }
 200:             NodeKind::Leaf { .. } => {}
 201:             NodeKind::Interior { .. } => {
 202:                 self.draw_mip_splat(cam, chunks, lighting, visit, projected)
 203:             }
 204:         }
 205:     }
 206: 
 207:     fn render_leaf(
 208:         &mut self,
 209:         cam: &Camera,
 210:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 211:         lighting: ChunkLighting<'_>,
 212:         visit: NodeVisit,
 213:         projected: &ProjectedVoxelNode,
 214:         payload: LeafPayload,
 215:     ) {
 216:         if payload.voxel_type == VOXEL_AIR {
 217:             return;
 218:         }
 219:         self.max_virtual_depth_reached = self.max_virtual_depth_reached.max(visit.virtual_depth);
 220: 
 221:         let should_subdivide = should_subdivide_leaf(&self.settings, visit, projected);
 222:         if should_subdivide {
 223:             self.visit_children(
 224:                 cam,
 225:                 chunks,
 226:                 lighting,
 227:                 visit,
 228:                 VoxelChildren::Virtual(payload),
 229:                 visit.crowded_siblings,
 230:             );
 231:         } else {
 232:             self.draw_leaf_splat(cam, chunks, lighting, visit, projected, payload);
 233:         }
 234:     }
 235: 
 236:     fn draw_leaf_splat(
 237:         &mut self,
 238:         cam: &Camera,
 239:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 240:         lighting: ChunkLighting<'_>,
 241:         visit: NodeVisit,
 242:         projected: &ProjectedVoxelNode,
 243:         payload: LeafPayload,
 244:     ) {
 245:         let half_px = projected.footprint.enclosing_radius().max(0.85);
 246:         let emission_strength = emission_strength(payload.voxel_type);
 247:         let emissive = emission_strength.is_some();
 248:         let Some(surface_depth) = project_surface_depth(
 249:             cam,
 250:             projected.center,
 251:             visit.size,
 252:             payload.voxel_type,
 253:             emissive,
 254:             visit.exposure,
 255:         ) else {
 256:             return;
 257:         };
 258:         if self.settings.toggles.deferred_shading
 259:             && !self.surface_splat_may_contribute(
 260:                 projected.footprint.center[0],

 304:         child_mask: u32,

 306:         // OPTIMIZATION (LOD): a subtree fitting in roughly a pixel becomes
 307:         // one prefiltered MIP splat instead of a full descendant walk.
 308:         let contains_emissive = self
 309:             .mips
 310:             .get(visit.node_idx)

 313:             self.draw_mip_splat(cam, chunks, lighting, visit, projected);
 314:             return;

 316: 
 317:         self.visit_children(
 318:             cam,
 319:             chunks,
 320:             lighting,
 321:             visit,
 322:             VoxelChildren::Atlas {
 323:                 base: child_base,
 324:                 mask: child_mask,
 325:             },
 326:             child_mask.count_ones(),
 327:         );
 328:     }

 330:     fn draw_mip_splat(
 331:         &mut self,
 332:         cam: &Camera,
 333:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 334:         lighting: ChunkLighting<'_>,
 335:         visit: NodeVisit,
 336:         projected: &ProjectedVoxelNode,
 337:     ) {
 338:         let mip = self.mips.get(visit.node_idx).copied().unwrap_or_default();
 339:         if mip.occupancy < self.settings.min_mip_occupancy {
 340:             return;
 341:         }
 342:         let half_px = projected.footprint.enclosing_radius().max(0.85);
 343:         let emissive = mip.emissive_coverage > 0.0;
 344:         let mip_voxel_type = if emissive {
 345:             vackrooms::domain::entities::voxel_grid::VOXEL_LIGHT as u32
 346:         } else {
 347:             mip.dominant_voxel_type
 348:         };
 349:         let Some(surface_depth) = project_surface_depth(
 350:             cam,
 351:             projected.center,
 352:             visit.size,
 353:             mip_voxel_type,
 354:             emissive,
 355:             mip.exposed_area,
 356:         ) else {
 357:             return;
 358:         };
 359:         if self.settings.toggles.deferred_shading
 360:             && !self.surface_splat_may_contribute(
 361:                 projected.footprint.center[0],
 362:                 projected.footprint.center[1],
 363:                 half_px,
 364:                 surface_depth,
 365:                 emissive,
 366:             )
 367:         {
 368:             return;
 369:         }
 370:         let hero_visibility = self.resolve_hero_light_visibility(
 371:             chunks,
 372:             projected.center,
 373:             visit.size,
 374:             emissive,
 375:             surface_depth.representative_depth(),
 376:             lighting.hero,
 377:         );
 378:         self.shade_predecoded_and_splat_with_frame_lighting(
 379:             cam,
 380:             chunks,
 381:             frame_lighting(lighting.fixtures, hero_visibility),
 382:             projected.center,
 383:             visit.size,
 384:             projected.footprint.center[0],
 385:             projected.footprint.center[1],
 386:             half_px,
 387:             surface_depth,
 388:             if emissive {
 389:                 mip.emitted_radiance_density
 390:             } else {
 391:                 mip.linear_albedo
 392:             },
 393:             mip.baked_rgb,
 394:             emissive.then_some(1.0),
 395:             mip_voxel_type,
 396:             mip.exposed_area,
 397:             visit.crowded_siblings,
 398:         );
 399:     }
 400: 
 401:     /// Visits occupied octants either near-to-far or in reference index
 402:     /// order. Virtual subdivision reuses one leaf payload at the next depth.
 403:     fn visit_children(
 404:         &mut self,
 405:         cam: &Camera,
 406:         chunks: &[ChunkDraw],
 407:         lighting: ChunkLighting<'_>,
 408:         parent: NodeVisit,
 409:         children: VoxelChildren,
 410:         crowding: u32,
 411:     ) {
 412:         for child in voxel_child_visits(
 413:             parent,
 414:             children,
 415:             crowding,
 416:             cam.pos,
 417:             self.settings.toggles.front_to_back,
 418:         )
 419:         .into_iter()
 420:         .flatten()
 421:         {
 422:             self.traverse_voxel_node(cam, chunks, lighting, child);
 423:         }
 424:     }
 425: }

 427: fn frame_lighting(
 428:     scene_lights: &[LightSource],
 429:     hero_visibility: Option<HeroLightVisibility>,
 430: ) -> FrameLighting<'_> {
 431:     match hero_visibility {
 432:         Some(hero) => {
 433:             FrameLighting::with_hero_visibility(scene_lights, hero.light_id, hero.visibility)
 434:         }
 435:         None => FrameLighting::unshadowed(scene_lights),
 436:     }
 437: }

============================== cpu_splatter/rasterizer/traverse_voxel_scene/project_voxel_node.rs 83
   1: //! Project one voxel node and conservatively reject invisible bounds.
   2: 
   3: use super::super::super::camera::{Camera, dot};
   4: use super::super::SoftwareRasterizer;
   5: use super::super::project_aabb_footprint::{
   6:     NEAR_PLANE_DEPTH, ScreenFootprint, aabb_camera_depth_interval, project_screen_footprint,
   7: };
   8: use super::NodeVisit;
   9: 
  10: /// Screen-space facts shared by traversal, LOD selection, and splat drawing.
  11: pub(super) struct ProjectedVoxelNode {
  12:     pub(super) center: [f32; 3],
  13:     pub(super) relative_to_camera: [f32; 3],
  14:     pub(super) camera_depth: f32,
  15:     /// One eight-corner projection shared by culling, LOD, splitting, HZ,
  16:     /// deferred-depth rejection, and the final splat write.
  17:     pub(super) footprint: ScreenFootprint,
  18: }
  19: 
  20: /// Returns `None` when the node cannot contribute a visible pixel.
  21: pub(super) fn project_voxel_node(
  22:     renderer: &SoftwareRasterizer,
  23:     camera: &Camera,
  24:     visit: NodeVisit,
  25: ) -> Option<ProjectedVoxelNode> {
  26:     let half_size = visit.size * 0.5;
  27:     let center = [
  28:         visit.min[0] + half_size,
  29:         visit.min[1] + half_size,
  30:         visit.min[2] + half_size,
  31:     ];
  32:     let relative_to_camera = [
  33:         center[0] - camera.pos[0],
  34:         center[1] - camera.pos[1],
  35:         center[2] - camera.pos[2],
  36:     ];
  37:     let camera_depth = dot(relative_to_camera, camera.forward);
  38:     let depth_interval = aabb_camera_depth_interval(camera, relative_to_camera, half_size);
  39:     if depth_interval[1] <= NEAR_PLANE_DEPTH {
  40:         return None;
  41:     }
  42: 
  43:     let footprint = project_screen_footprint(camera, visit.min, visit.size, depth_interval[0])?;
  44: 
  45:     if footprint.outside_framebuffer(renderer.target.width(), renderer.target.height()) {
  46:         return None;
  47:     }
  48: 
  49:     // OPTIMIZATION (hierarchical z): every overlapping tile must be fully
  50:     // covered and its farthest pixel nearer than the node's nearest bound.
  51:     if renderer.settings.toggles.hierarchical_z
  52:         && renderer.target.coarse_rect_occludes(
  53:             footprint.center,
  54:             footprint.half_extent,
  55:             footprint.nearest_depth.max(0.0),
  56:         )
  57:     {
  58:         return None;
  59:     }
  60: 
  61:     Some(ProjectedVoxelNode {
  62:         center,
  63:         relative_to_camera,
  64:         camera_depth,
  65:         footprint,
  66:     })
  67: }
  68: 
  69: /// True when a projected node may use the bounded flashlight-focus reserve
  70: /// between the soft degradation threshold and the absolute hard cap.
  71: pub(super) fn node_is_in_flashlight_focus(camera: &Camera, projected: &ProjectedVoxelNode) -> bool {
  72:     if !camera.flashlight {
  73:         return false;
  74:     }
  75:     let distance = projected.camera_depth.max(0.001);
  76:     if distance >= 15.0 {
  77:         return false;
  78:     }
  79:     let direction = projected
  80:         .relative_to_camera
  81:         .map(|component| component / distance);
  82:     dot(direction, camera.forward) > 0.88
  83: }
============================== cpu_splatter/rasterizer/traverse_voxel_scene/select_voxel_detail.rs 72
   1: //! Select real-node MIP detail and virtual leaf subdivision.
   2: //!
   3: //! These predicates are the complete LOD policy. Traversal performs the
   4: //! chosen action but does not hide additional quality thresholds.
   5: 
   6: use super::super::super::settings::CpuRenderSettings;
   7: use super::NodeVisit;
   8: use super::project_voxel_node::ProjectedVoxelNode;
   9: 
  10: pub(super) fn should_subdivide_leaf(
  11:     settings: &CpuRenderSettings,
  12:     visit: NodeVisit,
  13:     projected: &ProjectedVoxelNode,
  14: ) -> bool {
  15:     projected.footprint.enclosing_radius() > settings.max_splat_radius_px
  16:         && visit.size > settings.min_split_size
  17:         && visit.virtual_depth < settings.max_virtual_depth as usize
  18: }
  19: 
  20: /// OPTIMIZATION (MIP LOD): a subtree fitting in roughly one pixel becomes
  21: /// one prefiltered splat instead of a full descendant walk.
  22: pub(super) fn should_draw_mip_splat(
  23:     settings: &CpuRenderSettings,
  24:     projected: &ProjectedVoxelNode,
  25: ) -> bool {
  26:     settings.toggles.mip_lod && projected.footprint.enclosing_radius() < settings.lod_cutoff_px
  27: }
