  64: 
  65:         assert!(should_subdivide_leaf(&settings, visit(1.0, 0), &projected));
  66:         assert!(!should_subdivide_leaf(
  67:             &settings,
  68:             visit(1.0, settings.max_virtual_depth as usize),
  69:             &projected,
  70:         ));
  71:     }
  72: }
============================== cpu_splatter/rasterizer/traverse_voxel_scene/visit_voxel_octants.rs 84
   1: //! Construct occupied child visits in the configured traversal order.
   2: 
   3: use super::super::super::surface_geometry::exposure_for_octant;
   4: use super::{LeafPayload, NodeVisit};
   5: 
   6: #[derive(Clone, Copy)]
   7: pub(super) enum VoxelChildren {
   8:     Atlas { base: usize, mask: u32 },
   9:     Virtual(LeafPayload),
  10: }
  11: 
  12: impl VoxelChildren {
  13:     fn occupied_mask(self) -> u32 {
  14:         match self {
  15:             Self::Atlas { mask, .. } => mask,
  16:             Self::Virtual(_) => 0xFF,
  17:         }
  18:     }
  19: 
  20:     fn atlas_or_virtual_child(
  21:         self,
  22:         octant: u32,
  23:         parent_virtual_depth: usize,
  24:     ) -> (usize, Option<LeafPayload>, usize) {
  25:         match self {
  26:             Self::Atlas { base, .. } => (base + octant as usize, None, 0),
  27:             Self::Virtual(payload) => (usize::MAX, Some(payload), parent_virtual_depth + 1),
  28:         }
  29:     }
  30: }
  31: 
  32: /// Occupied child visits in near-to-far order, or plain atlas order when the
  33: /// optimization is disabled. Empty slots let the caller use one stack-free,
  34: /// fixed-size result without allocation.
  35: pub(super) fn voxel_child_visits(
  36:     parent: NodeVisit,
  37:     children: VoxelChildren,
  38:     crowded_siblings: u32,
  39:     camera_position: [f32; 3],
  40:     front_to_back: bool,
  41: ) -> [Option<NodeVisit>; 8] {
  42:     let half_size = parent.size * 0.5;
  43:     let parent_center = [
  44:         parent.min[0] + half_size,
  45:         parent.min[1] + half_size,
  46:         parent.min[2] + half_size,
  47:     ];
  48:     let camera_octant = (camera_position[0] >= parent_center[0]) as u32
  49:         | (((camera_position[1] >= parent_center[1]) as u32) << 1)
  50:         | (((camera_position[2] >= parent_center[2]) as u32) << 2);
  51: 
  52:     // XOR distances grouped by popcount: camera octant first, opposite last.
  53:     const NEAR_TO_FAR_XOR: [u32; 8] = [0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5, 6, 7];
  54:     let occupied_mask = children.occupied_mask();
  55:     let mut visits = [None; 8];
  56:     for (index, relative_octant) in NEAR_TO_FAR_XOR.into_iter().enumerate() {
  57:         let octant = if front_to_back {
  58:             camera_octant ^ relative_octant
  59:         } else {
  60:             index as u32
  61:         };
  62:         if occupied_mask & (1 << octant) == 0 {
  63:             continue;
  64:         }
  65: 
  66:         let min = [
  67:             parent.min[0] + (octant & 1) as f32 * half_size,
  68:             parent.min[1] + ((octant >> 1) & 1) as f32 * half_size,
  69:             parent.min[2] + ((octant >> 2) & 1) as f32 * half_size,
  70:         ];
  71:         let (node_idx, virtual_leaf, virtual_depth) =
  72:             children.atlas_or_virtual_child(octant, parent.virtual_depth);
  73:         visits[index] = Some(NodeVisit {
  74:             node_idx,
  75:             min,
  76:             size: half_size,
  77:             crowded_siblings,
  78:             virtual_leaf,
  79:             virtual_depth,
  80:             exposure: exposure_for_octant(parent.exposure, occupied_mask, octant),
  81:         });
  82:     }
  83:     visits
  84: }
============================== cpu_splatter/rasterizer/select_lights_for_chunk.rs 153
   1: //! Build an exact finite-support fixture cluster for one voxel chunk.
   2: //!
   3: //! Traversal shades many splats from the same chunk. Scanning every world
   4: //! fixture for every splat would dominate the CPU path, so the frame scans
   5: //! the fixture list once per chunk. A light is rejected only when the minimum
   6: //! distance between its emitter geometry and the chunk AABB exceeds its
   7: //! authored finite range; this changes no radiometric result.
   8: 
   9: use crate::application::ports::{ChunkDraw, LightKind, LightSource};
  10: 
  11: pub(super) fn select_lights_for_chunk(
  12:     scene_lights: &[LightSource],
  13:     chunk: &ChunkDraw,
  14:     selected: &mut Vec<LightSource>,
  15: ) {
  16:     selected.clear();
  17:     selected.extend(
  18:         scene_lights
  19:             .iter()
  20:             .copied()
  21:             .filter(|light| light_can_reach_chunk(light, chunk)),
  22:     );
  23: }
  24: 
  25: fn light_can_reach_chunk(light: &LightSource, chunk: &ChunkDraw) -> bool {
  26:     if !light.enabled
  27:         || !light.radius.is_finite()
  28:         || light.radius <= 0.0
  29:         || light
  30:             .position
  31:             .iter()
  32:             .any(|coordinate| !coordinate.is_finite())
  33:     {
  34:         return false;
  35:     }
  36: 
  37:     let emitter_half_size = match light.kind {
  38:         LightKind::Point => [0.0, 0.0],
  39:         LightKind::CeilingPanel | LightKind::Strip | LightKind::Emergency => {
  40:             if light
  41:                 .half_size
  42:                 .iter()
  43:                 .any(|half| !half.is_finite() || *half <= 0.0)
  44:             {
  45:                 return false;
  46:             }
  47:             light.half_size
  48:         }
  49:     };
  50: 
  51:     let chunk_max = chunk.origin.map(|minimum| minimum + chunk.world_size);
  52:     let dx = interval_distance(
  53:         light.position[0] - emitter_half_size[0],
  54:         light.position[0] + emitter_half_size[0],
  55:         chunk.origin[0],
  56:         chunk_max[0],
  57:     );
  58:     let dy = point_interval_distance(light.position[1], chunk.origin[1], chunk_max[1]);
  59:     let dz = interval_distance(
  60:         light.position[2] - emitter_half_size[1],
  61:         light.position[2] + emitter_half_size[1],
  62:         chunk.origin[2],
  63:         chunk_max[2],
  64:     );
  65:     dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz <= light.radius * light.radius
  66: }
  67: 
  68: fn interval_distance(a_min: f32, a_max: f32, b_min: f32, b_max: f32) -> f32 {
  69:     if a_max < b_min {
  70:         b_min - a_max
  71:     } else if b_max < a_min {
  72:         a_min - b_max
  73:     } else {
  74:         0.0
  75:     }
  76: }
  77: 
  78: fn point_interval_distance(point: f32, minimum: f32, maximum: f32) -> f32 {
  79:     if point < minimum {
  80:         minimum - point
  81:     } else if point > maximum {
  82:         point - maximum
  83:     } else {
  84:         0.0
  85:     }
  86: }
  87: 
  88: #[cfg(test)]
  89: mod tests {
  90:     use crate::application::ports::{ChunkDraw, LightKind, LightSource};
  91: 
  92:     use super::select_lights_for_chunk;
  93: 
  94:     fn chunk() -> ChunkDraw {
  95:         ChunkDraw {
  96:             origin: [0.0; 3],
  97:             root_index: 0,
  98:             world_size: 4.0,
  99:             voxel_size: 0.5,
 100:             svo_depth: 3,

All confirmed. Now read atlas.rs (build_mips, decode_node), update_atlas_rows.rs, cpu_surfel.rs (render_surfels), and input.rs.
   1: //! SVO atlas decoding and MIP filtering.
   2: //!
   3: //! The atlas is the GPU-serialized octree shared with every other renderer:
   4: //! 4 `u32` per node (see the core's `OctreeGpuSerializer`). This module owns
   5: //! the two pieces of *data preparation* the splatter needs:
   6: //!
   7: //! * [`decode_node`] — one node's texels → leaf payload or child pointer.
   8: //! * [`build_mips`] — a per-node pyramid of filtered attributes (average
   9: //!   linear albedo, max light, occupancy), computed once per atlas upload so the
  10: //!   LOD path can draw a whole subtree as a single splat.
  11: 
  12: use std::ops::Range;
  13: 
  14: use super::decode_srgb_albedo::decode_packed_albedo;
  15: use super::surface_geometry::SurfaceExposure;
  16: 
  17: /// Voxel type constants shared with the core serializer.
  18: pub const VOXEL_AIR: u32 = 0;
  19: 
  20: /// Single CPU-renderer predicate mirroring the domain's emissive palette.
  21: /// Keeping this beside atlas decoding prevents traversal and shading from
  22: /// growing renderer-specific material lists.
  23: pub fn is_emissive(voxel_type: u32) -> bool {
  24:     u8::try_from(voxel_type).ok().is_some_and(|material| {
  25:         vackrooms::domain::entities::voxel_grid::EMISSIVE_MATERIALS.contains(&material)
  26:     })
  27: }
  28: 
  29: /// Authored emitted-radiance scale for one emissive material.
  30: ///
  31: /// This is material data, not a color heuristic. Keeping it next to the
  32: /// palette predicate lets traversal carry only the compact material id while
  33: /// the shading boundary receives the exact scalar used by fixture extraction.
  34: pub fn emission_strength(voxel_type: u32) -> Option<f32> {
  35:     vackrooms::domain::entities::voxel_grid::material_emission_strength(
  36:         u8::try_from(voxel_type).ok()?,
  37:     )
  38: }
  39: 
  40: /// A decoded atlas node. Exactly one of the two shapes is meaningful:
  41: /// leaves carry material/color/light, while interiors carry
  42: /// `child_base`/`child_mask`.
  43: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
  44: pub struct DecodedNode {
  45:     pub is_leaf: bool,
  46:     pub child_base: usize,
  47:     pub child_mask: u32,
  48:     pub voxel_type: u32,
  49:     /// Leaf albedo in the renderer's 0..255 reference range. Production CPU
  50:     /// traversal uses the upload-prepared linear value instead; the encoded
  51:     /// form remains part of the shared decoder for diagnostic renderers.
  52:     pub color: [f32; 3],
  53:     /// Leaf scalar diffuse-fill level, 0..15.
  54:     pub light: f32,
  55:     /// Quantized colored diffuse-fill channels, 0..15.
  56:     pub baked_rgb: [u8; 3],
  57:     /// Exact authored faces open to air, decoded from the serialized neighbor
  58:     /// occlusion mask.
  59:     pub exposure: SurfaceExposure,
  60: }
  61: 
  62: /// Reads node `node_idx` out of the texel stream. Returns `None` when the
  63: /// index runs past the atlas (defensive: a truncated upload must degrade to
  64: /// missing geometry, not out-of-bounds panics).
  65: pub fn decode_node(atlas: &[u32], node_idx: usize) -> Option<DecodedNode> {
  66:     let t = node_idx * 4;
  67:     if t + 3 >= atlas.len() {
  68:         return None;
  69:     }
  70:     let is_leaf = atlas[t] == 1;
  71:     if is_leaf {
  72:         let packed_color = atlas[t + 2];
  73:         let packed_lighting = atlas[t + 3];
  74:         Some(DecodedNode {
  75:             is_leaf: true,
  76:             child_base: 0,
  77:             child_mask: 0,
  78:             voxel_type: atlas[t + 1],
  79:             color: [
  80:                 ((packed_color >> 16) & 0xFF) as f32,
  81:                 ((packed_color >> 8) & 0xFF) as f32,
  82:                 (packed_color & 0xFF) as f32,
  83:             ],
  84:             light: (packed_lighting & 0xFF) as f32,
  85:             baked_rgb: decode_baked_rgb(packed_lighting),
  86:             exposure: decode_surface_exposure(packed_lighting),
  87:         })
  88:     } else {
  89:         Some(DecodedNode {
  90:             is_leaf: false,
  91:             child_base: atlas[t + 1] as usize,
  92:             child_mask: atlas[t + 2],
  93:             voxel_type: 0,
  94:             color: [0.0; 3],
  95:             light: 0.0,
  96:             baked_rgb: [0; 3],
  97:             exposure: SurfaceExposure::NONE,
  98:         })
  99:     }
 100: }
 101: 
 102: /// Per-node MIP-filtered attributes, rebuilt on every atlas upload.
 103: #[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, Default, PartialEq)]
 104: pub struct MipNode {
 105:     /// Occupancy-weighted linear albedo of the subtree. Decoding before the
 106:     /// average is both physically correct and removes sRGB powers per splat.
 107:     pub linear_albedo: [f32; 3],
 108:     /// Occupancy-weighted colored diffuse fill (0..15). A maximum would smear
 109:     /// one bright receiver across the entire collapsed subtree.
 110:     pub baked_rgb: [f32; 3],
 111:     /// Fraction of the subtree volume that is solid, 0..1.
 112:     pub occupancy: f32,
 113:     /// Material owning the largest occupied fraction of this subtree.
 114:     pub dominant_voxel_type: u32,
 115:     /// Occupied fraction represented by `dominant_voxel_type`.
 116:     dominant_coverage: f32,
 117:     /// Fraction of the subtree occupied by emissive material.
 118:     pub emissive_coverage: f32,
 119:     /// Emitted radiance averaged over the whole subtree volume. Ordinary LOD
 120:     /// refuses to collapse emissive subtrees; this keeps a bounded hard-cap
 121:     /// fallback visible instead of silently deleting a panel.
 122:     pub emitted_radiance_density: [f32; 3],
 123:     /// Directional exposed surface area prepared from exact leaf masks.
 124:     pub exposed_area: SurfaceExposure,
 125: }
 126: 
 127: fn decode_baked_rgb(packed_lighting: u32) -> [u8; 3] {
 128:     [
 129:         ((packed_lighting >> 16) & 0xF) as u8,
 130:         ((packed_lighting >> 20) & 0xF) as u8,
 131:         ((packed_lighting >> 24) & 0xF) as u8,
 132:     ]
 133: }
 134: 
 135: fn decode_surface_exposure(packed_lighting: u32) -> SurfaceExposure {
 136:     SurfaceExposure::from_neighbor_occlusion(((packed_lighting >> 8) & 0x3F) as u8)
 137: }
 138: 
 139: /// Bottom-up MIP filtering over the whole atlas. Node order in the arena is
 140: /// not guaranteed (`SparseVoxelOctree::set` appends children after parents,
 141: /// `BuildOctreeUseCase` pushes the root last), so each subtree is resolved by
 142: /// memoized recursion instead of a single directional sweep. Still O(n): every
 143: /// node is computed exactly once.
 144: pub fn build_mips(atlas: &[u32]) -> Vec<MipNode> {
 145:     let node_count = atlas.len() / 4;
 146:     let mut mips = vec![MipNode::default(); node_count];
 147:     let mut done = vec![false; node_count];
 148:     for i in 0..node_count {
 149:         compute_mip(atlas, i, &mut mips, &mut done, 0);
 150:     }
 151:     mips
 152: }
 153: 
 154: /// Rebuilds one independent pooled-slot range without touching other MIPs.
 155: ///
 156: /// `done_scratch` is owned by the renderer and reused between stream updates;
 157: /// its size is proportional to the changed slot, never the complete atlas.
 158: pub(crate) fn rebuild_mips_in_node_range(
 159:     atlas: &[u32],
 160:     mips: &mut [MipNode],
 161:     node_range: Range<usize>,
 162:     done_scratch: &mut Vec<bool>,
 163: ) -> bool {
 164:     let node_count = atlas.len() / 4;
 165:     if atlas.len() % 4 != 0
 166:         || mips.len() != node_count
 167:         || node_range.is_empty()
 168:         || node_range.end > node_count
 169:     {
 170:         return false;
 171:     }
 172: 
 173:     mips[node_range.clone()].fill(MipNode::default());
 174:     done_scratch.clear();
 175:     done_scratch.resize(node_range.len(), false);
 176:     for node_index in node_range.clone() {
 177:         compute_mip(atlas, node_index, mips, done_scratch, node_range.start);
 178:     }
 179:     true
 180: }
 181: 
 182: fn compute_mip(atlas: &[u32], i: usize, mips: &mut [MipNode], done: &mut [bool], done_base: usize) {
 183:     let Some(done_index) = i.checked_sub(done_base).filter(|&index| index < done.len()) else {
 184:         return;
 185:     };
 186:     if done[done_index] {
 187:         return;
 188:     }
 189:     // Marked before descending: a malformed cycle degrades to a zero mip
 190:     // instead of infinite recursion.
 191:     done[done_index] = true;
 192: 
 193:     let t = i * 4;
 194:     if atlas[t] == 1 {
 195:         // Leaf.
 196:         let voxel_type = atlas[t + 1];
 197:         if voxel_type != VOXEL_AIR {
 198:             let c = atlas[t + 2];
 199:             let packed_lighting = atlas[t + 3];
 200:             let linear_albedo = decode_packed_albedo(c);
 201:             let strength = emission_strength(voxel_type);
 202:             mips[i] = MipNode {
 203:                 linear_albedo,
 204:                 baked_rgb: decode_baked_rgb(packed_lighting).map(f32::from),
 205:                 occupancy: 1.0,
 206:                 dominant_voxel_type: voxel_type,
 207:                 dominant_coverage: 1.0,
 208:                 emissive_coverage: if strength.is_some() { 1.0 } else { 0.0 },
 209:                 emitted_radiance_density: strength.map_or([0.0; 3], |scale| {
 210:                     linear_albedo.map(|channel| channel * scale)
 211:                 }),
 212:                 exposed_area: decode_surface_exposure(packed_lighting),
 213:             };
 214:         }
 215:     } else {
 216:         let child_base = atlas[t + 1] as usize;
 217:         let child_mask = atlas[t + 2];
 218:         let mut acc = MipNode::default();
 219:         for child in 0..8usize {
 220:             if child_mask & (1 << child) == 0 {
 221:                 continue;
 222:             }
 223:             let idx = child_base + child;
 224:             if idx >= mips.len() {
 225:                 continue;
 226:             }
 227:             compute_mip(atlas, idx, mips, done, done_base);
 228:             let m = mips[idx];
 229:             let w = m.occupancy / 8.0;
 230:             acc.linear_albedo[0] += m.linear_albedo[0] * w;
 231:             acc.linear_albedo[1] += m.linear_albedo[1] * w;
 232:             acc.linear_albedo[2] += m.linear_albedo[2] * w;
 233:             acc.baked_rgb[0] += m.baked_rgb[0] * w;
 234:             acc.baked_rgb[1] += m.baked_rgb[1] * w;
 235:             acc.baked_rgb[2] += m.baked_rgb[2] * w;
 236:             acc.emissive_coverage += m.emissive_coverage / 8.0;
 237:             acc.emitted_radiance_density[0] += m.emitted_radiance_density[0] / 8.0;
 238:             acc.emitted_radiance_density[1] += m.emitted_radiance_density[1] / 8.0;
 239:             acc.emitted_radiance_density[2] += m.emitted_radiance_density[2] / 8.0;
 240:             acc.exposed_area
 241:                 .add_saturating(m.exposed_area.scaled_to_parent());
 242:             let dominant_coverage = m.dominant_coverage / 8.0;
 243:             if dominant_coverage > acc.dominant_coverage {
 244:                 acc.dominant_coverage = dominant_coverage;
 245:                 acc.dominant_voxel_type = m.dominant_voxel_type;
 246:             }
 247:             acc.occupancy += w;
 248:         }
 249:         if acc.occupancy > 0.0 {
 250:             let inv = 1.0 / acc.occupancy;
 251:             acc.linear_albedo[0] *= inv;
 252:             acc.linear_albedo[1] *= inv;
 253:             acc.linear_albedo[2] *= inv;
 254:             acc.baked_rgb[0] *= inv;
 255:             acc.baked_rgb[1] *= inv;
 256:             acc.baked_rgb[2] *= inv;
 257:         }
 258:         mips[i] = acc;
 259:     }
 260: }
 261:

Atlas.rs confirmed:
- `build_mips` at 144-152: O(N) with memoized recursion; recursion depth bounded by SVO depth (≤20 clamped by serializer).
- `rebuild_mips_in_node_range` at 158-180: O(range) per row update; fill + scratch.
- `decode_node` 65-100: O(1) per node.

Now update_atlas_rows.rs and cpu_surfel.rs (render_surfels), input.rs.
   1: //! Validate and apply one independent pooled-atlas row update.
   2: //!
   3: //! Streaming supplies a complete row-aligned chunk slot whose child pointers
   4: //! were already rebased by [`crate::application::atlas::AtlasPool`]. This
   5: //! boundary validates that independence before mutating persistent renderer
   6: //! state, then rebuilds only the corresponding prepared MIP slice.
   7: 
   8: use std::ops::Range;
   9: 
  10: use crate::application::atlas::ROW_TEXELS;
  11: 
  12: use super::atlas::{MipNode, rebuild_mips_in_node_range};
  13: 
  14: const WORDS_PER_NODE: usize = 4;
  15: const WORDS_PER_ROW: usize = ROW_TEXELS * WORDS_PER_NODE;
  16: 
  17: #[derive(Debug, Clone)]
  18: struct ValidatedRowUpdate {
  19:     word_range: Range<usize>,
  20:     node_range: Range<usize>,
  21: }
  22: 
  23: /// Applies a complete independent slot update without reallocating the atlas.
  24: pub(super) fn update_prepared_atlas_rows(
  25:     atlas: &mut [u32],
  26:     mips: &mut [MipNode],
  27:     mip_visit_scratch: &mut Vec<bool>,
  28:     first_row: u32,
  29:     texels: &[u32],
  30: ) -> bool {
  31:     let Some(update) = validate_row_update(atlas, mips, first_row, texels) else {
  32:         return false;
  33:     };
  34: 
  35:     atlas[update.word_range].copy_from_slice(texels);
  36:     let rebuilt = rebuild_mips_in_node_range(atlas, mips, update.node_range, mip_visit_scratch);
  37:     debug_assert!(rebuilt, "validated row range must always rebuild");
  38:     rebuilt
  39: }
  40: 
  41: /// Validation is deliberately complete before the first persistent write.
  42: fn validate_row_update(
  43:     atlas: &[u32],
  44:     mips: &[MipNode],
  45:     first_row: u32,
  46:     texels: &[u32],
  47: ) -> Option<ValidatedRowUpdate> {
  48:     if atlas.is_empty()
  49:         || texels.is_empty()
  50:         || atlas.len() % WORDS_PER_ROW != 0
  51:         || texels.len() % WORDS_PER_ROW != 0
  52:         || mips.len().checked_mul(WORDS_PER_NODE)? != atlas.len()
  53:     {
  54:         return None;
  55:     }
  56: 
  57:     let first_row = usize::try_from(first_row).ok()?;
  58:     let word_start = first_row.checked_mul(WORDS_PER_ROW)?;
  59:     let word_end = word_start.checked_add(texels.len())?;
  60:     if word_end > atlas.len() {
  61:         return None;
  62:     }
  63: 
  64:     let node_range = word_start / WORDS_PER_NODE..word_end / WORDS_PER_NODE;
  65:     validate_independent_node_block(texels, node_range.clone())?;
  66:     Some(ValidatedRowUpdate {
  67:         word_range: word_start..word_end,
  68:         node_range,
  69:     })
  70: }
  71: 
  72: /// Every internal node in a streamed slot must keep all eight child slots
  73: /// inside that same update. This rejects partial-slot rows and corrupted or
  74: /// unrebased pointers before they can invalidate an unrelated slot's MIPs.
  75: fn validate_independent_node_block(texels: &[u32], global_nodes: Range<usize>) -> Option<()> {
  76:     for node in texels.chunks_exact(WORDS_PER_NODE) {
  77:         match node[0] {
  78:             1 => {}
  79:             0 => {
  80:                 if node[2] & !0xff != 0 {
  81:                     return None;
  82:                 }
  83:                 let child_base = usize::try_from(node[1]).ok()?;
  84:                 let child_end = child_base.checked_add(8)?;
  85:                 if child_base < global_nodes.start || child_end > global_nodes.end {
  86:                     return None;
  87:                 }
  88:             }
  89:             _ => return None,
  90:         }
  91:     }
  92:     Some(())
  93: }
  94: 
  95: #[cfg(test)]
  96: mod tests {
  97:     use super::*;
  98:     use crate::adapters::cpu_splatter::atlas::build_mips;
  99: 
 100:     const AIR: [u32; 4] = [1, 0, 0, 0];
 101: 
 102:     fn empty_rows(row_count: usize) -> Vec<u32> {
 103:         AIR.repeat(ROW_TEXELS * row_count)
 104:     }
 105: 
 106:     /// One root-last tree in a row-sized pool slot. All pointers are global,
 107:     /// exactly like `AtlasPool::rebased_block` produces.
 108:     fn tree_slot(first_node: usize, color: u32, light: u8) -> Vec<u32> {
 109:         let mut slot = empty_rows(1);
 110:         for child in 0..8 {
 111:             let word = child * WORDS_PER_NODE;
 112:             slot[word] = 1;
 113:             slot[word + 1] = 1;
 114:             slot[word + 2] = color;
 115:             slot[word + 3] = u32::from(light)
 116:                 | (u32::from(light) << 16)
 117:                 | (u32::from(light) << 20)
 118:                 | (u32::from(light) << 24);
 119:         }
 120:         let root_word = 8 * WORDS_PER_NODE;
 121:         slot[root_word] = 0;
 122:         slot[root_word + 1] = first_node as u32;
 123:         slot[root_word + 2] = 0xff;
 124:         slot
 125:     }
 126: 
 127:     #[test]
 128:     fn partial_slot_rebuild_matches_the_full_reference_without_touching_other_rows() {
 129:         let first_slot = tree_slot(0, 0xff0000, 3);
 130:         let old_second = tree_slot(ROW_TEXELS, 0x0000ff, 4);
 131:         let new_second = tree_slot(ROW_TEXELS, 0x00ff00, 11);
 132:         let mut atlas = [first_slot.as_slice(), old_second.as_slice()].concat();
 133:         let mut mips = build_mips(&atlas);
 134:         let untouched_atlas = atlas[..WORDS_PER_ROW].to_vec();
 135:         let untouched_mips = mips[..ROW_TEXELS].to_vec();

 144:         ));

 149:         assert_eq!(&atlas[..WORDS_PER_ROW], untouched_atlas);

   1: //! Input adapter: accumulates raw browser events (key codes, mouse deltas,
   2: //! pointer-lock state) and translates them into one [`InputFrame`] per tick.
   3: //!
   4: //! The driver layer feeds this from DOM event listeners; the frame loop
   5: //! drains it with [`InputCollector::take_frame`]. Keeping the mapping here —
   6: //! not in the driver — means "which key means forward" is a testable rule,
   7: //! not something buried in an event closure.
   8: 
   9: use crate::application::engine::InputFrame;
  10: use crate::application::player::MoveIntent;
  11: 
  12: #[derive(Debug, Default)]
  13: pub struct InputCollector {
  14:     intent: MoveIntent,
  15:     pending_dx: f32,
  16:     pending_dy: f32,
  17:     locked: bool,
  18:     flashlight: bool,
  19:     /// One queued flare drop, consumed by the next frame. Edge-triggered:
  20:     /// holding G never queues a second drop until the key is released.
  21:     flare_queued: bool,
  22:     flare_key_held: bool,
  23:     /// Deliberate consumption, edge-triggered exactly like the flare:
  24:     /// R drinks one carried almond water, T eats one carried ration.
  25:     drink_queued: bool,
  26:     drink_key_held: bool,
  27:     eat_queued: bool,
  28:     eat_key_held: bool,
  29: }
  30: 
  31: impl InputCollector {
  32:     pub fn new() -> Self {
  33:         Self::default()
  34:     }
  35: 
  36:     /// Handles a keydown/keyup pair by `KeyboardEvent.code` value.
  37:     /// Unknown codes are ignored. Both WASD and arrow keys are mapped.
  38:     pub fn key_event(&mut self, code: &str, pressed: bool) {
  39:         #[cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")]
  40:         let doom = crate::DOOM_CONTROLS.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed);
  41: 
  42:         #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
  43:         let doom = false;
  44: 
  45:         if pressed && code == "KeyF" {
  46:             self.flashlight = !self.flashlight;
  47:         }
  48: 
  49:         // Flare drop: fires once per press, only while playing (the engine
  50:         // additionally ignores drops when the session is not locked, so a
  51:         // G typed into the settings menu can never reach gameplay).
  52:         if code == "KeyG" {
  53:             if pressed && !self.flare_key_held && self.locked {
  54:                 self.flare_queued = true;
  55:             }
  56:             self.flare_key_held = pressed;
  57:         }
  58: 
  59:         // Deliberate consumption: one supply per key press, only in play.
  60:         if code == "KeyR" {
  61:             if pressed && !self.drink_key_held && self.locked {
  62:                 self.drink_queued = true;
  63:             }
  64:             self.drink_key_held = pressed;
  65:         }
  66:         if code == "KeyT" {
  67:             if pressed && !self.eat_key_held && self.locked {
  68:                 self.eat_queued = true;
  69:             }
  70:             self.eat_key_held = pressed;
  71:         }
  72: 
  73:         if doom {
  74:             match code {
  75:                 "KeyW" | "ArrowUp" => self.intent.forward = pressed,
  76:                 "KeyS" | "ArrowDown" => self.intent.backward = pressed,
  77:                 "KeyQ" => self.intent.left = pressed,
  78:                 "KeyE" => self.intent.right = pressed,
  79:                 "KeyA" | "ArrowLeft" => self.intent.turn_left = pressed,
  80:                 "KeyD" | "ArrowRight" => self.intent.turn_right = pressed,
  81:                 _ => {}
  82:             }
  83:         } else {
  84:             match code {
  85:                 "KeyW" | "ArrowUp" => self.intent.forward = pressed,
  86:                 "KeyS" | "ArrowDown" => self.intent.backward = pressed,
  87:                 "KeyA" | "ArrowLeft" => self.intent.left = pressed,
  88:                 "KeyD" | "ArrowRight" => self.intent.right = pressed,
  89:                 "KeyQ" => self.intent.turn_left = pressed,
  90:                 "KeyE" => self.intent.turn_right = pressed,
  91:                 _ => {}
  92:             }
  93:         }
  94:     }
  95: 
  96:     /// Accumulates a mouse movement (only meaningful while pointer-locked).
  97:     pub fn mouse_delta(&mut self, dx: f32, dy: f32) {
  98:         if self.locked {
  99:             #[cfg(target_arch = "wasm32")]
 100:             let (sens, invert) = {
 101:                 use std::sync::atomic::Ordering;
 102:                 (
 103:                     f32::from_bits(crate::MOUSE_SENSITIVITY_BITS.load(Ordering::Relaxed)),
 104:                     crate::INVERT_Y.load(Ordering::Relaxed),
 105:                 )
 106:             };
 107:             #[cfg(not(target_arch = "wasm32"))]
 108:             let (sens, invert) = (1.0f32, false);
 109: 
 110:             self.pending_dx += dx * sens;
 111:             self.pending_dy += dy * sens * if invert { -1.0 } else { 1.0 };
 112:         }
 113:     }
 114: 
 115:     /// Sets movement intent from an analog source (the touch joystick).
 116:     /// `x` is strafe (-1..1, right positive), `y` is walk (-1..1, forward
 117:     /// positive). Values inside the dead zone clear the intent, so lifting
 118:     /// the thumb stops the player.
 119:     pub fn set_move_axes(&mut self, x: f32, y: f32) {
 120:         const DEAD_ZONE: f32 = 0.25;
 121:         self.intent.forward = y > DEAD_ZONE;
 122:         self.intent.backward = y < -DEAD_ZONE;
 123:         self.intent.right = x > DEAD_ZONE;
 124:         self.intent.left = x < -DEAD_ZONE;
 125:     }
 126: 
 127:     /// Toggles the flashlight (touch button; keyboard uses KeyF).
 128:     pub fn toggle_flashlight(&mut self) {
 129:         self.flashlight = !self.flashlight;
 130:     }
 131: 
 132:     /// Queues one flare drop (touch button; keyboard uses KeyG). One queued
 133:     /// drop per frame regardless of how many events bubble in.
 134:     pub fn queue_flare(&mut self) {
 135:         if self.locked {
 136:             self.flare_queued = true;
 137:         }
 138:     }
 139: 
 140:     /// Queues one deliberate drink (touch button; keyboard uses KeyR).
 141:     pub fn queue_drink(&mut self) {
 142:         if self.locked {
 143:             self.drink_queued = true;
 144:         }
 145:     }
 146: 
 147:     /// Queues one deliberate meal (touch button; keyboard uses KeyT).
 148:     pub fn queue_eat(&mut self) {
 149:         if self.locked {
 150:             self.eat_queued = true;
 151:         }
 152:     }
 153: 
 154:     /// Pointer lock engaged/released. Releasing clears held keys so the
 155:     /// player doesn't keep walking while the overlay is up.
 156:     pub fn set_locked(&mut self, locked: bool) {
 157:         self.locked = locked;
 158:         if !locked {
 159:             self.intent = MoveIntent::default();
 160:             self.pending_dx = 0.0;
 161:             self.pending_dy = 0.0;
 162:             self.flare_queued = false;
 163:             self.drink_queued = false;
 164:             self.eat_queued = false;
 165:         }
 166:     }
 167: 
 168:     pub fn is_locked(&self) -> bool {
 169:         self.locked
 170:     }
 171: 
 172:     /// Drains accumulated input into a frame snapshot. Mouse deltas reset;
 173:     /// key intent persists until the matching keyup.
 174:     pub fn take_frame(&mut self) -> InputFrame {
 175:         let frame = InputFrame {
 176:             intent: self.intent,
 177:             look_dx: self.pending_dx,
 178:             look_dy: self.pending_dy,
 179:             locked: self.locked,
 180:             flashlight: self.flashlight,
 181:             drop_flare: self.flare_queued,
 182:             drink: self.drink_queued,
 183:             eat: self.eat_queued,
 184:         };
 185:         self.pending_dx = 0.0;
 186:         self.pending_dy = 0.0;
 187:         self.flare_queued = false;
 188:         self.drink_queued = false;
 189:         self.eat_queued = false;
 190:         frame
 191:     }
 192: }
 193: 
 194: #[cfg(test)]
 195: mod tests {
 196:     use super::*;

 198:     #[test]
 199:     fn wasd_maps_to_intent_and_persists_across_frames() {
 200:         let mut input = InputCollector::new();
 201:         input.set_locked(true);
 202:         input.key_event("KeyW", true);
 203:         input.key_event("KeyD", true);
 204:         let f1 = input.take_frame();
 205:         assert!(f1.intent.forward && f1.intent.right);
 206:         let f2 = input.take_frame();
 207:         assert!(f2.intent.forward, "held keys persist");
 208:         input.key_event("KeyW", false);

 182:         [v[0] / len, v[1] / len, v[2] / len]

 186: /// Renders one frame. `origin` is the world offset of the cloud's chunk,
 187: /// because surfel positions are chunk-local fixed point.
 188: pub fn render_surfels(
 189:     clouds: &[(&[PackedSurfel], [f32; 3])],
 190:     camera: &SurfelCamera,
 191:     settings: &SurfelRenderSettings,
 192: ) -> SurfelImage {
 193:     let basis = Basis::new(camera, settings);
 194:     let pixels = settings.width * settings.height;
 195: 
 196:     // Per pixel: nearest depth seen, plus a weighted colour accumulator for
 197:     // every fragment inside that pixel's blending window.
 198:     let mut depth = vec![f32::INFINITY; pixels];
 199:     let mut accum = vec![[0.0f32; 3]; pixels];
 200:     let mut weight = vec![0.0f32; pixels];
 201:     let mut stats = SurfelStats::default();
 202: 
 203:     for (surfels, origin) in clouds {
 204:         for surfel in *surfels {
 205:             stats.submitted += 1;
 206:             let local = surfel.world_position();
 207:             let world = [
 208:                 local[0] + origin[0],
 209:                 local[1] + origin[1],
 210:                 local[2] + origin[2],
 211:             ];
 212:             let to_surfel = [
 213:                 world[0] - camera.position[0],
 214:                 world[1] - camera.position[1],
 215:                 world[2] - camera.position[2],
 216:             ];
 217:             let view_depth = dot(to_surfel, basis.forward);
 218:             if view_depth <= 0.05 {
 219:                 stats.behind_camera += 1;
 220:                 continue;
 221:             }
 222:             let normal = surfel.normal();
 223:             // A disc is one-sided: its back is the inside of a wall.
 224:             if dot(normal, to_surfel) >= 0.0 {
 225:                 stats.back_facing += 1;
 226:                 continue;
 227:             }
 228: 
 229:             let radius = surfel.world_radius();
 230:             let (t1, t2) = tangents(normal);
 231:             let Some((centre, ndc)) = project(to_surfel, &basis) else {
 232:                 stats.behind_camera += 1;
 233:                 continue;
 234:             };
 235: 
 236:             // Frustum cull before the footprint, not after.
 237:             //
 238:             // The Jacobian below linearizes the perspective divide about
 239:             // this point, and that linearization is only meaningful while
 240:             // the point is roughly in front of the eye. A surfel *beside*
 241:             // the camera — a metre to the left, a hand's breadth ahead —
 242:             // has a small depth and a large lateral offset, so its `ndc`
 243:             // runs to the hundreds and its footprint to the size of a
 244:             // continent. A handful of those wash the entire frame out to
 245:             // flat grey, which is not a subtle failure but is a very
 246:             // confusing one, because the geometry and the shading are both
 247:             // fine.
 248:             //
 249:             // The margin is the disc's own angular radius, so a surfel
 250:             // just off the edge of the screen still contributes the part of
 251:             // itself that reaches onto it.
 252:             let margin = radius / view_depth;
 253:             if ndc[0].abs() > basis.half_w / basis.focal_px + margin
 254:                 || ndc[1].abs() > basis.half_h / basis.focal_px + margin
 255:             {
 256:                 stats.off_screen += 1;
 257:                 continue;
 258:             }
 259: 
 260:             let axis_a = screen_axis(t1, radius, ndc, view_depth, &basis);
 261:             let axis_b = screen_axis(t2, radius, ndc, view_depth, &basis);
 262: 
 263:             if draw_ellipse(
 264:                 centre,
 265:                 axis_a,
 266:                 axis_b,
 267:                 view_depth,
 268:                 surfel,
 269:                 settings,
 270:                 &mut depth,
 271:                 &mut accum,
 272:                 &mut weight,
 273:                 &mut stats,
 274:             ) {
 275:                 stats.drawn += 1;
 276:             } else {
 277:                 stats.off_screen += 1;
 278:             }
 279:         }
 280:     }
 281: 
 282:     let mut rgb = vec![0u8; pixels * 3];
 283:     let mut covered = 0usize;
 284:     for i in 0..pixels {
 285:         let color = if weight[i] > 0.0 {
 286:             covered += 1;
 287:             let inv = 1.0 / weight[i];
 288:             let lit = [accum[i][0] * inv, accum[i][1] * inv, accum[i][2] * inv];
 289:             fog_blend(lit, depth[i], &settings.fog)
 290:         } else {
 291:             settings.fog.color
 292:         };
 293:         for c in 0..3 {
 294:             rgb[i * 3 + c] = (color[c].clamp(0.0, 1.0).powf(1.0 / 2.2) * 255.0).round() as u8;
 295:         }
 296:     }
 297: 
 298:     SurfelImage {
 299:         width: settings.width,
 300:         height: settings.height,

Confirmed cpu_surfel.rs render_surfels:
- Lines 198-200: three full-frame vec allocations per call: depth (f32), accum ([f32;3]), weight (f32) → 4+12+4 = 20 bytes/pixel; 1080p ≈ 41.5 MB allocated and zeroed each frame (actually f32::INFINITY fill).
- Line 282: rgb vec another 6 MB per frame.
- draw_ellipse per surfel: O(bbox area).

Let me check draw_ellipse quickly for the bbox scan (lines 300-460) and confirm no early-out.
 301:         rgb,
 302:         stats,
 303:         coverage: covered as f32 / pixels.max(1) as f32,
 304:     }
 305: }
 306: 
 307: /// Perspective-projects a camera-relative point to pixels, returning the
 308: /// normalized image coordinates alongside — the footprint's Jacobian needs
 309: /// them, and recomputing a divide per tangent would be wasteful.
 310: ///
 311: /// `None` when the point is at or behind the eye plane and has no image.
 312: fn project(to_point: [f32; 3], basis: &Basis) -> Option<([f32; 2], [f32; 2])> {
 313:     let depth = dot(to_point, basis.forward);
 314:     (depth > 0.05).then(|| {
 315:         let ndc = [
 316:             dot(to_point, basis.right) / depth,
 317:             dot(to_point, basis.up) / depth,
 318:         ];
 319:         (
 320:             [
 321:                 basis.half_w + basis.focal_px * ndc[0],
 322:                 basis.half_h - basis.focal_px * ndc[1],
 323:             ],
 324:             ndc,
 325:         )
 326:     })
 327: }
 328: 
 329: /// Where one of the disc's tangents lands on screen: the projection's
 330: /// Jacobian at the surfel's centre, scaled by the radius.
 331: ///
 332: /// Both terms matter, and each of them alone is a distinct, believable
 333: /// failure:
 334: ///
 335: /// * Keep only `t·right / depth` — the naive "project the offset at the
 336: ///   centre's depth" — and any tangent pointing along the view direction
 337: ///   contributes nothing. A floor disc then flattens to a horizontal line,
 338: ///   and the floor renders as a radial spray of dashes from the vanishing
 339: ///   point.
 340: /// * Drop the Jacobian entirely and project the tangent's *endpoint*
 341: ///   exactly, and the secant explodes whenever the endpoint is much nearer
 342: ///   the eye than the centre. Every grazing disc smears across the frame.
 343: ///
 344: /// The second term, `-ndc * (t·forward)`, is the perspective stretch: how
 345: /// much the image slides because the offset changed the depth. With it, a
 346: /// disc lying along the view direction elongates toward the horizon, which
 347: /// is what a real floor tile does.
 348: fn screen_axis(
 349:     tangent: [f32; 3],
 350:     radius: f32,
 351:     ndc: [f32; 2],
 352:     depth: f32,
 353:     basis: &Basis,
 354: ) -> [f32; 2] {
 355:     let along_view = dot(tangent, basis.forward);
 356:     let scale = basis.focal_px * radius / depth;
 357:     [
 358:         scale * (dot(tangent, basis.right) - ndc[0] * along_view),
 359:         -scale * (dot(tangent, basis.up) - ndc[1] * along_view),
 360:     ]
 361: }
 362: 
 363: #[allow(clippy::too_many_arguments)]
 364: fn draw_ellipse(
 365:     centre: [f32; 2],
 366:     axis_a: [f32; 2],
 367:     axis_b: [f32; 2],
 368:     view_depth: f32,
 369:     surfel: &PackedSurfel,
 370:     settings: &SurfelRenderSettings,
 371:     depth: &mut [f32],
 372:     accum: &mut [[f32; 3]],
 373:     weight: &mut [f32],
 374:     stats: &mut SurfelStats,
 375: ) -> bool {
 376:     // Screen-space bound of the ellipse: the extent of |a| and |b| on each
 377:     // axis, floored so a surfel that has shrunk below a pixel still marks
 378:     // one. A surface that vanishes at range is worse than one that aliases.
 379:     let ext_x = (axis_a[0].abs() + axis_b[0].abs()).max(settings.min_radius_px);
 380:     let ext_y = (axis_a[1].abs() + axis_b[1].abs()).max(settings.min_radius_px);
 381:     let x0 = (centre[0] - ext_x).floor().max(0.0) as usize;
 382:     let x1 = ((centre[0] + ext_x).ceil() as isize).clamp(0, settings.width as isize) as usize;
 383:     let y0 = (centre[1] - ext_y).floor().max(0.0) as usize;
 384:     let y1 = ((centre[1] + ext_y).ceil() as isize).clamp(0, settings.height as isize) as usize;
 385:     if x0 >= x1 || y0 >= y1 {
 386:         return false;
 387:     }
 388: 
 389:     // Invert [a b] so a pixel offset can be expressed in disc coordinates.
 390:     // A degenerate matrix means the disc is exactly edge-on and covers no
 391:     // area; the min-radius path below still marks its centre pixel.
 392:     let det = axis_a[0] * axis_b[1] - axis_a[1] * axis_b[0];
 393:     let invertible = det.abs() > 1e-6;
 394:     let color = shade(surfel, settings);
 395: 
 396:     let mut touched = false;
 397:     for py in y0..y1 {
 398:         for px in x0..x1 {
 399:             let d = [px as f32 + 0.5 - centre[0], py as f32 + 0.5 - centre[1]];
 400:             let inside = if invertible {
 401:                 let u = (d[0] * axis_b[1] - d[1] * axis_b[0]) / det;
 402:                 let v = (axis_a[0] * d[1] - axis_a[1] * d[0]) / det;
 403:                 u * u + v * v <= 1.0
 404:             } else {
 405:                 d[0].abs() <= settings.min_radius_px && d[1].abs() <= settings.min_radius_px
 406:             };
 407:             if !inside {
 408:                 continue;
 409:             }
 410:             let i = py * settings.width + px;
 411:             stats.fragments += 1;
 412:             touched = true;
 413: 
 414:             if view_depth < depth[i] - settings.blend_window {
 415:                 // A genuinely nearer surface: the window moves to it and
 416:                 // everything accumulated behind is discarded.
 417:                 depth[i] = view_depth;
 418:                 accum[i] = [color[0], color[1], color[2]];
 419:                 weight[i] = 1.0;
 420:             } else if view_depth <= depth[i] + settings.blend_window {
 421:                 // Coplanar with what is already here: average, and keep the
 422:                 // nearest depth so the window tracks the front surface.
 423:                 depth[i] = depth[i].min(view_depth);
 424:                 for c in 0..3 {
 425:                     accum[i][c] += color[c];
 426:                 }
 427:                 weight[i] += 1.0;
 428:             }
 429:         }
 430:     }
 431:     touched
 432: }
 433: 
 434: /// Albedo, baked light, occlusion and emission. Deliberately the simplest
 435: /// shading that can be compared against the other renderers: any difference
 436: /// in the resulting image is then a difference in the *representation*, not
 437: /// in the lighting model.
 438: fn shade(surfel: &PackedSurfel, settings: &SurfelRenderSettings) -> [f32; 3] {
 439:     let packed = material_visual(surfel.material).color;
 440:     let albedo = [
 441:         ((packed >> 16) & 0xFF) as f32 / 255.0,
 442:         ((packed >> 8) & 0xFF) as f32 / 255.0,
 443:         (packed & 0xFF) as f32 / 255.0,
 444:     ];
 445:     if surfel.flags & SURFEL_FLAG_EMISSIVE != 0 {
 446:         return albedo;
 447:     }
 448:     let light = surfel.baked_light as f32 / 15.0;
 449:     let occlusion = if surfel.ao != 0 { 0.55 } else { 1.0 };
 450:     let intensity = settings.ambient + light * occlusion;
 451:     [
 452:         albedo[0] * intensity,
 453:         albedo[1] * intensity,
 454:         albedo[2] * intensity,
 455:     ]
 456: }
 457: 
 458: fn fog_blend(color: [f32; 3], depth: f32, fog: &SurfelFog) -> [f32; 3] {
 459:     let transmittance = (-fog.density * (depth - fog.start).max(0.0)).exp();
 460:     [
